Looked like the answer. But a probe on wheel routing (with a *control* that had to move the page first, after two earlier attempts produced meaningless numbers — one swapped `__class__` on a live shiboken object, which does not rebind Qt's virtual dispatch; the other scrolled a bar already at its maximum) showed that **a nested scroll area with nothing left to scroll does not pass the wheel on to its parent.** Overriding `wheelEvent` to `ignore()` did not help either, because `QAbstractScrollArea` handles the wheel in `viewportEvent` on the *viewport*. So the tidy fix would have traded a visible scrollbar for a **dead wheel zone over the biggest widget in the pane** — worse than the complaint, and invisible to every test we had.

  Also found on the way: sizing the table needed `horizontalHeader().isHidden()`, not `isVisible()` — the latter is False before the widget is shown, which silently dropped 18 px and clipped the last row. **The same distinction bit twice in one session**: the Details-tab warning marker first failed to appear because it asked `isVisible()` of a label in a background tab. `isVisible()` answers "is this on screen right now", `isHidden()` answers "was this switched off" — and the second is almost always the question meant.

  **The fix is structural: never nest a scroll surface.** The pane is now three bands — a fixed header holding the progress bars, the status line and the trust verdict (never scrolled, never behind a tab); a `QTabWidget` with *Tracks* / *Details* / *Live log*, so exactly one scroll surface exists at a time; and the pinned button row. Measured on the real widget: **at most one live scrollbar, zero nested scroll areas, zero overlapping siblings**, on every tab from 1900×980 down to 940×300. The v0.5.15 overlap fix is preserved rather than traded away.

  Two deliberate mitigations, because tabs can hide things: the pane **follows the rip** (console while ripping, results when the log lands — unless there are no tracks, in which case the log stays, since it is the only thing that can explain why), and the **Details tab label carries a ⚠** whenever a caveat is sitting behind it, so a warning can never wait silently for a click. The pane's guaranteed minimum height grew 200 → 246 px, and that is the honest price of keeping the verdict permanently on screen; the test bound moved to 260 with the reason written down rather than being quietly relaxed.

  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) ***Never nest one scroll surface inside another*** — graduated to `architecture.md` §3.9 with the wheel measurement and the band table. (2) ***A fix can be correct and still be the next bug's cause.*** v0.5.15 was right about the overlap and created the nesting; shipping it was still correct, but "the reported symptom is gone" is not "the pane is good". (3) ***Measure with a control.*** Two of three wheel probes produced confident nonsense; the one with a control experiment produced the finding that changed the design. A probe that cannot fail is not evidence. (4) ***`isHidden()` vs `isVisible()`*** — ask "was it switched off", not "is it on screen". (5) ***A tab is a good way to get one scroll surface and a bad way to store warnings*** — if you hide something, mark where it went.

- **Sixth hardware run (v0.5.14): the headline read-stability fix is finally *proven*, and EAC parity came in at 13/14 (2026-07-29, continued session).** Verified with the project's own `parity.compare_logs` against the committed EAC baseline (decoded through `decode_log_bytes` — the genuine EAC log is UTF-16, and reading it as UTF-8 once produced a false 0/14 in this project). The single mismatch is **track 3**: `1AC787A1` against the baseline's `59D352DD`. That is the disc, not the app — track 3's history is now `52DFDF7D` / `3D8FCF0C` / `59D352DD` / `1AC787A1` across six runs, so run 5's clean read was the outlier, not the new normal. The log checksum was recomputed by hand (`head -n -1 … | sha256sum`) and matches its footer exactly.

  **Test A1 — the fix that had been unprovable for four releases — passed.** Track 3 failed to converge, and the log did precisely the right thing: `Copy CRC 1AC787A1 (re-reads did NOT agree — this read is not confirmed reproducible)` with **no `Test CRC` line**, plus the `Read stability` roll-up naming track 3. Track 5, which *did* converge over three re-reads, correctly kept its `Test CRC`/`Copy CRC` pair — so the fix discriminates between the two cases rather than just suppressing a line. Retired from the hardware sheet. Worth noting *why* it took six runs: the negative case needs a track that misbehaves, and the disc had briefly stopped misbehaving. **Some fixes can only be proven when the hardware chooses to cooperate**, and the honest move in the meantime was leaving it on the sheet marked NOT EXERCISED rather than quietly calling it done.

  CTDB returned **no match** this run, against run 5's match — and that is consistent rather than flaky: CTDB is a single CRC over the *whole* disc, so one divergent track changes it. The app said so, in the reconciliation line, which is the wording added in v0.5.12 for exactly this situation.

- **The overlapping text was a *vertical* overflow, and v0.5.14 fixed the wrong axis (v0.5.15, 2026-07-29, continued session).** The maintainer installed v0.5.14, re-ripped, and reported plainly: *"didn't seem to fix the minimize text issue."* They were right, and the entry immediately below this one is a worked example of confidently diagnosing the wrong thing.

  **What v0.5.14 actually fixed** was real but was not this: an un-wrapped `QLabel` makes its whole single line the pane's minimum *width*, so the window refused to *narrow* (906 px vs 366 px, measured). That cannot by itself paint text over text — and wrapping a label *increases* vertical demand by a line, so the release marginally worsened the symptom it was credited with fixing. The tell was in the original report all along: *"when smaller"*, not *"when narrower"*.

  **The real mechanism, reproduced before touching anything.** A probe built the real `RipProgress`, fed it the real hardware rip log through the real `parse_cyanrip_log`, laid it out at the screenshot's size, and compared every sibling geometry pairwise. Two overlaps, naming exactly the widgets in the screenshot: the verdict banner across the live-log box, and the CTDB line across the AccurateRip table's first row. **A `QVBoxLayout` given less height than its children's minimums does not clip and does not scroll — it overflows, and overflowing means sibling rectangles collide.** Compounding it, the pane *under-reported* its own needs: a word-wrapped label's `minimumSizeHint` height is one line while its `heightForWidth` is two or three, so the pane claimed a 326 px minimum and then allocated ~405 px. The deficit was the overlap, which is why it appeared at the pane's own stated minimum.

  **Two candidate fixes were measured, not argued.** A scroll area: **0 overlapping widgets at every height from 620 px down to 200 px**. Teaching every wrapped label to report its true height-for-width: also 0 overlaps, but it drove the pane's minimum height to **1418 px** — a window taller than most screens. So the scroll area shipped and the alternative is written down as rejected, with its number, so nobody re-derives it. `DiscInfoPanel` was probed the same way and is *clean* at every size down to 300×80 — its minimum is honest — so the fix is scoped to the one pane that needed it rather than applied reflexively.

  **The detector nearly shipped blind, which is the most transferable part.** After the fix, the first overlap probe reported "0 overlaps" — because it walked `pane.layout()`, which now holds a *single* item (the scroll area). It was comparing one widget against nothing. The same trap would have made the committed test permanently green. The kept test returns *how many widgets it examined* and asserts that count is ≥ 8, so a structural refactor fails it loudly instead of quietly emptying it.

  Also fixed alongside: **the completion notification now logs its outcome.** Whether it fired was unanswerable from `log.txt` — the success path logged nothing, the failure path logged at `debug` — so this run's A5 result was inconclusive purely because the maintainer was gaming and then at dinner while the eight-second toast came and went. Every branch now records posted / skipped-and-why at INFO.

  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) ***Reproduce the symptom before believing the diagnosis.*** A mechanism that plausibly explains a screenshot is not the mechanism; the probe took ten minutes and would have prevented an entire wrong release. (2) ***A layout that runs out of room overlaps — it does not clip.*** Any pane of variable-length text belongs in a `QScrollArea`; graduated to `architecture.md` §3.9 with the rejected alternative and its 1418 px. (3) ***A detector that can be satisfied by finding nothing needs a floor.*** Assert on the size of the search space, not only on the result — graduated to `testing.md` §5.v. (4) ***A feature whose only evidence is transient must record its outcome*** or neither a test nor a bug report can reach it.

- **The overlapping-text bug was a minimum-width bug (v0.5.14, 2026-07-28, continued session).** *(Superseded by the entry above — the width finding here is real, but it was not the cause of the overlap.)* The run-5 screenshot had shown the CTDB and loudness lines apparently drawn *on top of* the AccurateRip table, and I could not tell from a static capture whether it was real — so it went onto the hardware sheet as an open question (A5b). The maintainer answered it from the rig: *"it looked good when the window was maximized, but when smaller was all over the place."* That single sentence converts the symptom from a paint bug into a **layout-constraint** bug, and it was diagnosable immediately.

  **Cause: two labels were never told to word-wrap.** An un-wrapped `QLabel` reports its *whole single line* as its `minimumSizeHint`, and a minimum propagates up through every containing layout to the window itself. Measured on the real widgets rather than reasoned about: `RipProgress`'s minimum width went **366 px → 906 px** the moment it was handed a genuine end-of-rip status line, and `DiscInfoPanel`'s went **208 px → 575 px** once its value labels held real post-rip values (the MusicBrainz match label alone demanded 426 px). Below those widths the layout could not satisfy its children, so it drew them outside their allotted rows — which is what the screenshot showed. Maximised there was room, which is precisely why it survived five hardware runs and an eight-reviewer audit.

  `RipProgress._status_label` was the *only* label in that pane without `setWordWrap(True)`; in `DiscInfoPanel` every value label was un-wrapped, all from the one shared `_value_label()` helper, so both fixes are one line at a single seam. Field-*name* captions stay un-wrapped deliberately — they are short, fixed, and their width *is* the column. Wrapping costs nothing for the identifiers either: a disc ID is a single token with no spaces, so it still reserves its own width and is never split.

  The regression tests are stated as a **font-metric-independent invariant** — the same vocabulary repeated ten times must not change the pane's minimum width — because the first attempt asserted exact equality against the short-text minimum and failed at 219 vs 188: a wrapped label's minimum is its longest *word*, which is irreducible and font-dependent. Each pane also gets a fitness test asserting every label holding a dynamic message wraps, so a newly added label cannot reintroduce this quietly. Both were verified non-vacuous by reverting the fix (they fail at 3389 px and 3359 px).

  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) ***An un-wrapped `QLabel` is a minimum-width assertion about the whole window.*** Any label that displays *dynamic* text must wrap, or the longest string it is ever handed becomes a floor the user cannot resize below — graduated to `docs/architecture.md`. (2) ***"Looks fine maximised" is not a layout test.*** The failure mode only exists below the minimum, so a check that never shrinks the window cannot see it; the tests assert on `minimumSizeHint()`, which needs no window at all. (3) ***A vague symptom plus one sentence of user context can be worth more than a screenshot.*** I had the image for a full release cycle and could not act on it; "good maximised, bad smaller" named the mechanism outright.

- **Fifth hardware run (v0.5.12): full 14/14 EAC parity, the first CTDB match — and one regression the log caught (2026-07-28, continued session).** The maintainer ran the released v0.5.12 on the reference rig and sent the full artifact set. **The best result this disc has produced.** `parity.compare_logs`, reading only the genuine EAC V1.8 baseline and our EAC-compatible log, reports **14/14 tracks byte-identical** — up from 12/14, then 13/14. The reason is track 3: the disc's chronic problem child, which had produced a different CRC on every earlier run (`52DFDF7D`, `3D8FCF0C`), **read cleanly on the first pass** as `59D352DD` with an exact AccurateRip match at confidence 200 — and that value agrees with EAC. Track 5 remains a genuine offset-variant pressing (v1/v2 both "not found", `Accurip 450` matching at confidence 200), was re-read three times, converged, and was swapped in; its `E0036697` has held across all five runs and also matches EAC. So the disc's honest ceiling is 13/14 exact + 1 offset-variant, and we are at it.

  **CTDB matched for the first time**: `verdict: match`, `confidence 1`, `our_crc == matched_crc == 5DA89FCD` against 102 database entries, `crc_validated: true`. Our reimplemented CTDB CRC is now confirmed against a real database hit on real hardware — independent whole-disc corroboration on top of AccurateRip. I also recomputed the log's SHA-256 by hand (`head -n -1 … | sha256sum`) and it matched the footer exactly.

  **The regression the log caught — mine, from v0.5.12.** Every completed rip raised `AttributeError: 'MainWindow' object has no attribute '_tray_icon'` inside `_ensure_tray_icon`, swallowed because notifications are best-effort, so **the desktop notification silently never fired for anyone**. Cause: to satisfy mypy's `warn_return_any` I replaced `getattr(self, "_tray_icon", None)` with a plain attribute read and declared `_tray_icon` on `MainWindowShared` — but that seam's declarations live under `if TYPE_CHECKING:`, so they inform the type checker and *create nothing*. The read preceded the only assignment. Fixed by initialising it in `__init__`, with two regression tests: the pre-existing notification test had passed throughout because it **stubbed** `_ensure_tray_icon` instead of calling it — the same "tested one layer below the layer that broke" shape the whole audit was about, landing on the audit's own fix.

  Also confirmed from the artifacts: A4 **passed** (Overall bar at 100%, retired from the run sheet); the stall watchdog fired correctly (`no forward progress for 3m 0s at 35.5% (track 5)`); the swap addendum agrees with the log body; `read_speed.secure_rerip` records `engaged: true, interrupted: false`; the `issues` list carries exactly `not_bit_perfect` + `heavy_reread` for track 5 and no false `ctdb_no_match`. The blank disc panel in the screenshot was **not** a bug — the log shows `disc removed from /dev/sr0 — clearing the disc view`. **A1 was not exercised**: nothing failed to converge, so the forged-Test-CRC fix's negative case is still unproven and stays on the sheet with a suggestion for provoking it.

  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) ***A type-only declaration is not an initialisation.*** A `TYPE_CHECKING` seam that satisfies the checker can leave a runtime attribute that never exists — and silencing a type warning by *removing* a `getattr` default is precisely how to convert a safe read into a crash. When a fix is "make mypy happy", ask what the old defensive form was protecting against. (2) ***Best-effort error handling hides regressions indefinitely.*** This one shipped, ran on real hardware, and produced a perfect rip while a headline feature was dead; only the debug log knew. Anything wrapped in a swallow deserves a test that drives the *real* path and asserts nothing was logged as an exception. (3) ***The artifacts are worth reading even when everything looks green.*** The screen said success on every surface; the bug was three lines deep in `log.txt`.

- **The suite's oldest bug: cyclic GC on a worker thread (2026-07-28, continued session).** The v0.5.12 release shipped green, but I'd had to flag one thing as unfixed: a forced `gc.collect()` segfaulted the suite, and **unmodified `main` did it 5 runs out of 5**. The maintainer's instruction was exact — *"make sure to allow fix, but verify at the root"* — so the first thing written was the **detector**, not the fix: `tests/test_qt_teardown_fitness.py` forces a full all-generations collection every run and asserts no worker thread survived. Baseline with it in place: **3 crashes in 3 runs.** Everything after that is measured against a test that can actually see the bug.

  **Three things compounded.** (1) **`deleteLater()` never executes here.** Qt delivers `DeferredDelete` only when the posting event loop exits or when it is asked for *by type*; this suite runs no event loop, and neither `processEvents()` nor a bare `sendPostedEvents()` qualifies — so every window a fixture "deleted" stayed alive, pinned, for the whole process. (2) **Post-rip work runs on daemon `threading.Thread`s** — hashing, verify, transcode, library move — and the existing backstop only tracked `QThread`. (3) **A cyclic collection can begin on any thread.** Whichever thread was inside the collector when the GUI thread destroyed a widget was the one that died, which is exactly why the traceback always named an innocent bystander: a dialog test that called `gc.collect()`, or a `pathlib.rglob` in a checksums worker. On CPython ≤ 3.11 a collection can even start part-way through a C-extension allocation, which is why only the 3.11 CI leg ever failed.

  **The fix generalises a guard the codebase had already reached for twice locally** — `process_until` pauses the collector around its pump, and the e2e rip fixture pauses it for a whole test, both for this reason. It is now suite-wide: the cyclic collector is off for the duration of each test, with a cheap gen-0 collect at teardown and the one full sweep in the detector. Reference counting is untouched. **Fixture order is part of the fix**: autouse fixtures tear down in reverse setup order, so the GC fixture is declared *first* to tear down *last* — after the join fixtures have stopped every worker, the one moment no other thread can be inside the collector. Alongside it, `stop_window_threads` went from four QThread slots to all seven **plus** all nine daemon slots, and a `threading.Thread` backstop now mirrors the `QThread` one.

  **The specific leak that made it visible** was a test bug worth naming: `test_checksums_skipped_when_post_rip_work_never_settles` shortened `_CHECKSUM_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_S` to 0.05s, then undid its monkeypatches in a `finally` **while the digest worker was still running**. The worker reads that global and resolves `checksums.compute_digests` at call time, on its own thread — so it got handed the real 120-second bound and the real function, and kept walking a `tmp_path` pytest had already deleted, for two minutes, across dozens of later tests, until some other test's event pump delivered a queued signal to the by-then-destroyed window.

  **Result: 0 crashes in 10 randomized runs, and 0 in 8 under `--cov`** (coverage matters — it slows workers and was the condition under which an earlier, incomplete fix still failed). Coverage 93%, gate green, ruff and mypy clean.

  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to `docs/testing.md` §5.w):** (1) ***Write the detector before the fix.*** Twice this session I measured a clean suite and nearly declared victory; both times I had removed the thing that looks at the garbage, not the garbage. The rule that saved it: re-introduce the detonator and prove it survives. (2) ***"Daemon" means "won't block exit", not "safe to abandon".*** A daemon thread alive during a collection is a live participant in that collection. (3) ***A crash's location is not its cause when a collector is involved*** — the collector runs wherever it is triggered, so the traceback names the trigger, never the leak. Stop reading the file in the traceback as a suspect. (4) ***Join the worker before undoing anything it reads.*** A `finally` that restores module state while a thread is still executing is a race with a two-minute fuse. (5) ***When a guard has been applied locally twice for the same reason, that is the design telling you it belongs globally.***

- **Whole-application audit: eight parallel reviewers, and one bug wearing twenty disguises (2026-07-28, continued session).** The maintainer's direction was open-ended — *"do an entire audit of the application… make sure we conform to type checking so we look for errors and bugs, and any other best practices… in addition to fixing bugs, fix that"* — so eight reviewers ran concurrently over typing strictness, input validation & security, architecture, UX honesty, latent bugs, test-suite quality, and documentation drift, each told to prove its findings by execution and to edit nothing. What came back was not eight unrelated lists. **It was one finding, restated in seven vocabularies: a fact that is true in one place is not true in another.** The renderer knew and the plumbing didn't; the EAC exporter had the guard and the trust banner didn't; the Settings preview sanitised and the argv builder didn't; the JSON report recorded the interruption and the durable log didn't.

  **The three worst, all fixed with a regression test each (`tests/test_audit_regressions.py`, 41 tests):**
  1. **A forged EAC verification pair.** `verified = converged is True or reads >= 2` — but `rip_count` is how many passes cyanrip *took*, not how many *agreed*. A `-Z` run that exhausts its repeat limit prints **both** "no matches found, but hit repeat limit" *and* "(after 5 rips)", so the `or` short-circuited the measured negative and rendered `Test CRC == Copy CRC` for precisely the tracks that had failed to reproduce. One SHA-256-attested archival document asserted the reads were identical *and*, in its own status report, that they were not. The `else` branch carrying the honest caveat — written for exactly these tracks — was unreachable.
  2. **The `*** INCOMPLETE RIP ***` banner had never rendered in the shipped app.** `_last_outcome` is a dict; the call site read it with `getattr`, which always returned the default. The v0.5.9 fix for run 1's worst bug was live only inside the renderer, through four releases, with a green suite — because the regression test called `render_eac_style_log(outcome_status="cancelled")` directly instead of going through `_write_eac_log`. mypy could not see it either: `getattr(x, "literal", default)` is `Any`.
  3. **`validate_config()` failed open.** One non-string field made the first rule raise, and the single catch-all returned the issues gathered so far — an empty list, which `Config._sanitized()` read as "valid". A hand-edited `config.toml` with `library_dir = 5` therefore let a `..`-traversal template, an absolute template and a 12-digit read offset all survive, defeating a shipped regression test one line at a time.

  **Also fixed:** `%Y` was the one naming token *we* substitute rather than cyanrip, and it took the Year box verbatim — a year of `../.` wrote the album outside the output directory while the Settings preview, which does sanitise, showed the safe string. The read offset reached `cyanrip -s` from three paths that never validated it (and the wizard's spin box enforced ±2000 against the validator's ±5000). Five `read_text(encoding="utf-8")` calls could raise `UnicodeDecodeError` — a `ValueError`, so every `except OSError` missed it; one is `DriveProfileStore.load`, called from `MainWindow.__init__`, so a corrupt cache **locked the user out of the app**. Embed-only cover art (the default) wrote its `metaflac` scratch file as `cover.jpg` and then deleted it, destroying a cover the user had placed there. A slow Cover Art Archive fetch could write album A's result into album B's report. An unmounted NAS/removable library was graded an *error*, and `_sanitized()` resets error-level fields on load, so it silently retargeted the library to `~/Music/rips`. Uninstall orphaned `~/.local/bin/cd-paranoia` — the exact repeat of the `flac` bug (#34) that the teardown docstring memorialises. The green "Bit-perfect" banner dropped failed tracks from its denominator and never heard about post-rip failures. The overall progress bar froze at 95% on every successful rip. The desktop notification sent the reassuring summary captured *before* the read-stability warning overwrote it. `issues: []` read as "clean" for a rip nothing could verify, and `_issues` accepted the CTDB block and never read it.

  **Two structural changes, both worth more than any single fix.** (1) `mypy` no longer sets `ignore_missing_imports` globally: that flag let the type gate **collapse silently** — an unresolvable PySide6 turned the whole Qt surface into `Any` while mypy still printed "Success". Only `musicbrainzngs` and the build-generated `platterpus._build` are exempt now, per module, and `disallow_subclassing_any` is the tripwire that makes the collapse fail loudly; five more zero-cost strictness flags came along free. (2) `conftest.py`'s `os._exit` workaround for a PySide teardown race was discarding pytest's **entire terminal summary** — a red CI run produced progress dots and nothing else: no failing test names, no tracebacks, no `--durations`. It now prints the summary before exiting. Cost me twenty minutes of this very session before I noticed.

  Suite **2056 green**, coverage 93%, ruff + mypy clean. Released as **v0.5.12** — the EAC-layout batch had been *prepared* as v0.5.12 the day before but never tagged, so per the maintainer's standing instruction (*"merge all that is ready and make the new release from that"*) both batches ship as one release rather than leaving a phantom version behind. The run sheet was rewritten around the fixes that can only be proven on the rig, with the Police disc's chronically unstable track 3 as the natural test case for the forged-pair fix.

  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to `architecture.md` and `docs/testing.md`):** (1) ***Test the wiring, at the call site.*** A test that hands a fact to a renderer proves the renderer honours it; it proves nothing about whether anything ever *tells* it. Four of the five recent escapes were verified one layer below the layer that broke, and the one that shipped broken for four releases is the pure form of it. (2) ***A `getattr` with a default is an assertion-free cast.*** It returns `Any`, it silently swallows a shape change, and it defeats every type check downstream of it. Where the type is known, read the attribute; where the shape is external, `isinstance`-narrow at entry. (3) ***Fail open is worse than fail loud, and a broad `try` is how you get it.*** A validator that returns "no problems" because it crashed is indistinguishable from one that found nothing — isolate each rule so a crash costs one rule's findings, and type-check before you parse. (4) ***A guard added in one place is a bug report about every other place.*** The clean-sweep denominator and the offset-variant predicate were each fixed once, in the EAC exporter, and left wrong in the banner, the status line, the disc panel and the JSON. When you add a guard, grep for the predicate. (5) ***Diagnosability is not a nice-to-have.*** A workaround that trades away every failure message to dodge a teardown race is a bad trade, and nobody noticed because the suite was green.

- **EAC-layout parity: checked against a real EAC log, not an idea of the format (2026-07-27, continued session).** The maintainer's direction: *"make sure the EAC log is doing everything it can to possibly be the same as a real EAC log… but if you can, I expect it to look exactly the same"*, then *"you should be able to determine the accuracy vs EAC via the logs alone."* The yardstick was already in the repo — `output_reference/EAC_flac/eac_baseline_police_classics.log`, a genuine EAC V1.8 log of the **same disc on the same drive** — so every claim below is a diff against real EAC bytes. **Parser first** (the rows were unfillable because the facts weren't captured): album/album-artist, `C2 errors:`, `Paranoia level:`, and per-track `Start LSN`/`End LSN`/`Pregap LSN`. **Then the renderer, rebuilt to EAC's section order and row layout:** the **`TOC of the extracted CD` table** — derived from those sectors and **byte-identical to EAC's, all 20 lines, values and column alignment** (EAC's Start/Length columns are pure functions of the disc geometry, so this is derivation, not approximation); the `Artist / Album` disc line; the full archival header (`Read mode` = Secure from the paranoia level, `Make use of C2 pointers` = No from "unsupported by drive", `Fill up missing offset samples with silence` from the overread mode, plus `Delete leading and trailing silent blocks`, `Null samples used in CRC calculations`, `Used interface`, `Utilize accurate stream`, `Gap handling`); the output-format block; and the end-of-rip **status report in EAC's own sentences** (`N track(s) accurately ripped` … `End of status report`) replacing our own phrasing. Unfillable rows read `(not reported by cyanrip)` — one constant wording, so a reader can grep every gap. Attribution header and checksum footer stay deliberately un-EAC: layout is parity, provenance would be forgery (KDD-11/13/28). Also fixed: the rendered title showed cyanrip's U+2236 colon stand-in (`Every Breath You Take∶ …`) — an artefact of our own workaround, not a fact about the disc, so the *rendering* now shows the real colon while on-disk names are untouched. **The "from the logs alone" requirement is now a pinned test:** `parity.compare_logs` reads a real EAC log and ours and pairs all 14 tracks with no other input — and doing that surfaced a genuine result. With v0.5.11 reporting the *shipped* read, the reference rip matches EAC on **13 of 14 tracks (was 12)**: the auto-fix's re-read of track 5 converged on **exactly** the bytes EAC got. Only track 3, the disc's chronically unstable track, still differs. New `tests/test_eac_layout_parity.py` (8 tests) pins layout, labelling, non-impersonation and comparability; two older tests were restated (they encoded the superseded "omit the row entirely" policy). Suite **1992 green**, coverage 93%, ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *An honesty rule and a completeness rule are not opposites — the old export omitted rows to avoid inventing values, which quietly cost real parity.* The better rule is: print EAC's row, fill it when measured, and name the absence when not. Silence and fabrication are both failures to inform; a labelled gap is the only honest third option. (2) *Check format parity against the real artifact.* The TOC block existed in EAC's format for years and we'd never rendered it — not because it was hard, but because nobody diffed our output against a real EAC log we already had committed. (3) *A fix aimed at honesty can raise measured quality.* v0.5.11 was written to stop the log misdescribing the shipped bytes; the side effect is that measured EAC parity went 12/14 → 13/14, because the read we were failing to report was the better one.

- **Third hardware run (v0.5.10): the Test & Copy fix was half a fix — a swapped-in re-rip's CRC named the wrong bytes (2026-07-26, continued session).** The maintainer ran step 1 of the new sheet on the released v0.5.10. Both v0.5.10 fixes *fired* — tracks 3 and 5 rendered as EAC `Test CRC` == `Copy CRC` pairs with the "reads converged" note — but the CRC in the pair was **wrong**. This run, unlike run 2, the auto-fix converged *and* swapped in **both** tracks, and each re-read produced *different* audio from the first pass; the whole-disc `.log` still describes the first pass, so the EAC-compatible log and the JSON report printed the **discarded** read's CRC beside the shipped file's name (track 3: `52DFDF7D` in the log, `3D8FCF0C` on disk per cyanrip's own swap addendum; track 5: `6902BCF0` vs `E0036697`) — and my new convergence note then attached a *proof* to the wrong value, which is worse than the silence it replaced. Run 2 had hidden this twice over: track 5's re-read happened to produce byte-identical audio, and track 3 was never swapped. The same reasoning extends past the CRC: a per-track **AccurateRip verdict is a claim about specific bytes**, so after a swap it too must be the swapped-in read's. **Fix:** the worker now keeps each swapped track's *parsed re-rip record* (`swapped_track_records`), and `_apply_auto_fix_results` folds its measured fields — CRC, AR v1/v2/offset, read counts, status, peak, ReplayGain — over the first pass via a new pure module function `_merge_shipped_track`, which names every field explicitly (readable line by line, and mypy verifies each one — a `**dict` splat can't be checked). Identity fields (`number`, `filename`) are deliberately excluded (the re-rip ran in a throwaway dir), and `_reported()` guarantees a field the re-rip's log didn't print can't blank a real one — deleting a known fact would be worse than the stale value being fixed. 14 regression tests (was 10), including the exact hardware case and the "absent value must not erase" rule; suite **1974 green**, coverage 93%, ruff + mypy clean. **Also verified from the same artifacts:** `Defeat audio cache : Yes`, `Overread : No`, offset `+667 confirmed`, the offset-variant wording, the checksum, 14/14 FLAC verify, cover art in 14 tracks, and `unresolved: false` / `unstable_tracks: []` — so tracks 3 *and* 5 converged this time, a first.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-30 + `architecture.md`):** (1) *When one output already documents a correction, the others owe the same correction.* cyanrip's `.log` had carried a correct written **swap addendum** ("Each CRC below is the SHIPPED file's and supersedes the value recorded above") since the auto-fix shipped. That addendum was a *design note describing a bug we hadn't fixed anywhere else* — the EAC log and JSON had no supersession mechanism at all. A prose caveat in one artifact is not a fix; it's a to-do with good manners. (2) *Adding a claim to data you haven't verified is a downgrade, not a wash.* v0.5.10 turned a missing proof into a **false** proof. Before rendering any new assertion, ask what the assertion is *about* and whether that referent is still the thing on disk. (3) *A bug can hide behind a coincidence for a whole release cycle.* Run 2 concealed this because one re-read happened to be byte-identical and the other was never swapped; the test that would have caught it — a swapped re-rip whose CRC *differs* — is now pinned. When a fix's correctness depends on which of several paths a real run takes, enumerate the paths in tests rather than trusting the run you happened to get. (4) *Enrichers are not all the same kind.* "Fill only, never overwrite" was the right rule for the cache verdict and the wrong rule here; the distinguishing question is *whose read is this record about?*

- **Second hardware run (v0.5.9): every v0.5.9 fix confirmed on the rig, ⭐ INDEX-00 answered, 2 new honesty defects fixed (2026-07-26, continued session).** The maintainer re-ran the released v0.5.9 with Overread OFF and sent a complete 14-track rip's artifacts. **All eight v0.5.9 fixes verified on real hardware:** the cache probe now finishes (disc panel: *"Yes — cache defeated on re-read (measured, cd-paranoia)"*, and the value reaches the EAC log as `Defeat audio cache : Yes` and the JSON as `defeat_audio_cache: true` — KDD-29's wiring proven end-to-end); the wizard reports *"Done."* on a cache-only run; the offset trust line reads **"+667 — confirmed — two independent sources agree (high confidence)"** (KDD-31 earned on hardware, was "entered by hand"); `Overread into Lead-In and Lead-Out : No` (was "(unknown)", correctly derived from cyanrip's *mode* line); and tracks 3 & 5 now read *"Matched an offset-variant pressing — partially accurate (confidence 200) [BF62B1DA] (AR +450)"* instead of the false "not present in AccurateRip database". I re-verified the new log checksum independently (`d0a4b025…` → `True`; a one-CRC tamper → `False`). **⭐ Test 17 answered:** `grep -nE "INDEX|PREGAP"` on the rip's `.cue` returned 14 lines, **every one `INDEX 01 00:00:00`** — no `PREGAP`, no `INDEX 00`. The gap is real on the deployed cyanrip 0.9.3, so KDD-32's build-from-source step is now *required*, not speculative (recorded in the KDD). **Two new defects, both the same shape as v0.5.9's worst one — the app knew something the log wasn't told:** (1) the album's whole-disc `.log` records only the **first** read pass, so track 5 — which the per-track auto-fix re-read at `-Z 2`, whose reads *agreed*, and whose converged copy was swapped in — rendered a lone `Copy CRC`, dropping the Test & Copy proof we had actually earned (KDD-30), while the JSON's per-track record contradicted its own `read_speed.retried_tracks` entry saying `converged: true`. (2) Conversely, track 3 was re-read and **no two reads agreed**, yet its log block was indistinguishable from a clean track's — cyanrip's health line says "No errors occurred" even then, so the durable artifact was *more reassuring* than the on-screen warning the maintainer had already seen. Fixed with one fill-only enricher (`_apply_auto_fix_convergence`) that folds the auto-fix's per-track verdict — positive **and** negative — into the parsed `RipLog` before the report and EAC log are written, plus a *"re-reads did NOT agree — this read is not confirmed reproducible"* CRC caveat and a whole-disc `Read stability :` line. The honesty gate: only a track that both converged **and** was swapped in may be marked proven (a converged re-read that never replaced the file proves nothing about the file that's there), and a track nobody re-read stays unknown. 10 new regression tests; suite green, ruff + mypy clean. Also corrected two stale rows in `docs/eac-parity.md` (cache defeat still described as permanently "(unknown)" per KDD-25; the CRC row predated Test & Copy). Run sheet trimmed again to only what's left.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-30 + `architecture.md` §"Verification & parity"):** (1) *A log is only as honest as the data it's handed* — three shipped honesty bugs in two releases (the incomplete-rip banner, the missing Test & Copy proof, the unflagged unstable track) were all the same failure: Platterpus learned a fact **after** the ripper wrote its log and never told the renderer. `architecture.md` now states the pattern as a contract (fill-only enrichers, run before every rendering) so the next post-rip fact gets wired to *all* surfaces, and names the question to ask: *which renderings need telling?* (2) *Under-claiming has two directions.* The obvious discipline is not asserting proof you didn't earn; the mirror is not withholding a caveat you did measure. A measured non-convergence is data, not an absence — suppressing it lets a genuinely unreproducible track pass as clean. (3) *A "rendering-only" change can still have a data-flow bug* — KDD-30 was scoped as pure presentation because the fields were already parsed; what wasn't checked was whether they were still *accurate* by the time the renderer ran on the path that mattered most.

- **First v0.5.8 hardware run: 5 defects found and fixed, 3 gates closed (2026-07-26, continued session).** The maintainer ran the released v0.5.8 on the Bazzite + BDR-209D rig and sent the artifacts. **Verified green:** the log checksum end-to-end (I recomputed `b291adbe…` independently from the real `(EAC-compatible).log` and it matched byte-for-byte, and the tamper test broke it as designed — KDD-28 proven on hardware); force-stop + post-cancel button recovery; and **the wizard's `dnf install /usr/bin/cd-paranoia` provides-install + `distrobox-export`, which had been flagged *unverified*, both worked on a real Fedora container**. The ⭐ `cd-paranoia -A` capture arrived and is now the committed fixture `tests/fixtures/cdparanoia_A_bdr209d.txt` — **closing the KDD-29 hardware gate**. Notably the conservative signal table was *already right*: fed the real text it returns `defeat=True, cache_sectors=140`. **Five defects found, all fixed** (a workflow fanned out one verifier per suspect plus a completeness critic, each adversarially refuted before acceptance): (1) **`_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S` was 90 s and `-A` exceeded it** — the app log pinned it exactly (probe 14:05:31 → "timed out" 14:07:01 = 90.009 s), because `-A` runs a seven-point seek/read timing sweep plus a full cache analysis; raised to 600 s. (2) An unknown verdict collapsed *five* distinct causes into one undiagnosable "could not be determined" — new `cache_probe.describe()` names the cause, a new `RipBackend.cache_analysis_detail()` seam carries it to the dialog without touching `analyze_drive`'s `bool | None` contract, and an inconclusive verdict now logs the captured output. (3) `DriveSetupResult.ok` meant "got an offset", so **every** cyanrip cache-only run — including a perfect measurement — announced "Finished with issues." (screen readers too); redefined as "every step that RAN produced a value". (4) **The EAC log called offset-variant tracks absent from AccurateRip** — "Track not present in AccurateRip database" for tracks that matched the +450 pressing at confidence 200, contradicting cyanrip's own log *and* our verdict banner; now uses the project's one wording ("matched an offset-variant pressing — partially accurate") and still never claims an exact match. (5) `Overread into Lead-In and Lead-Out` rendered "(unknown)" though cyanrip states the mode outright — now parsed, keyed on the **mode** line after verifying against two real logs that the `+2 frames` count is printed identically in both modes. Bonus catch: the dialog described an *undefeatable* cache reassuringly ("doesn't cache audio, so Platterpus doesn't need to read around a cache") — the polarity was effectively inverted on the one genuinely worrying outcome; it now warns. 13 new regression tests; suite green, ruff + mypy clean, coverage floor held. The run was cut short because **Overread was ON and hung the drive at 99.76% of track 14**, so no `.cue` was written and ⭐ test 17 (INDEX 00, the last KDD-32 input) is still unanswered — the run sheet now opens with an Overread-OFF warning and a run-1 results table.
  - **Two further defects the adversarial-refute pass caught (worth recording — both were in *my own fixes* or invisible without cross-reading):** (a) the overread fix was **incomplete** — cyanrip labels the line `Underread mode:` whenever the frame count is negative, and that sign comes straight from the read offset, so every negative-offset drive would have kept reporting "(unknown)"; a refuter found this by reading cyanrip's own `cyanrip_log.c`/`cyanrip_main.c` rather than trusting the one sample log. (b) **HIGH severity, and the most serious finding of the session:** the EAC-compatible log for the interrupted rip rendered 13 consecutive "Copy OK" blocks, an empty conclusive section, and a *valid* SHA-256 footer — a **checksum-attested document that read as a clean, complete rip of a 13-track disc**, when the disc had 14 and the ripper was force-stopped mid-track-14. The app knew (the JSON recorded `status: "cancelled"`); the renderer was simply never told. Now an `*** INCOMPLETE RIP (cancelled) — this log covers 13 of 14 disc tracks ***` banner sits *above* the checksum (so it is covered by it and can't be stripped silently), and an absent end-of-rip summary is stated instead of left blank. The refuter also corrected the first proposed wording, which would have asserted "the ripper was stopped" on a condition that *also* fires for a successful-but-trimmed log — the cause now lives only in the banner, which appears only when the caller actually measured the outcome.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-29):** (1) *A probe whose failure modes are indistinguishable is a probe you cannot debug.* The verdict logic was correct from the start; a wrong 90-second ceiling plus a single catch-all "could not be determined" hid it. Any new external probe should name its failure cause and log the tool's own output on an inconclusive result **from day one** — a few lines that turn a hardware round-trip into a log read. (2) *Cross-check your own surfaces against each other.* The offset-variant bug was invisible in isolation but obvious the moment the EAC log, cyanrip's log, and the verdict banner were read side by side — three renderings of one parsed `RipLog` that disagreed. Worth doing deliberately whenever a new output surface is added. (3) *When a boolean means "is the good thing true?", say what it measures in the wording.* `can_defeat_cache=False` got a reassuring sentence because the text was written as if the field meant "does this drive have a cache". (4) *Timeouts derived from guesswork need a real-hardware number before they gate a feature* — this is the second time a too-tight probe ceiling misreported a working setup (cf. the 10 s container cold-start cap).

- **v0.5.8 released: hardware run sheet + full doc sweep (2026-07-24, continued session).** Closed the cycle. **(1) New `docs/hardware-test-checklist.md`** — the fillable, every-step-written-out run sheet the maintainer asked for, replacing the transient scratchpad checklists of earlier sessions with a committed, reused-each-cycle artifact (indexed in `docs/README.md`, cross-linked from `test-plan.md`, which stays the full reference). Ordered so it's actually runnable: setup (incl. the new wizard step + the reminder that the EAC log is **off by default** and must be enabled for tests 3–4) → this release's four features → the two v0.5.7 features never hardware-tested (per-track selection, offset-variant re-read) → **six never-tested-before risk areas** → a regression sweep → **INDEX 00 last, because it swaps the cyanrip binary** and every earlier result would otherwise be on a different ripper. Two steps are starred as *send this back*: the raw `cd-paranoia -A` capture and the `.cue`'s `grep INDEX` output. **(2) Documentation sweep** — the README carried a **stale** capability row (cache-defeat "attempted, not measured") plus two more now-wrong rows; updated both matrices, the "in short" paragraph, and the status banner; TASKS.md's "only remaining EAC gap" claim and hardware-gated queue refreshed (incl. the honestly-flagged unverified `dnf install /usr/bin/cd-paranoia` step). **(3) Release** — version 0.5.8, CHANGELOG rolled under a dated heading with a compare link, all 12 docs touched this cycle restamped, suite green + ruff/mypy clean, merged to `main`, release dispatched.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *A test plan has an execution order, and getting it wrong invalidates results* — the INDEX-00 test replaces `~/.local/bin/cyanrip`, so it has to run last; likewise the EAC-log tests are meaningless until the off-by-default toggle is on. Encode ordering + preconditions in the sheet, not in the tester's head. (2) *"All documentation updated" means hunting the stale claims, not just adding new ones* — the README's cache-defeat row had confidently said "not measured" since KDD-25 and would have shipped contradicting the release's headline feature; a doc sweep should grep for *superseded* statements, not only append. (3) *Commit the run sheet* — the earlier per-session checklists were scratchpad files that vanished; a stable, indexed, stamped doc updated in place is the version that survives and can't drift from the docs index.

- **INDEX 00 pre-gap → cyanrip soft-fork made the committed mechanism — EAC gap #4 of 4 (2026-07-24, continued session).** The maintainer directed forking cyanrip "for this and other issues that are upstream and we need to fix." INDEX 00 turns out to be a *fork* problem, not a Platterpus one: Platterpus consumes cyanrip's `.cue` verbatim (never post-processes it), so INDEX 00 markers appear the moment the `~/.local/bin/cyanrip` we run emits them — and cyanrip **`master` already** synthesises the track-1 pregap and writes `INDEX 00`/`PREGAP`/`INDEX 01` (source-confirmed 2026-07-07); the deployed 2-year-old v0.9.3.1 COPR tag doesn't. The exact-pregap + HTOA layer is the open **PR #115** (subchannel-Q via MMC). **Decision (KDD-32):** elevate the soft-fork (already scaffolded: `docs/cyanrip-fork.md`, `docs/cyanrip-upstream.md`, `scripts/cyanrip/`) from "prepared" to the committed delivery mechanism — the `ripping` container builds cyanrip from the `platterpus` integration branch (`master` + our topic branches) instead of the stale tag, which yields INDEX 00 for free and carries PR #115 until it lands. Enumerated the fork's target set (colon-fix ⭐, encoder-opts, `feat/pregap` carrying #115, and a future AR-CRC rider for the KDD-31 offset finder). Added Contribution 3 (INDEX 00 / PR #115) to the soft-fork doc, a KDD-32 note atop the roadmap, KDD-32 in PLANNING; bumped the two fork docs' stamps. **No Platterpus code + no user-facing change this session** — forking/building/PR-#115-testing all require a repo-seeded session or the rig (this cloud session is scoped to platterpus, has no C toolchain, no disc), and the container build-from-source is the real (maintainer-accepted) new maintenance commitment. `[skip changelog]` — pure design-record/planning commit.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-32):** (1) *Locate the gap in the right layer before "fixing" it* — INDEX 00 looked like a Platterpus cue-writing task but is entirely a "which cyanrip do we build" question; the honest fix is building `master`/the fork, not writing subchannel code we'd have to maintain. (2) *A soft-fork is a maintenance commitment, name it as one* — building cyanrip from source in the container (vs. a distro package) is the real cost of every "just carry the patch upstream" plan; KDD-32 records it as the accepted trade, not a free win. (3) *Carry, don't re-author, a live upstream PR* — #115 is actively reviewed; the high-value move is hardware-testing + carrying it on a topic branch, never a rival PR.

- **Read offset auto-confirmed by AccurateRip — EAC gap #3 of 4 (2026-07-24, continued session).** EAC's Key-Disc offset finder derives a drive's offset by matching a known disc against AccurateRip. Two honest findings shaped the call: (1) our offset is *already correct* for listed drives (from the bundled AR drive list, +667 verified on the BDR-209D), so a from-scratch finder mainly helps *unlisted* drives; and (2) a real from-scratch finder needs an AccurateRip **CRC calculator** we don't have in-tree (cyanrip computes AR matches itself; `ctdb/crc.py` is a different algorithm) plus raw-PCM extraction + per-offset search — a heavy, hardware-gated lift that duplicates cyanrip. **Decision (KDD-31): ship the equal-or-stronger *confirmation*, defer the from-scratch finder.** A rip that matches the AccurateRip *global consensus* proves the applied offset is correct on the user's own unit — stronger than a one-shot Key-Disc check because it re-earns itself every matching rip. New `OffsetSource.ACCURATERIP_CONFIRMED` (MEDIUM alone; HIGH only by agreement); `_confirm_offset_from_accuraterip` records the applied offset under it after a rip with ≥1 AR-verified track (+ an offset override applied), and `reconcile_offset` promotes LIST+CONFIRMED (same value) → CONFIRMED/HIGH, so the disc panel reads "confirmed — two independent sources agree". Reuses the whole drive-profile agreement model (KDD-23) — no new storage/UI, just a source + one post-rip hook; best-effort, never raises, records only a real match. Tests: reconcile (LIST+CONFIRMED→HIGH), source confidence/label, and the helper via a fake window (records on match / skips no-match / skips no-override / never-raises). Docs: KDD-31, CHANGELOG, parity matrix row, User Guide. The from-scratch finder for unlisted drives is explicitly deferred to the cyanrip soft-fork roadmap. Suite green, ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-31):** (1) *"Confirm the value we have" can beat "find it from scratch"* — the goal of an offset finder is a trustworthy offset, and a global-consensus match is a stronger proof than one key disc; we didn't need an AR-CRC calculator to deliver the actual value. (2) *Draw the honest scope line explicitly* — the part we can't do well now (from-scratch finding for unlisted drives) is recorded as fork/hardware work in the roadmap, not silently dropped, so "done" doesn't over-claim.

- **EAC-style Test & Copy — EAC gap #2 of 4 (2026-07-24, continued session).** cyanrip's `-Z N` secure re-read ("rip until N reads' checksums agree") *is* EAC's Test & Copy guarantee (two independent reads produce identical audio) — we just weren't presenting it that way. Purely a rendering + one-checkbox change (the parser already captured `rip_count` and `secure_rerip_converged` from the KDD-27 era): `eac_log_export._crc_lines` now renders a track confirmed by ≥2 agreeing reads as an EAC-style **Test CRC == Copy CRC** pair (provably equal — that's what convergence means) with an honest "confirmed across N secure re-reads" note; a single-read track still shows only a Copy CRC (never a fabricated test read); a native dual-read `test_crc` (whipper) renders verbatim. Surfaced the previously hand-edit-only `Config.secure_rerip_dynamic` as a Settings checkbox **"Verify every track with a second read (EAC-style Test & Copy)"** (checked = whole-disc `-Z`; default unchecked = fast path) so the every-track mode is reachable without editing the TOML. Docs: KDD-30, CHANGELOG, parity matrix row (Absent → Present), User Guide + the guide-currency/tooltip maps updated (the old "no checkbox" test flipped to a round-trip-through-the-checkbox test). Suite green, ruff + mypy clean. The cheapest of the four gaps — no new parser work, honesty rule identical to the checksum/cache decisions (match EAC's rigor, never fake its output).
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-30):** (1) *"Present the guarantee we already have" beats "imitate the mechanism"* — adding a literal second full read-pass to mimic EAC's two-pass structure would double rip time to re-derive what `-Z` convergence already proves; the honest move was to render the converged CRC as the Test/Copy pair, not re-rip. (2) *A default that only acts on marginal cases isn't the whole feature* — the dynamic fast path double-reads only AccurateRip-missing tracks, so "Test & Copy" wasn't really reachable disc-wide until `secure_rerip_dynamic` got a checkbox; surfacing an existing hand-edit flag turned a power-user escape hatch into a real feature.

- **Measured cache-defeat verdict via `cd-paranoia -A` — EAC gap #1 of 4 (2026-07-24, continued session).** The maintainer wants all four remaining EAC gaps closed as *honest, equal-or-stronger* features and asked what cache-defeat specifically needs; after grounding the answer in KDD-25 (which had already scoped `cd-paranoia -A` as the deferred path), they approved adding `cd-paranoia` as a new dependency. Built the whole feature: a new **`adapters/cache_probe.py`** (Critical Rule #1 adapter over `cd-paranoia -A`; pure argv builder + never-raises parser with a hypothesis property test + off-thread `probe_cache_defeat`; verdict defaults to `None`/unknown and only becomes Yes/No on a clear signal — never a forged "Yes"); `CyanripBackend.analyze_drive` delegates to it; a new `RipBackend.supports_cache_analysis()` (True for cyanrip, distinct from `supports_offset_detection()` which stays False) so the Set up drive wizard offers an **Analyse cache** action even though cyanrip has no offset finder; the `DriveSetupWorker` now only runs the offset finder when the backend actually has one (cyanrip's analyze-only path no longer prints a spurious "can't auto-detect"). The verdict rides the **dormant `DriveProfile.cache_defeat` seam** (built whipper-era, KDD-23) through the single `_record_drive_fact` funnel, shows in the disc panel's new **Cache defeat** row, and is folded into the parsed `RipLog.ripping_info.defeat_audio_cache` (fill-only `dataclasses.replace`) so the EAC-compatible log + JSON report carry a *measured* Yes/No instead of "(unknown)". `cd-paranoia` is registered `optional`/`from_setup_wizard`; the wizard installs+exports it as a **final, non-blocking** step (not part of `is_ready()`), so a system where the package can't be resolved still sets up the ripper fully. New `tests/test_cache_probe.py` (+hypothesis never-raises), `tests/test_cache_defeat_wiring.py`, and additions to the backend/checks/panel/dialog/worker/host-setup suites (the worker + host-setup tests updated for the intended behaviour change). Docs: **KDD-29** (+ KDD-25 superseded-note + KDD-13 row), DEPENDENCIES.md (new row + updated cache note), dependency-contracts.md (`cd-paranoia -A` contract), parity-doc matrix row, User Guide. Suite green, ruff + mypy clean, coverage floor held. **Hardware-gated finalization (BDR-209D rig):** capture real `-A` output → tune the centralized signal phrases → add a real-output regression fixture, and confirm `/usr/bin/cd-paranoia` provides-install + export succeed in the container.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-29):** (1) *Check for a dormant seam before building storage* — `DriveProfile.cache_defeat`/`_record_drive_fact(cache_defeat=…)`/`DriveSetupResult.can_defeat_cache`/`analyze_drive` were all already wired from the whipper era; the feature was ~80% "connect the producer + a display surface", not new plumbing. (2) *Split a capability that was one flag into two* — `supports_offset_detection()` conflated "can find the offset" with "can calibrate the drive at all"; adding `supports_cache_analysis()` let cyanrip do the half it can (measure cache) without pretending to do the half it can't (find offset), and cleanly gated the worker's finder. (3) *An optional tool must never gate the core* — cd-paranoia installs as a deliberately-last wizard step outside `is_ready()`, so a package-resolution failure shows one ✗ row and nothing else, never a blocked ripper. (4) *Honest-by-default for a hardware-tuned parser* — ship conservative (unknown unless a clear signal), centralize the signal phrases in one place, and pin the *logic* in unit tests while the *phrases* get pinned by the first real capture — the same pattern the cyanrip-log parser used.

- **EAC-parity, reframed as "archival correctness, honestly not-EAC": log integrity checksum at least as strong as EAC's (2026-07-24, continued session).** The maintainer asked what "full EAC parity for audiophiles" needs, then corrected the framing decisively: *"I am not asking you to forge anything to look like EAC, we want to be up front we are NOT EAC. What I want is a checksum that is at least as strong as EAC's. Same with everything else."* That dissolved my earlier "a real EAC checksum is impossible/forgery" framing — the goal was never forgery; it's *equal-or-stronger rigor, honestly labelled as ours*. Applied to the one gap that was a concrete, non-hardware-gated deliverable: the EAC-*layout* companion log (`eac_log_export.py`) had a "NOT signed by EAC" disclaimer where EAC's `==== Log checksum <hex> ====` sits, but **no integrity checksum of its own**. Added one: a **plain SHA-256 of every byte above the footer**, on a self-describing line (`==== Platterpus log checksum (SHA-256 of the text above; NOT an EAC checksum): <hex> ====`). Equal hash strength to EAC's, but **openly verifiable** — `head -n -1 "<name> (EAC-compatible).log" | sha256sum` reproduces it with no secret key, where EAC's is SHA-256 obfuscated with a fixed key only its Logchecker checks. New pure `verify_eac_style_log_checksum()` (never-raises, returns `None` for any log lacking our footer); 7 regression tests (present, never-EAC's-marker, openly-verifiable, accepts-untouched, rejects-tampered, returns-None-without-footer, never-raises-on-garbage). Docs: **KDD-28** (splits EAC's checksum into *integrity* — which we match/beat — vs *provenance* — which we refuse to forge — refining KDD-11's "weaker integrity" note and the two stale KDD-13 lines); CHANGELOG; the parity-doc matrix row (Unsigned → "Present — our own checksum, not EAC's"); the User Guide's EAC-log bullet. Suite green for the touched files, ruff + mypy clean, doc-stamps pass. Landed under `[Unreleased]` (no version bump). **Still open (the "same with everything else" gaps, presented to the maintainer to steer):** INDEX 00 pre-gap markers (cyanrip PR #115 / cdrdao fallback — hardware-gated on the BDR-209D); cache-defeat verdict (stays "(unknown)" honestly until measurable); the Key-Disc offset finder (nice-to-have — the offset is already correct from the drive list); Test&Copy is already met-or-beaten by AccurateRip+CTDB+`-Z`.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-28):** (1) *A log checksum is two guarantees, not one* — **integrity** (was this altered?) and **provenance** (who made it?). EAC's single footer does both because its key is secret; conflating them is what made "match EAC's checksum" *look* like it required forgery. Separate them and the honest path is obvious: match the integrity guarantee with a plain SHA-256, never reproduce the provenance signature. (2) *"At least as strong" can mean "more open"* — an un-obfuscated SHA-256 anyone can verify with `sha256sum` is a **stronger** integrity signal than one gated behind a single vendor's tool, not a weaker one; strength isn't only bit-length. (3) *When a maintainer corrects the framing, re-derive the plan from their words, not from my prior scoping* — the correction turned an "impossible" item into the highest-value shippable one.

- **v0.5.6 hardware checklist complete + tracks 3 & 5 read-instability diagnosed → opt-in offset-variant re-read (2026-07-23, continued session).** The maintainer finished the v0.5.6 remaining-tests sheet on the BDR-209D. **All functional fixes PASS:** the stall banner fired on a deliberate Overread-ON hang and named overread (confirmed in `log.txt`: `force-stopping drive (auto trigger)` → `force_stop_drive: killed=True ejected=True`); Open-folder + View-log work after cancel/force-stop; `cyanrip │ …` lines persist to `log.txt` in real time (thousands of them); single progress bar; guide covers every setting. So the whole freeze-recovery + observability batch is validated on real hardware. **The real finding (test 7):** a `--compare` of two v0.5.6 rips of *The Police — Every Breath You Take: The Classics* showed 11/13 comparable tracks byte-identical but **tracks 3 & 5 differing each rip** — yet both rips called them offset-variant ("partially accurate") matches at confidence 200. An agent traced the cause: dynamic secure re-rip (`read_speed_ladder.tracks_failing_accuraterip`) treats an offset-variant match as *proven* and skips the `-Z` re-read (deliberate, documented — but the hardware disproves "stable" for those tracks). **Fix (KDD-27): opt-in re-read.** New `Config.rerip_offset_variant` (default **False**) + Settings toggle; when on, `tracks_failing_accuraterip(..., include_offset_variant=True)` returns offset-variant tracks so they get the same secure `-Z` re-read and converge on a reproducible read. Default off preserves the fast path (offset-variant is usually a genuine pressing difference, and re-reading every compilation would regress responsiveness). Guide + both currency tests + the validation-completeness gate updated; `RipParameters.rerip_offset_variant` threaded through. Suite **1881 green**, ruff + mypy clean. Deferred: an automatic single-rip "not confirmed reproducible" flag on offset-variant tracks (the amber verdict + `--compare` already surface it) — noted as a follow-up.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to KDD-27):** (1) *An offset-variant AccurateRip match is not proof of a reproducible read* — it confirms the audio matches a shifted pressing, but the same track can offset-variant-match two rips with different full-track audio; only a cross-rip `--compare` (or a converged `-Z` re-read) proves reproducibility. (2) *When hardware disproves a documented design premise, refine it with an opt-in, don't silently flip the default* — flipping "offset-variant = re-read" would slow every compilation/remaster rip; a default-off setting fixes the reported case without a broad responsiveness regression. (3) *Use an agent to pin a deliberate-vs-accidental behaviour before changing it* — the trace found the skip was intentional (`read_speed_ladder.py:265`) with a written rationale, which turned "fix the bug" into "refine the premise + KDD," the right framing for a maintainer review.

- **Per-track rip selection + Settings-tooltip currency (2026-07-23, continued session; v0.5.6 validated on hardware first).** The maintainer ran the published v0.5.6 AppImage (build `0cd0112`) on the BDR-209D with Overread OFF: a clean 14-track rip, 0 errors, 12/14 exact at AccurateRip confidence 200 + 2 offset-variant (tracks 3 & 5), FLAC-verify 14/14, CTDB no-match-but-crc-validated (expected with 2 offset-variant tracks) — confirming the freeze fixes, the drive-firmware line (1.1/1.2 PASS), and real-time logs during a rip (2.1 PASS). Two dev-note feature requests from that run, both built after the maintainer chose the interaction model: **(1) Per-track "Rip?" selection.** A leading checkbox column in the track table (all ticked by default) + a right-click menu on highlighted rows ("Rip only these" / include / exclude / select all / none). Start rips whatever's ticked; all-ticked → whole disc (empty `-l`), a subset → cyanrip `-l`. The backend/worker already accepted `only_tracks` (built for the per-track auto-fix re-rip), so this was a `RipParameters.only_tracks` field + wiring `_run_rip`'s main pass + the UI; `validate()` blocks a zero-selection start. Model gained a `CheckStateRole` column (indices shifted: Rip?=0, #=1, …, Status=5) + `selection_changed` signal. **(2) Settings tooltip currency.** Most controls already had rich tooltips; filled the last gaps (output/working dir, disc template, both unknown templates, Picard, metaflac) and added `test_every_documented_setting_has_a_tooltip` — it maps each guide-documented user-facing `Config` field to its Settings widget and asserts a non-empty tooltip, tying tooltip coverage to the same field list `test_user_guide_currency` uses (imported from it). Guide gained a "choose which tracks to rip" step. Landed on `main` under `[Unreleased]` (no version bump — a v0.5.7 release is deferred until the maintainer finishes the v0.5.6 hardware checklist so any fix batches in). Suite **1878 green**, ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *A feature whose backend seam already exists is mostly UI* — `only_tracks`/`-l` was built for auto-fix re-rips, so per-track selection reused it end-to-end; check for an existing seam before adding plumbing. (2) *Adding a model column shifts every index* — the fix is to route all column access through the `_COL_*` constants (code and tests), then the shift is a one-line renumber; literal `index(row, 0)` in tests is the thing that breaks. (3) *Tie a second currency check to the first's classification* — the tooltip test imports the guide test's user-facing-field set instead of re-listing it, so there's one authoritative "these are the user-facing settings" and the two enforcement tests can't disagree.

- **Real-hardware UI polish: drive identity, one progress bar, guide currency enforced (2026-07-22, same session, follow-on to the freeze fixes).** Four maintainer requests from the same hardware run, all in-scope and reversible: **(1) Drive identity in the disc panel.** The panel's "Drive:" row showed only `/dev/sr0`; it now shows make · model · **firmware** · device ("PIONEER BD-RW BDR-209D · firmware 1.51 · /dev/sr0") — the firmware revision is what a hardware bug report actually needs, and it matches cyanrip's own log header ("Device model: PIONEER BD-RW BDR-209D 1.51"). The `DriveDescriptor` already carried vendor/model/release (sysfs `vendor`/`model`/`rev`); wired the full descriptor through `_on_drive_changed` → `set_drive`, formatted by a pure, tested `format_drive_summary` (collapses sysfs double-spacing, omits missing firmware, falls back to the bare device). The value stays a copy-selectable label. **(2) Removed the per-track progress bar** (shipped v0.5.0, task #13): it duplicated the current-task bar in the progress pane — same percent in two places — so the grid's Status column is back to plain "⟳ Ripping"/"✓ Done" text and live progress lives in the one two-tier bar. Stripped the whole `PROGRESS_ROLE`/`TrackStatusDelegate`/`set_current_progress`/`on_rip_progress` machinery + its worker connection; regression test asserts none of it comes back. **(3) About screen + copyable fields — verified, no change needed:** About is built entirely from runtime values (version/build/Qt/PySide/platform) plus `help_content` metadata (license `GPL-3.0-only` matches `pyproject`, repo URL casing consistent), so it's accurate by construction; disc-panel value labels were already `TextSelectableByMouse|Keyboard`. **(4) User-guide currency, now enforced.** Filled the guide's gaps (Working directory, Read speed, completion notification, EAC-compatible log, back-cover/booklet art) and added `tests/test_user_guide_currency.py`: every `Config` field must be either documented in `help_content.USER_GUIDE` (mapped to a phrase that must appear) or explicitly listed as internal — a new setting that's neither trips the test, so the guide can't silently fall behind the settings. Suite **1871 green** (+15 net new tests), ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *When two UI surfaces show the same number, one is noise* — the per-track bar and the current-task bar were both driven by the same task-percent stream; the fix was to delete the duplicate, not reconcile them. (2) *Enforce guide currency the same way doc-stamps are enforced* — a curated field→phrase map plus a completeness check over `Config` fields turns "remember to update the guide" (which rots) into a test that fails on the next undocumented setting. Graduated as the pattern behind `test_user_guide_currency.py`; same shape as `test_doc_version_stamps.py` and the retired-field guard. (3) *cyanrip's log header is a second source for drive identity* ("Device model" + C2 support), richer than sysfs at rip time — worth remembering if the panel ever wants to show C2/offset capability post-scan.

- **Real-hardware freeze diagnosed + three observability/recovery fixes (2026-07-22, continued session).** The maintainer ran v0.5.5 on the Bazzite + Pioneer BDR-209D rig and hit a hang: the progress bar sat near 100 % (~99.47 %) for ~23 min with no way to tell whether it was frozen; a force-cancel followed, and afterward the "Open rip folder" button didn't work. From the uploaded artifacts (cyanrip `.log` with 13 of 14 tracks, a `.platterpus.json` status `failed` at elapsed 4343 s, an empty `.cue`, and a Settings screenshot showing **Overread ON**) the cause was clear: **overread (`-O`) hung the drive on the last track's lead-out** — exactly the upstream-warned "may freeze if unsupported by drive." Immediate workaround given to the maintainer (Settings → Overread → uncheck); overread stays off by default. Because a freeze is observability-blind regardless of *which* flag triggers it, shipped three fixes so a future hang is visible and recoverable: **(1) Real-time logs + access buttons during a rip** — "Open rip folder" and "View log" are enabled from rip *start* (not just on success), "View log" opens the live app log (`log.txt`) while the rip runs, and cyanrip's own stdout is mirrored line-by-line to the app log under Debug logging (`rip_worker`), so a frozen run leaves a real-time on-disk trail. **(2) Stall/liveness watchdog** — a GUI-thread `QTimer` (`_rip_liveness_timer`, 5 s tick; `_RIP_STALL_THRESHOLD_S = 45 s`) tracks the last `log_line`/`progress`/`status` signal and shows a warning banner when the ripper goes quiet, naming overread as the likely culprit when it's on; the banner clears the instant output resumes. The watchdog lives on the GUI thread and only *reads* worker-updated state (never blocks). **(3) "Open rip folder" after cancel/partial** — root cause was `_finish_rip` calling `set_log_path(None)` on a non-success, which disabled the folder/log buttons; now the in-progress folder is tracked separately (`_inprogress_rip_dir` in `rip_progress`) from the last finished rip, so the folder and live log stay reachable through cancel/freeze/failure. +13 regression tests (8 in `test_ui_rip_progress`, 5 in `test_ui_main_window`), suite **1869 green** (3 skipped), ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *A true freeze produces no output, so the worker's own progress-driven stall detector can't fire* — `RipHandle.log_lines()` blocks in `for line in self._process.stdout`, so on a hung drive the worker simply parks there and never emits another signal. Stall detection therefore has to live **on the GUI thread** as a timer that watches the *absence* of signals, not inside the worker waiting for one. (2) *A failure/cancel path must not tear down the user's means of investigating it* — disabling the folder/log buttons on a non-success rip is exactly backwards: that's precisely when the user needs to reach the partial output and the log. (3) **Overread confirmed unsafe on the BDR-209D** — graduated to `docs/dependency-contracts.md` (the `-O` row now carries the dated real-hardware finding); the GUI default (off) is correct and should stay.

- **Three maintainer-gated blockers decided + executed as far as scope allows (2026-07-21, continued session).** After the small-batch above, surveyed the remaining backlog: the doable-now work was essentially cleared, and what's left needs the maintainer's hardware, a decision, or a cyanrip-repo session. Wrote a decision brief on the three decision-gated items; the maintainer chose, and each was taken to its in-scope limit: **(1) Update signing — offline key + `cryptography`.** Shipped the *verify* side of Ed25519/minisign release signing (KDD-26): new `update_signing.py` (pure, never-raises; parses `.minisig`, Ed25519-verifies via `cryptography`, handles legacy `Ed` + prehashed `ED`/BLAKE2b, streams the prehash for the big AppImage; trusted-comment global sig deliberately unchecked), wired **fail-closed** into `update_install.py` after the SHA-256 gate. Ships **dormant** — `PUBLIC_KEY_B64` empty ⇒ SHA-256-only, unchanged — until the maintainer bakes in an *offline* key (the crux: a CI-held key would only re-prove what the SLSA attestation already does). Added `cryptography` (the approved new dep), 19 tests incl. every tamper/negative case with spec-accurate fixtures, `docs/release-signing.md` (the out-of-CI signing ritual + the fail-closed transition), SECURITY.md. Remaining is maintainer-only: keygen, bake the key, sign the first release. **(2) CTDB repair — "D → B" (recorded, KDD-14).** Not bundling .NET (A) nor porting parity to Python (C): keep the manual workflow (D) now, wire `ctdb-cli` as an optional dep-subsystem tool (B) only when a real user hits an uncorrectable-error rip. Now a settled, demand-gated deferral, not "undecided." **(3) Reproducible build — Option A plumbing.** `build/lock-requirements.sh` (pip `--report`-based hashed-lock generator) + a guarded `build_appimage.sh` block that, when `requirements.lock` exists, `pip download --require-hashes`-es the closure into a verified wheelhouse and installs python-appimage's per-line deps offline from it. Opt-in/additive — no lock ⇒ today's install unchanged; full-AppImage reproducibility validation is the maintainer's real-build step. Also did the one pure-code housekeeping leftover: removed the vestigial `DependencySpec.fallback_tiers` (dead since the TD-3 cascade removal) and blessed `tier` as a descriptive label. Suite **1858 green**, ruff + mypy clean at every commit.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *for release signing, the key's location is the decision, not the crypto* — an Ed25519 signature keyed in CI collapses to "trust CI," which the SLSA attestation already gives; only an **offline** key (signing outside CI) is a real gain, so that's the axis to settle first. (2) *ship a security gate dormant + fail-closed* — verify-only code with an empty baked-in key changes nothing until the maintainer arms it, and once armed it refuses rather than falls through; the arming release must carry the first signature (a transition worth documenting, not discovering). (3) *the sandbox's `cryptography` had a broken Rust/cffi backend* — top-level `import cryptography` worked but the ed25519 primitive panicked on a missing `_cffi_backend`; `pip install cffi` fixed it. Watch for "imports but the primitive panics" when a distro-packaged crypto lib is half-present.

- **v0.5.0 released, then a doc-drift fix + a second small feature/housekeeping batch (2026-07-21, continued session).** Merged PR #86 (the feature batch below) once CI went green, cut **v0.5.0** via the release `workflow_dispatch` (AppImage + `.sha256` + `.zsync` + install scripts attached, published as a pre-release per the `v0.*` convention; PyPI publish succeeded), and recreated the session branch fresh from the new `main`. Then, on the maintainer's "keep going": **(1) Doc-drift fix (maintainer-caught).** The public README still read *"Status: v0.4.x"* after the release. Root cause: the doc-stamp convention bumps a footer to the `__version__` *current at commit time* — always one release behind what the change ships in — and **nothing enforced it**, so all 44 docs the v0.5.0 cycle touched shipped stamped v0.4.24. Fixed the content (README banner → v0.5.x + the v0.5.0 features + the corrected overread wording; SECURITY.md → v0.5.x), restamped all 44 cycle docs, and added a **gating** `tests/test_doc_version_stamps.py` (exactly one footer per doc; no future stamps; **any doc changed since the latest release tag must stamp the current `__version__`** — so the release-prep bump itself forces the restamp). CI's `test` job now checks out full history for the tag diff; the release checklist (CLAUDE.md) gained the restamp as step 3. **(2) MP3 VBR-quality Settings knob** — the plumbed-but-fixed `mp3_vbr_quality` field got a spinbox (0–9, enabled only for MP3 output, not goal-driven). **(3) Cue-sheet button** — a *View cue* button beside View log/report opens cyanrip's `.cue` in the in-app viewer; derived from the log path (so a library move repoints it) and enabled only when the file is present (10th prominent-button mnemonic, `&u`). **(4) EAC gap-handling parity closed as already-satisfied** — the "we set no gap mode" concern was false: cyanrip's default *is* EAC's, verified verbatim against upstream README + source (0.9.3.1 + master); the exposable `-p` overrides (`merge`/`drop`/`track`) are per-track and archival-unsafe (`drop` deletes audio, `track` renumbers), so there was no knob worth adding — corrected `test-plan.md`/`eac-parity.md`, recorded the `-p` contract in `dependency-contracts.md`, closed the TASKS item. **(5) Housekeeping** — logged the v0.5.0 dependency review (no new deps), closed three stale whipper-era TASKS rows, and re-verified the upstream "verify-before-you-invest" checklist live (cyanrip PR #115 still open/mid-revision; libcdio-paranoia #3 still open, zero comments). Suite **1839 green**, ruff + mypy clean at every commit.
