  - **Lesson (durable, graduated to CLAUDE.md release checklist + `docs/README.md`):** a *convention* that's only trusted rots — the doc-stamp footer drifted a whole release because the bump timing (version-at-commit vs. version-at-ship) was inherently one behind and nothing tested it. The forcing function is a test that diffs docs against the latest release tag and demands the current `__version__`, so the release bump *is* the trigger. Same session, same shape as the earlier `-x` and udev/ReplayGain lessons: **verify the premise before building** — the "EAC gap-mode exposure" task dissolved once cyanrip's own README was read (its default already equals EAC), turning a feature into a doc correction + an honest closure.

- **Feature batch: overread toggle, cross-FS warning, per-track progress bars, library auto-move + two backlog closures (2026-07-21, same session, maintainer-scoped batch).** The maintainer picked "small batch + both features" for one merge → one release. Shipped, one atomic commit each: **(1) Overread toggle** (Settings → Overread → `Config.force_overread` → cyanrip **`-O`**, off by default = EAC's baseline setting). Verifying the flag against upstream *before* wiring it caught a would-be shipped bug: the contracts doc/TASKS/scorecard all said the flag is `-x`, but **`-x` doesn't exist in cyanrip's getopt** (checked on the deployed 0.9.3.1 *and* master) — the docs' letter came from whipper's own `-x/--force-overread`. Same check corrected `-f`'s description (it IS a find-drive-offset mode, deliberately unused since the 2026-06 mis-scrape — not "force-overread"); a vetted `-f` re-integration is now a tracked maintainer call. The whipper-era retired-field guard was deliberately updated (force_overread is back as a live field). **(2) Cross-FS naming warning** (maintainer approved — closes the 2026-07-08 trust audit's last open item): `settings_validation.cross_fs_hazards` warns (never blocks) on templates whose *literal* text is Windows/NTFS-unsafe; value-driven hazards stay the documented limitation (ripper owns naming). **(3) Live per-track progress bars**: `PROGRESS_ROLE` + `TrackStatusDelegate` paint a real bar on the ripping row from the same task-percent stream as the bottom bar; DisplayRole stays text for AT. **(4) Library auto-move** (`Config.library_dir`, empty = off): the album folder files into the library only after **every** post-rip worker settles — a 500ms settlement poll (`_post_rip_work_settled` over the six daemon-thread handles + the report debounce timer) arms a generation-guarded move on the shared post-rip daemon; new pure `library_move` module (never raises; "(N)" collision siblings; refuses self-nesting/workspace-root); buttons repoint to the new home; `find_prior_report` gained deduped `extra_roots` so the re-rip comparison scans the library too. **(5) Backlog audit closures:** "udev auto-detect" (already delivered by the CDROM_DRIVE_STATUS media watcher) and "ReplayGain" (cyanrip writes the full track+album RG/R128 tag set by default — verified upstream; `-K` never passed) closed as satisfied with honest notes. Suite **1830 green** (+~50 new tests), ruff + mypy clean at every commit.
  - **Lesson (durable):** *verify a CLI flag against the deployed version's actual getopt before plumbing it through* — project docs had confidently named a nonexistent flag for six weeks (inherited from the old backend's flag of the same purpose), and every layer of record repeated it. The five-minute upstream check was the difference between a feature and a 100%-reproducible rip abort. Same session, same lesson in reverse: two backlog rows turned out to be already-delivered — audit a feature request against what the stack already does before building it.

- **Docs-consolidation leftovers executed + accessibility gap #4 completed (2026-07-21, follow-on session to the audit).** Two maintainer-approved work items. **(1) The TASKS.md consolidation item is now almost fully ticked:** the already-shipped DEPENDENCIES review-cadence checkbox flipped (record-only); the **"⭐ START HERE" queue re-ranked** around what is actually open (trust hardening → soft-fork PRs → the consolidated hardware proof queue → docs backlog → UX remainder → parked CTDB repair), with the 2026-06-09 numbered list preserved as *ranked history*, numbering intact, because other text cites "current-plan item N"; the **UX gap backlog single-homed** (the doc's table canonical, item 15 reduced to a checklist); the four remaining **single-home cleanups** applied (log-format-comparison → §3.7 pointer; fixtures README → output_reference pointer + the UTF-16 warning; dependency-contracts got its explicit exclusions scope note; architecture §2 got the "Qt-free domain modules → PLANNING §2" row); the **manual CUETools/`ctdb-cli` repair workflow written** (`docs/manual-ctdb-repair.md` — strictly from the research record, unexecuted steps marked *(unverified)*, linked from both recommending docs + the index); and the **KDD-24 ripper-landscape corrections condensed** to their designated home (feasibility doc keeps a one-liner + link). *Still open by design:* the strategy-§9 citation locating the maintainer's ripper-landscape doc — needs the maintainer to say where it lives. *(Resolved later the same session: the maintainer didn't recall; a repo-wide search — corroborating the audit — confirmed the doc was session-provided research input never committed, and §9 now records that plus the archive-if-it-resurfaces convention, closing the consolidation item.)* **(2) UX gap #4's remaining half shipped** (see the CHANGELOG bullet + `docs/architecture.md` §3.8): a full manual keyboard-reachability sweep over every UI module (~10k lines read; findings: the copyable disc-ID labels and drive-diagnosis fix command were `ClickFocus`-only so Tab skipped them; the accuraterip.com link was mouse-only; composite-row Settings fields + three identical Browse buttons + the release table were anonymous; prominent buttons had no mnemonics) and **focus-safe live announcements** via the new `ui/accessibility.py` `announce()` (Qt announcement events, feature-detected, never raises) wired into every live status/verdict surface with per-phase throttling (`status_phase_key` — announce the clause before the "…", so a new track speaks but a percent tick never does). Gap #4 flipped ✅ in the doc with the honest remainder named: a **live screen-reader (Orca) session on the rig**, queued with the hardware-gated proofs. Also caught in this session: **mypy 2.3.0 broke two Qt typing seams on the clean tree** (`_Index` no longer inferred as a type alias; the conditionally re-assigned `_SeamBase` no longer accepted as a base class) — fixed with an explicit `TypeAlias` and the import-as conditional-base form, MRO/metaclass verified unchanged. Suite 1798 green (+32 new a11y regression tests incl. a mnemonic-uniqueness pin), ruff + mypy clean at every commit.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated to architecture §3.8):** (1) *Qt's "keyboard-selectable" label flag is dead without an explicit focus policy* — `TextSelectableByKeyboard` sets only `ClickFocus`, so a keyboard-only user can never reach the text it supposedly enables; every such label needs `StrongFocus` set by hand. (2) *Live announcements need a throttle policy designed per surface* — the rip status redraws many times a second, so the dedup key must be the phase clause, not the text; announcing outcomes-not-transients keeps a screen reader useful instead of drowned. (3) A conftest that hard-exits at session finish (the SIGABRT defusal) also swallows pytest's failure section — `--junit-xml` is the reliable way to read failures in this repo.

- **Docs-audit decisions executed — consolidation map + all open questions resolved (2026-07-21, same branch).** The maintainer approved the audit's section-B doc map and answered its §C questions; all executed in 9 further commits: **B** — `audit-2026-07-02.md` archived with a graduation row; the colon proof harness moved to `scripts/cyanrip/verify-meta-colon.c`; the soft-fork runbook's duplicate issue-body blockquotes replaced by links to the canonical kit paste files; the upstream-process facts consolidated into the roadmap's Process block; README's second EAC-parity telling folded into the top matrix; **the test-plan whipper-era cases rewritten** (Test 3 → wizard success-screens absorbing Test 4; Test 8 → the cyanrip parity record with `-Z` convergence as its open core; Test 10 + A8 retired as stable-ID stubs — numbers are IDs referenced from TASKS/Part C/`scripts/ctdb_verify.py`, so stubs beat renumbering) plus **new Tests 12–14** (ladder/auto-fix/speed-locked `-S`; CD-Extra CTDB TOC; EAC-companion log + goal presets); CLAUDE.md's companion list slimmed to one-line pointers with `docs/README.md` named canonical. **C** — Critical Rule #3 now names its approved force-stop exception (explicitly authorized locked-section edit); the precedence rule reads "the brief *as amended by the maintainer-approved KDDs* wins"; `build` pinned `>=1,<2` at all three install sites; a catch-up dependency review for v0.4.19–v0.4.24 logged (all pins healthy; **mypy is 2.3.0 upstream — the Dependabot `<3` bound is now load-bearing**); duplicate per-release CHANGELOG headings merged (322 bullets before and after). `trust-audit-2026-07-08.md` deliberately NOT archived yet — its own condition (release-signing item closed) isn't met. Decisions + execution recorded in the audit doc's C-bis addendum.
- **Full documentation audit — 239 findings, ~160 fixed, consolidation plan captured (2026-07-21, maintainer-requested "audit, correct, and consolidate ALL project documentation").** Ran a 29-reader multi-agent audit over every Markdown file (plus CLI/CI ground-truth extractors), cross-checked against `src/`, the workflows, and the live GitHub tag list; applied the easy tier in 16 atomic commits, all suite/ruff/mypy green. **The two systemic causes:** the whipper removal (2026-06-30) and the CTDB CRC validation (2026-07-07) had each outrun ~a dozen doc locations; and every *multi-homed* status had drifted while single-homed facts stayed correct — so fixes declared canonical homes (TASKS item 8 for CTDB status; `scripts/cyanrip/` for upstream paste text) instead of just re-syncing copies. **Notable recoveries:** the CHANGELOG had *lost the v0.3.10 release* (heading restored verbatim from the tag; the cyanrip_flac README's "≥ 0.3.10" claim turned out right); dead `v0.2.0`/`v0.0.1` compare links annotated; CLAUDE.md's ops section finally records the 2026-07-08 CI reality (7 jobs, weekly mutation, `.zsync` + provenance assets); whipper-era test-plan cases got ⚠️ SUPERSEDED banners; the research brief / session-start / archive docs / dated audits got preservation banners (bodies unedited); PLANNING's tree gained its ~25 missing files and whipper-era KDDs got dated cyanrip-era annotations in the KDD-13/18 style; `/cyanrip/` is now git-ignored (root-anchored — the first attempt matched `scripts/cyanrip/` and was caught by the failing `git add`); two stale adapter comments contradicting the `-S` abort contract were corrected to the 2026-07-01 hardware finding. **Record:** [docs/archive/audit-2026-07-21.md](archive/audit-2026-07-21.md) (patterns, before→after map, open questions — incl. the "brief as amended by KDDs" precedence-wording question and the `[skip changelog]` scope question); the unexecuted medium-tier restructures are a checkboxed TASKS.md Documentation-backlog item. **Lesson (graduating candidate):** duplication is the leading indicator of doc rot here — when a status must appear twice, one copy should be a marker + pointer, never a second prose record.
- **mypy UI ratchet, part 3 (final) — the MainWindow god-object + its five mixins joined the strict gate via a typing seam (2026-07-20).** The last `ignore_errors` exclusion is gone: `disallow_untyped_defs` now covers the **entire** package. The blocker was structural — a mixin method's `self` IS the concrete `MainWindow` at runtime but mypy types it as the bare mixin, so cross-mixin `self._x` / `self.sibling_method()` couldn't resolve (205 of the 317 errors were `attr-defined`). The fix is a new **type-only seam**, `ui/main_window_shared.py::MainWindowShared`, that declares the whole surface the window exposes to its mixins (injected deps, child widgets, per-session state, the 11 Qt signals, and the ~12 cross-mixin methods); every mixin inherits it, so mypy resolves the access from any mixin. It is **runtime-neutral**, proven three ways (full suite green, a runtime MRO/metaclass smoke check, and a gate-bites check): attribute lines are bare annotations (no runtime state), the cross-mixin method stubs live under `if TYPE_CHECKING:` (they don't exist at runtime — the real impls are the only ones called), and the base is `TYPE_CHECKING`-conditional — `QWidget` for the checker (so `self` is a Qt widget: resolves `self.close()` etc. and accepts `self` as a QWidget parent), plain `object` at runtime (so the mixins gain no Qt base; MRO + Shiboken metaclass unchanged). Clearing the 317 → 0 also fixed ~10 **real** residual type-gaps rather than silencing them: retyped the `object|None` worker/dialog/manager attrs to concrete classes (`_disc_info_worker: DiscInfoWorker | None`, `_install_dialog: QProgressDialog | None`, `_last_rip_log: RipLog | None`, …), `_build_gui_dependency_manager -> DependencyManager` (which cascaded away most of the deps errors), typed the unknown-post-processing `album`/`track_rows` params to `AlbumMetadata`/`Sequence[TrackSummary]`, and widened two duck-typed `list[object]` helpers in `drive_profiles.py` to `Sequence[object]` (they only iterate). Full suite **1770 green**, mypy clean (110 files), ruff clean. `[skip changelog]` — contributor/CI-facing, no user-visible change.
  - **Lesson (durable):** typing a Qt "god-object composed from mixins" is a solved problem — a **shared type-only base** the mixins inherit, with a `TYPE_CHECKING`-conditional `QWidget`/`object` base so mypy sees `self` as a widget while runtime stays untouched. Pick `QWidget`, not `QMainWindow`: the concrete window lists `QMainWindow` first in its own bases, so a seam deriving `QMainWindow` makes the MRO demand it both before and after the mixins (unsatisfiable); `QWidget` is never a *direct* base of the window, so no C3 conflict. And turning a strict gate on over untyped code is where the **real bugs surface** — budget time to fix the residual errors honestly (retype the `object` attrs to what they actually hold) instead of blanket-`# type: ignore`; several were genuine type-gaps.

- **mypy UI ratchet, part 2 — the standalone UI widget modules joined the strict gate (2026-07-10).** The 2026-07-09 ratchet left *all* of `platterpus.ui.*` behind `ignore_errors=true`, which meant the entire Qt layer got **zero** type-checking — a real bug (a wrong attribute, a `None` misuse) there would sail through. Measured the UI layer per-file under strict mode: the 317 errors are **concentrated in the six god-object modules** (`main_window` + its five concern mixins — the bulk being `attr-defined` from cross-mixin `self._x` access mypy can't resolve without a shared typing seam). The ~14 **standalone widget/dialog** modules were nearly clean — only 8 residual errors, all real and low-risk: (1) `track_table`'s five `QAbstractTableModel` overrides violated Liskov because they annotated the index/parent arg as `QModelIndex` while Qt's supertype types it `QModelIndex | QPersistentModelIndex` — widened via a `_Index` alias (pure signature match; Qt still passes a plain `QModelIndex` at runtime); (2) a `QWidget | None` from `.get()` rebound onto a loop var typed `QWidget` in `settings_dialog` — renamed the second binding; (3) a missing `Callable[[bool, bool], HostTeardown] | None` annotation on `uninstall_dialog`'s injected `build_teardown`. Then **narrowed the `ignore_errors` override from `platterpus.ui.*` to just the six `main_window*` modules**, so every other UI module is now checked at the same strict def-typing as the rest of the codebase. Verified the gate bites on a newly-included module and stays quiet on an excluded one; full suite (1769) green, mypy clean (109 files), ruff clean. `[skip changelog]` — contributor/CI-facing, no user-visible change.
  - **Lesson (durable):** before excluding a whole subtree from a gate, **measure per-file** — a blanket exclusion often hides that most of the subtree already complies and only a small core is the real blocker. Splitting the exclusion down to just the blocking modules recovers most of the coverage immediately, and shrinks the remaining "hard" work to a well-scoped last step (here: the six mixin modules need a shared-Protocol/typed-base seam for cross-mixin `self` access). Also: PySide6's model/view overrides type their index arg as `QModelIndex | QPersistentModelIndex`; annotating an override with the bare `QModelIndex` is an LSP violation under strict mypy — widen it.

- **mypy strict ratchet — `disallow_untyped_defs` enforced everywhere except `ui` (2026-07-09).** The v0.4.22 mypy baseline was non-strict (it only flagged *incompatible* types, not *missing* annotations). Gauged strict mode (`--disallow-untyped-defs --disallow-incomplete-defs`) per package and found **every non-UI package already passes with zero errors** — `parsers`/`adapters`/`workers`/`ctdb`/`deps` and the top-level core modules were all already fully annotated (the "type hints mandatory" convention held). So enabling it was a **zero-code-change config tighten**: added the two flags to `[tool.mypy]`, kept the `ui.*` `ignore_errors` exclusion. Verified the gate actually bites (a throwaway untyped def errors `no-untyped-def`). Now any untyped/partially-typed function outside `ui` fails CI. Also confirmed + corrected a stale trust-audit note: the **config-file input surface is already validated at load** (`config.load()` → `_sanitized()` runs `validate_config` over the loaded TOML and resets error-level fields), so that "still open" item was actually closed; TASKS updated. Full suite green, mypy clean (109 files). `[skip changelog]` — a contributor/CI change, not user-facing.
  - **Lesson (durable):** when a convention has been held consistently (here "type hints mandatory"), *enforcing* it via a stricter gate is often free — measure first (per-package strict run), and if the code already complies you get a permanent regression guard for a one-line config change and no risk. The only real cost is the layer that never complied (the Qt `ui` mixins), which stays excluded behind a documented ratchet.

- **Re-rip comparison + read-effort/reconciliation trust improvements (2026-07-09).** Born from a trust audit of a v0.4.23 re-rip of The Police — *Every Breath You Take: The Classics*: 12/14 tracks were byte-identical to the prior rip but track 3 had silently regressed from an exact AccurateRip match to an offset-variant read, and that was **invisible to the tool** — it only surfaced by diffing two `.platterpus.json` reports by hand (Platterpus is stateless per rip). Shipped five improvements, all built on one new pure, never-raises module (`rip_compare.py`):
  1. **Re-rip comparison (`rip_compare.compare_reports`)** — track-by-track diff of two reports of the same disc: identical / differ, and per-track *better master* (exact AR match > offset-variant > not-in-DB; confidence tiebreak; genuine ties flagged `unknown`). Surfaced three ways: a `--compare` CLI, a results-pane banner after a rip (discovery via `find_prior_report`, run **off the GUI thread** through `_launch_post_rip_daemon` since it scans the library), and it feeds the best-of assembler.
  2. **Best-of-both assembler (`--assemble-best-of`)** — copies the better per-track file of two rips into a new folder; strictly **non-destructive** (never touches the sources), refuses across different discs.
  3. **Per-track read-effort warning** (`heavy_reread` report issue + results footnote) — flags tracks that needed heavy re-reading / a `-Z` that never converged even if AR matched.
  4. **AR↔CTDB reconciliation** (`verdict.reconcile_ar_ctdb`) — one line explaining that a CTDB "no match" beside an AR "mostly accurate" is the *same* finding (whole-disc CRC can't match when a couple of tracks differ), not two.
  5. **Offset-variant explanations** — a tooltip on the affected AR cells + a User-Guide glossary, with the re-rip caveat.
  - **Enablers:** the cyanrip parser now captures the TOC-derived **MusicBrainz Disc ID + CDDB ID** (the truest "same physical disc" key, stable across re-rips and independent of MB release edits); report **schema → v9** surfaces them in the `rip` block and serializes the long-parsed-but-dropped `secure_rerip_converged` per track. Refactored the AR tally into one shared `verdict.accuraterip_counts` so the banner, JSON, and reconciliation can't diverge. ~1746 tests pass (+~60 new), ruff + mypy clean.
  - **Bug caught in self-review (fixed + regression-tested):** `find_prior_report`'s recency sort compared an ISO string against a `"mtime:…"` string, so ordering was by first character (`"m" > "2"`) — a report lacking `generated_at` always sorted as "newest". Fixed to a uniform epoch-float key.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) **A stateless per-rip tool can't catch a regression across rips — but the durable per-rip artifact (`.platterpus.json`) can, if something diffs them.** The report was already the "one file explains a rip" record; making it *comparable across rips* turned a by-hand catch into an automatic one. (2) **Read-effort (`rip_count`/`-Z` convergence) is a complement to, not a substitute for, the cross-rip diff:** a disc that quietly reads one wrong-but-consistent answer per pass (no re-reads, `-Z` off) trips *neither* the ripper's error count *nor* the read-effort flag — only a second rip reveals it (or per-track paranoia counts, which cyanrip emits disc-wide only today → a tracked upstream JSON-output ask). Kept honest in the docs so a clean read-effort result is never read as "provably reproducible". (3) **TOC-derived disc IDs beat the MB release id for "same disc":** a release can be merged/split/edited in MusicBrainz, but the Disc ID is a hash of the physical TOC.

- **cyanrip upstream-contribution sessions — soft fork decided, two PRs prepared, execution kit built (2026-07-08/09; catch-up entry written 2026-07-21 from the merged PRs #75–#78, #80 — these sessions predate this entry).** Decided the **soft fork** model (`rmccann-hub/cyanrip` = upstream `master` + a small rebased patch set, each patch PR'd back upstream and dropped once merged) after the §6 research finding that cyanrip's *releases* are stalled but `master` is live — since we build in the `ripping` container from any commit, release cadence never gates us (PR #76). Wrote the runbook [`cyanrip-fork.md`](cyanrip-fork.md) with two prepared contributions: the **`-a`/`-t` colon-parsing fix** (the bug behind our U+2236 workaround) and **full libavcodec encoder args** (PR #77); proved the colon fix with a standalone **ASan/UBSan-clean C harness** (`cyanrip-soft-fork-verify-meta-colon.c`, PR #78); and shipped the **execution kit** `scripts/cyanrip/` — a verified dry-run-first patcher (`apply-colon-fix.py`, unit-tested), fork/build scripts, and paste-ready issue/PR bodies (PR #80). TASKS pruned of stale whipper-era items the same day (PR #75). Execution is env-gated: this repo's cloud session can't fork/build cyanrip, so the kit is built to run locally or in a cyanrip-seeded session.
- **v0.4.21 — in-app User Guide version + whole-repo file-versions report (2026-07-08; catch-up entry written 2026-07-21 from the release PR #54).** Closed a doc-currency gap the Markdown stamp pass missed: the in-app User Guide (`help_content.py`) carried **no version at all** — it now footer-stamps `Platterpus vX.Y.Z (build …)` at render time from `__version__`, so it can't go stale (same approach as the About dialog). Added `scripts/file_versions.py`, a git-derived "last updated (version)" report over every tracked file (`--markdown` / `--path`) — the whole-repo currency view without embedding stamps in source files (git is the source of truth there; docs keep visible footers because they're read where git history isn't at hand).
- **Reproducible build — `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` shipped; dependency hash-pinning documented as blocked (2026-07-08, maintainer-chosen "do it, validate next release").** `build_appimage.sh` now pins every timestamp the build embeds (the wheel's zip entries + the AppImage squashfs) to the HEAD commit time (`git log -1 --format=%ct`; overridable, git-less fallback to the 1980 zip epoch floor), exported before every build step. **Verified locally that the wheel is byte-identical across two rebuilds with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` set** (same sha256) — concrete proof the timestamp half works; the full AppImage's squashfs reproducibility can only be confirmed on a real release build. **The `pip --require-hashes` half is genuinely blocked** and now documented rather than forced: python-appimage installs each `requirements.txt` line in its own shell `pip install`, so require-hashes mode can't resolve a line's transitive-dep hashes (PySide6 → shiboken6), and the `platterpus` line is a locally-built, per-commit-changing unhashed wheel. Dependabot also doesn't watch the recipe dir (only `/`), so exact-pinning there would rot. Shipping a fragile `--require-hashes` into an untestable release path risked breaking every release, so I shipped the safe, validated half and wrote the blocker + the needed approach (a python-appimage-compatible pre-resolved/hashed install step) into `requirements.txt` + TASKS.
  - **Lesson (durable):** "reproducible + hash-pinned" is two separable guarantees — *deterministic timestamps* (cheap, safe, `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, verifiable on the wheel alone) and *exact dependency bytes* (needs a lockfile the packager can honor). When the packaging tool's install model can't express the second, ship the first and document the blocker with the specific reason — don't force a fragile pin into a release path you can't test. Validate what you *can* in-sandbox (the wheel) even when the full artifact needs hardware/CI.

- **Known-disc re-rip overwrite confirm dialog (2026-07-08, maintainer-chosen).** Closed the last data-safety gap from the trust audit: re-ripping an *already-identified* album to a folder that already held audio used to overwrite it silently (v0.4.22 fixed only the *unknown*-disc collision, via auto-suffix). The maintainer chose a **confirm dialog** over always-auto-suffix (a known re-rip is often deliberate). Implemented as pure, tested helpers in `main_window_helpers.py` — `known_album_folder` reproduces cyanrip's folder derivation by rendering the disc template through `naming.render_preview` (so the `:`→`∶` sanitisation matches) and taking the file's parent; `suffix_album_folder_template` appends `(N)` to the *album-folder* segment only (the second-to-last `/`-segment), so the tag tokens stay intact and only the on-disk folder gets a `(2)` — the FLAC album tag is unchanged; `free_album_folder_templates` finds the smallest free sibling — plus a `_confirm_known_overwrite` QMessageBox (**Replace / Rip to a new folder / Cancel**, Cancel as the safe default) wired into `_on_rip_requested`'s known-disc branch. The folder probe + dialog are cheap/interaction-only (no blocking I/O on the GUI thread). +7 tests (folder derivation, suffix, smallest-free, and each dialog branch via a button-picker monkeypatch); 1682 pass, ruff+mypy clean.
  - **Lesson (durable):** a known disc's folder comes from cyanrip rendering the template from *tags*, so "rip to a new folder" must suffix the **folder segment of the template**, not the album tag (suffixing the tag would corrupt the FLAC's album name). And the pre-rip existence check is fail-safe by design — it mirrors the naming *preview*, so on an exotic title cyanrip sanitises differently it can only *miss* a collision, never invent one (never a false block).

- **Trust-claim honesty sweep — 11 copy defects fixed (2026-07-08).** With Ultracode on and trust as the north star, ran the trust-claim-render audit as a **two-phase workflow**: (1) adversarially re-verify an initial 6-finding single-agent audit against the current code + `verdict.py`, and (2) four diverse-lens finders hunting for *missed* overclaims. All 6 originals CONFIRMED — one upgraded to **trust-critical** (the User Guide's grey verdict claimed the Copy CRC "proves the disc was read securely", an overclaim `verdict.py` explicitly contradicts: a lone Copy CRC proves only lossless encoding, not a correct read/offset — a wrong-offset rip has a self-consistent Copy CRC). The find-more phase caught **5 more** the single agent missed, including a stale CTDB parenthetical it had *wrongly cleared*, two bit-perfection overclaims, an `_eac_cell` tooltip that mislabels a present-but-no-match track as "isn't in the AccurateRip database", and stale `rip_progress` comments/docstring. **Verdict: the trust *engine* is sound** (verdict.py, `track_accuraterip_verified` conf≥1, AR cells, disc panel, JSON report, EAC-log renderer all gate correctly and the EAC renderer never signs/forges); every defect was **static copy that drifted from the code** — the original overclaim plus the "experimental" CTDB caveats the KDD-16 hardware-validation flip (v0.4.20) left behind in Settings, the User Guide, the doctor, and rip-progress. Fixed all 11 across 6 files with **regression tests** (`tests/test_trust_copy_honesty.py` + strengthened `_eac_cell`/`fidelity_summary` tests); 1675 pass, ruff+mypy clean.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *When you flip a long-standing safety flag (CRC_VALIDATED), the stale caveat text it justified is scattered across labels, tooltips, help prose, hints, comments, and docstrings — grep every surface, not just the logic.* (2) A single auditor both misses findings AND mis-judges some as fine (it cleared the lines-70-72 CTDB passage the find-more lenses correctly flagged) — a verify+find-more fan-out with distinct lenses beats one thorough pass for an honesty sweep. (3) The trust engine being correct is necessary but not sufficient: the *words around it* are themselves trust claims and drift independently of the code — they need the same regression-test discipline. Assert single-line tokens (prose in a triple-quoted guide re-wraps).

- **Static type-checking (`mypy`) added to CI — non-strict baseline (2026-07-08, maintainer-approved).** The project mandated type hints but nothing verified them. Added `mypy` to the `dev` extra + a gating `typecheck` CI job (SHA-pinned; installs the PySide6 wheel for its stubs but NOT the Qt system libs — mypy reads `.pyi`, never imports Qt). A bare `mypy` run showed **326 errors, 309 of them in `ui/`** — the deliberate MainWindow mixin god-object pattern (attributes declared in one mixin, assigned `| None` in the concrete class; `self` inside a mixin isn't a `QWidget`), which mypy can't follow without a shared Protocol/typed base. So the baseline **excludes `platterpus.ui.*`** via a `[tool.mypy]` override (documented ratchet) and checks everything else. Fixed the **17 non-UI errors properly** (not blanket-ignored): loose `object` types at loosely-coupled/injection seams given real types via `TYPE_CHECKING` imports (`metaflac: MetaflacAdapter`, `manager: DependencyManager`, preflight `host: HostSetup`, `app_icon() -> QIcon | None`), `Callable[..., Any]` for the urlopen-style injection openers, an `isinstance` guard in the config v3→v4 migration, `text=True` on the DEVNULL drive-control runner to make its `CompletedProcess[str]` return honest, and a `cast` for `QApplication.instance()`'s base-class typing on `setWindowIcon`. Full suite green (1670 passed), ruff clean, mypy clean (107 files). DEPENDENCIES.md/CHANGELOG/TASKS updated.
  - **Lesson (durable):** introduce a type-checker as a *scoped, non-strict baseline*, not a big-bang — measure where the errors cluster first (here 95% were one architectural pattern), exclude that layer with a documented ratchet, and fix the genuine remainder with real annotations (`TYPE_CHECKING` imports keep a duck-typed/loosely-coupled seam duck-typed at runtime while giving the checker the shape). A green gate that covers the clean 90% today beats a red gate that demands a UI-typing sprint first.

- **Naming-scheme cross-filesystem safety — audited directly, documented as a known limitation (2026-07-08).** Picked up the highest-value *un-run* audit category (the 2-core sandbox had blocked the fan-out) and did it directly by reading `naming.py`, `settings_validation.py`, and cyanrip's naming contract. **Verdict: no shippable bug on the Linux target.** cyanrip owns filename generation (Critical Rule #3) and maps the only path-illegal chars for ext4/btrfs (`:`→`∶`, a value `/`→`∕`); the Settings/config boundary (`settings_validation.validate_config`) rejects every control char incl. NUL, blocks `..` traversal and absolute templates, and — enforced by a completeness meta-test — has a rule for *every* `Config` field; any genuinely-unwritable name (e.g. a component over ext4's 255-byte limit) fails the rip **loudly**, never silently. The residual hazards (Windows/NTFS/exFAT reserved chars `< > " \ | ? *`, reserved device names CON/PRN/NUL/…, trailing dots/spaces, case-insensitive collisions) are all *legal on Linux* and bite only on a non-native output volume or when the library is copied to Windows/macOS — a **documented cross-filesystem limitation**, now written up in `docs/dependency-contracts.md`. **Deliberately did NOT** add a filename re-sanitiser: it would duplicate cyanrip's logic (Rule #3) and break the Settings naming preview↔reality round-trip. The only open item is an *optional* non-blocking Settings warning on a cross-FS-unsafe template — a maintainer **feature** call (warning Linux users about Windows may just be noise), not a fix, so it stays tracked, not imposed. Trust-audit doc + TASKS updated (category → done); no code change.
  - **Lesson (durable):** an "audit category" doesn't always yield a code change — the honest outcome here was *proving the design is sound for the target* and turning a vague "cross-FS?" worry into a precise, documented limitation. When the fix would override a sacred seam (the ripper owns naming) and the risk is only in a non-primary scenario, document the boundary and surface the optional feature; don't manufacture a change to look busy.

- **Supply-chain hardening rounds 2 & 3 — cleared the v0.4.22 audit's deferred CI/release items (2026-07-08).** Continuing the trust-audit follow-up under the maintainer's standing "make your own decisions, always more" mandate, took the *safe, self-validating* deferred items (no new runtime dep, no maintainer-held asset) to done. **Round 2 (PR #60, merged):** SHA-pinned every `uses:` across `ci.yml`/`release.yml`/`publish-pypi.yml`/`appimage.yml` + the new `mutation.yml` to full commit SHAs (`# vN` comment) so a re-pointed tag can't inject code into the pipeline that builds the auto-updated binary; added a **gating `pip-audit`** CI job (dependency-vuln scan, currently clean), an **advisory `tests-touched`** warn-check (src changed without a test), and a **weekly non-blocking `mutation.yml`** (`mutmut` over parsers/verdict/CTDB-CRC) for test-efficacy signal. Dependabot (`github-actions`) keeps the pins current. **Round 3 (this branch):** added **build-provenance attestation** for the released AppImage — `release.yml` now runs `actions/attest-build-provenance` (SLSA, GitHub OIDC + Sigstore; **no maintainer key, no new dep**), so a downloader can prove the binary came from this repo's pipeline with `gh attestation verify … --repo rmccann-hub/Platterpus`. Placed *after* the release publishes so a transient attestation hiccup can never block shipping (the never-block-the-release principle); it binds to the artifact digest, so ordering is safe. PyPI wheels are already attested via Trusted Publishing.
  - **Still deferred, with justification:** real **signing** (minisign/ed25519) + making the updater *check* the attestation / fail-closed on a bad signature — needs a **new crypto dep (CLAUDE.md must-ask)** and a **maintainer-held key**, so it is not a unilateral call; reproducible/hash-pinned AppImage build and static type-checking (mypy ratchet) touch surfaces best validated on a real build/large pass; known-disc re-rip overwrite confirm (GUI/HW-gated); the audit's un-run research categories (naming cross-FS safety, input-validation coverage, trust-claim-render sweep) want a higher-core environment for the consensus fan-out.
  - **Doc currency (rule #7):** `SECURITY.md`, `TASKS.md`, and `docs/trust-audit-2026-07-08.md` (now `docs/archive/trust-audit-2026-07-08.md`) now mark SHA-pinning / pip-audit / mutmut **done** and the AppImage provenance **attested** (updater-check + signing still open), and honestly state that integrity (SHA-256) — not yet authenticity — is what the auto-updater enforces today.
  - **Lesson (durable):** *authenticity ≠ integrity* — a checksum proves a download is intact, not that **we** built it; build-provenance attestation is the honest, key-free interim that closes the "who built this" half without a signing key, and the docs must not blur the two. A release-only workflow can't be exercised in CI, so land it with YAML + pinned-SHA discipline and an ordering that can't block a real release.

- **Trust & quality deep audit → v0.4.22 hardening (2026-07-08).** Maintainer asked to "find what we don't know we don't know," especially trust-breaking gaps — via a fan-out research workflow across 7 categories (preventative, best-practices, input-validation, naming, + chosen: trust-integrity, security/supply-chain, reliability) with a 5-reviewer unanimous-by-justification consensus gate. **The 2-core sandbox caps agent concurrency at 2**, so the 14-agent web-research workflow serialized past a practical wall-clock; stopped it after the security/reliability/preventative researchers produced **12 concrete, code-grounded findings**, and did the consensus/justification step directly against the code. Full write-up: [docs/trust-audit-2026-07-08.md](archive/trust-audit-2026-07-08.md).
  - **Shipped (v0.4.22):** (1) **unknown-disc overwrite guard** — two unrecognized discs both default to `Unknown Artist/Unknown Album/` and the second silently clobbered the first's master; `unique_album_title()` now lands a collision in a fresh `(N)` sibling (pure + tested). (2) **Durable atomic writes** — config/drive-profile/rip-report claimed power-loss safety but never `fsync`'d; new `atomic_write.py` (temp→fsync→replace→dir-fsync) makes the claim true. (3) **Config downgrade no longer drops newer keys** — `save()` re-merges unknown keys + keeps a higher `schema_version`. (4) **CI supply-chain**: least-privilege `permissions: contents: read`, a server-side `media-guard` job enforcing Critical Rule #8, `dependabot.yml`, `SECURITY.md`.
  - **Deferred (tracked in TASKS.md + the audit doc), with justification:** updater *authenticity* (signing needs a new crypto dep = must-ask + a maintainer key; interim = build-provenance attestation + fail-closed hook), action SHA-pinning (release workflows unverifiable in-sandbox; Dependabot drives it meanwhile), reproducible/hashed AppImage build, static type-checking (mypy ratchet), pip-audit + scheduled mutmut, known-disc re-rip confirm dialog.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) *A "power-loss-safe" atomic write needs `fsync` of both the file and its parent dir — temp+rename alone only survives a process crash.* → graduate to a code-convention (via `atomic_write.py` as the single seam). (2) *Never let two discs resolve to one output path — an unknown-disc default is a silent-data-loss trap; disambiguate before writing.* (3) Environment shapes method: a fan-out consensus workflow is only practical where cores allow real concurrency — on a 2-core box, verify directly and reserve the fan-out for a bigger environment.

- **CTDB CRC hardware-validated — `CRC_VALIDATED` flipped to True (KDD-16, 2026-07-07).** Immediately after the trust audit (below), the maintainer ran `platterpus --ctdb-calibrate` from the shipped AppImage on the same disc. Result: `✅ MATCH — offset=+0 → CRC 5da89fcd (aligned)`, whole-disc PCM 631,998,864 B = **157,999,716 frames**, trim **front 5880 / back 9996**. Our offset-0 CTDB CRC reproduced a database-stored value bit-exactly on real hardware — the KDD-16 gate the code had waited on since v0.4.16. (It matched a confidence-1 pressing, not the dominant confidence-1350 one; that only means this physical disc is a less-common pressing — irrelevant to *algorithm* correctness, which is all the gate tests.)
  - **The flip + fixture.** `ctdb/crc.py`: `CRC_VALIDATED = True`, plus a documented `CONFIRMED_VECTOR = (157_999_716, 5880, 9996, 0, 0x5DA89FCD)`. Regression test `test_kdd16_confirmed_vector_trim` pins the trim math for that disc (the 632 MB of PCM can't ship — Critical rule #8 + size — so the executable pin is the length-dependent trim; re-confirm any time by re-running the calibrate command).
  - **Not a one-liner — blast radius handled.** `verify.py` reads the gate live and now stamps `crc_validated` onto every result (so field/message/`trustworthy` stay consistent under test monkeypatching). Four tests keyed to the old `False` default were made robust: the two "experimental / don't-blame-the-rip" honesty paths now `monkeypatch` `CRC_VALIDATED=False` (kept covered for a future re-opened gate — matters for the 91% coverage floor), and three UI/verdict tests pin `crc_validated=False` explicitly; added validated-state counterparts. Full suite **1662 passed, 0 failed, coverage 93%** (crc.py 100%, verify.py 98%); ruff clean.
  - **Doc currency (rule #7).** Updated `ctdb-crc-algorithm.md` (gate → PASSED, with the pasted calibrate output), `PLANNING.md` (7 KDD-16 status mentions), `test-plan.md` (Test 1/1b marked done), the two EAC investigation docs, and the `ctdb_worker.py`/`config.py` comments — every "CRC_VALIDATED=False / experimental / hardware-gated" claim now reflects the validated state.
  - **Lesson (durable):** flipping a long-standing safety flag is rarely one line — the *dormant* honesty branches it guarded still need explicit coverage (monkeypatch the flag) or the coverage gate falls and the fail-safe path silently rots. Test both sides of a gate, not just the current default.

- **v0.4.19 re-rip trust audit — logs verified truthful, offset chain sound (2026-07-07).** Maintainer re-ripped *The Police — Every Breath You Take: The Classics* (14 tracks, Pioneer BDR-209D) on the shipped v0.4.19 build (f50258c), offset **+667**, and asked for a full trust audit: verify correctness *and* that the GUI's reporting is truthful against independent sources — explicitly "don't trust the GUI's own summary." Audited the cyanrip `.log`, the `.platterpus.json`, the EAC-compatible companion log, the `.cue`, and the app `log.txt` (the cyanrip argv source). **Verdict: everything correct and honestly reported — no bug found, no v0.4.20 cut.**
  - **Offset — six independent sources agree at +667, zero mismatch.** cyanrip log `Offset: +667 samples`; app-log argv `-s 667`; `.platterpus.json` `settings.read_offset={configured,applied,effective:667}` + `rip.read_offset_correction:667`; EAC-log header `Read offset correction : 667`; app-log `known AccurateRip offset for PIONEER BD-RW BDR-209D: +667`; and `adapters/accuraterip_offsets.py` curated `PIONEER BD-RW BDR-209D → 667`. The old "+0 fabricated as measured/high-confidence" path is confirmed gone in code (`find_offset` → `NotImplementedError`; `drive_profiles.py` labels the value "from the AccurateRip list" at MEDIUM, HIGH only via two-source agreement).
  - **Flags.** argv = `cyanrip -d /dev/sr0 -s 667 -o flac -r 5 -N -a … -t … -D … -F …`. `-s 667` (offset), `-N` (MB disabled, Critical rule #5 — GUI feeds tags via `-a`/`-t`), `-o flac`, `-r 5` (max_retries). `-Z` correctly **absent**: dynamic secure-rerip disengaged because the disc is in AccurateRip (`read_speed.secure_rerip.engaged=false`, `disc_in_accuraterip=true`) — exactly what `_build_rip_argv` does (emits `-Z` only when `secure_rerip_matches>0`).
  - **True reporting — line-for-line clean (programmatic diff).** A script parsed the cyanrip raw log and compared every value against the JSON + EAC log: all 14 tracks' EAC CRC32 (= JSON `copy_crc` = EAC-log Copy CRC), AR v1/v2 CRC + result string + confidence, the track-5 offset-variant (`Accurip 450` → `offset_450`), the `accuraterip_verified` flags, the 13/14 summary, paranoia counts (22055/1598/16/465), `Ripping errors: 0`, and the log `FUN512` checksum — **all match exactly.** No invented or rounded number. `test_crc` is honestly `null` (cyanrip has no separate EAC-style Test pass; its single EAC CRC32 is the Copy CRC) — not fabricated to equal Copy.
  - **Bit-perfection.** 13/14 tracks provably bit-perfect (AR v1 **and** v2 at the standard offset; v2 confidence 200). Track 5 matched an **offset-variant pressing** (`Accurip 450`, confidence 200) — audio consistent with a known-good rip but not an *exact* canonical match; a known-consistent quirk of this pressing (also seen v0.4.14/0.4.15). Platterpus reports this honestly and conservatively — verdict `warn` "13 of 14 … the other 1 matched an offset-variant pressing (partially accurate)", plus an `issues` entry `not_bit_perfect` — it does **not** overclaim. All 14 FLACs decode cleanly.
  - **CTDB.** No `--ctdb-calibrate` was run; the automatic post-rip **verify** computed the offset-0 whole-disc CRC `5DA89FCD`, found it in the 102-entry CTDB set for this disc → `verdict:match` at offset 0, honestly flagged `trustworthy:false / crc_validated:false / "experimental (KDD-16)"`. Strong supporting evidence the algorithm is correct (a wrong algorithm matches nothing — cf. the v0.4.17 no_match), but the KDD-16 gate specifically wants a `--ctdb-calibrate` sweep confirming 0 is the landing offset. **Left `CRC_VALIDATED=False`** and recommended the maintainer run `platterpus --ctdb-calibrate "<album folder>"`; on a clean offset-0 sweep match, flip the flag + bake the vector as the KDD-16 regression fixture.
  - **Lesson (durable):** a trust audit of self-reported results must diff the GUI's output against the *independent* upstream artifact mechanically, value-by-value — that is what "don't trust the summary" operationally means. Here it confirmed fidelity; the same harness would flag a single altered digit.

- **v0.4.19 — the read-offset trust chain, honestly (2026-07-07).** Triggered by the maintainer, mid-rip on real hardware, asking *"should my offset be 0?"* — the Set-up-drive wizard had auto-"detected" **+0** and labelled it **"measured on this drive (high confidence)"** for a Pioneer BDR-209D whose true offset is **+667**. The app had told them a wrong value was correct — a trust failure ("there were some major things missing here that you told me were correct"). Mandate: fix the entire mess, then merge + release. Handled with an **adversarial audit workflow** (10 subsystem finders → per-finding skeptic verify → synthesis; 48 agents, 34 confirmed findings → 7 fixes), all fixes implemented + a second **adversarial review workflow** (4 agents) before merge.
  - **cyanrip has no offset finder (root cause).** `cyanrip_backend.find_offset` ran `cyanrip -f` (which is *force-overread*, not a detector) and regex-scraped "offset…N" from its help/default echo → returned **0**, stored as `OFFSET_FIND`/HIGH, which then **outranked and clobbered** the correct AccurateRip-list +667. Removed the override entirely (inherits base `NotImplementedError`); the test that "proved" it worked fed a fabricated `"Detected drive offset: 667"` string cyanrip never emits. Added `RipBackend.supports_offset_detection()` (default False) so the wizard **hides the Detect button** for a backend that can't measure — honest UI, not a button that only fails.
  - **Agreement-based confidence (maintainer's model).** Replaced `should_replace_offset` with `reconcile_offset`: HIGH is earned only when **two independent sources agree** (→ new `OffsetSource.CONFIRMED`); a lone reading is MEDIUM at most; a disagreeing automatic reading never clobbers a MANUAL/CONFIRMED/incumbent value. `evaluate_drive_state` gained an AccurateRip-list disagreement warning (the silent-wrong-offset detector it always should have had).
  - **whipper.conf no longer satisfies the offset gate.** `is_offset_configured` now returns `bool(override_read_offset)` only — a leftover whipper.conf value never reaches cyanrip's `-s`, so treating it as "configured" made an upgrader's rip silently run at offset 0.
  - **Rip-preflight self-heal.** A saved offset that disagrees with the AccurateRip-list value is surfaced with an offer to use the list value (so the maintainer's already-saved bogus 0 self-corrects on next Start), recording the accepted value so the trust line updates — but it **respects a deliberate MANUAL/CONFIRMED per-unit offset** (never clobbers a measured value). *(The review workflow caught both of these as defects in my first cut: a stale false "+0" warning after healing, and clobbering a MANUAL value with default-Yes — both fixed + regression-tested.)*
  - **Verdict honesty.** A zero-AccurateRip-match verdict no longer claims matches are "expected for a CD-R" or that the Copy CRCs "prove a secure read" (false reassurance on what may be a wrong-offset rip); it now says the audio is *not independently verified* and names the wrong-offset possibility.
  - **CI flake root-caused + fixed (NOT what it looked like).** The offscreen-Qt SIGSEGV/SIGABRT (~40–55% on two files) was a **wrong-thread QObject destruction**: `PendingInstallsDialog._on_install_finished` (GUI thread) dropped the last Python ref to the install worker while its `QThread` was still alive. Fixed by destroying the worker on its own thread via the queued `deleteLater` and clearing refs only on `QThread.finished`; abort rate 40–55% → 0/25. Removed the 6-attempt retry band-aid (restored single-pass CI + a `timeout-minutes` backstop). The *other* "residual hang" turned out to be **not a Qt flake at all** but the CTDB calibrate being pathologically slow under coverage.
  - **CTDB calibrate ~150× faster (40 s → 0.3 s).** The ±5879 sweep rebuilt the GF(2) `crc32_combine` operator for every one of ~11,759 offsets *and* every prefix length. The window length is constant and prefix lengths are arithmetic, so build each operator once and apply a cheap per-offset multiply / incremental shift — bit-identical (verified 0 mismatches across all offsets; equivalence test strengthened to the full band + edges). Fixed both the coverage-time CI stall and the real `--ctdb-calibrate` wait.
  - **Lessons (durable, graduated):** (1) *A worker moved to a `QThread` must be destroyed on that thread (queued `deleteLater`), never by dropping its last Python ref from the GUI thread — clear the owning refs on `QThread.finished`, not the worker's `finished`* (→ `testing.md`). (2) Never advertise a capability the backend lacks — capability-gate the UI. (3) Confidence is earned by agreement between independent sources, never granted to a lone reading; a self-heal must obey the same authority model as the ledger (MANUAL/CONFIRMED win). (4) Hoist invariant operator construction out of a swept loop. (5) *"Claimed correct but actually wrong" is a first-class bug class* — when trust is lost, audit adversarially and verify each finding against the real source, don't patch the symptom.

- **v0.4.18 — software-version provenance + EAC results column (2026-07-07).** Driven by the maintainer noticing, on a real 0.4.16 rip, that version info was "missing in multiple places, for multiple softwares." A version-provenance audit (7-surface workflow) found the JSON report + About dialog already complete, but three real gaps in Platterpus-authored surfaces; all fixed from one seam (`platterpus.__version__` + `build_info.build_fingerprint()` + a new DRY `build_info.encoder_versions()` that reads the launch-time dependency probe — never a fresh probe, which would enter the container and freeze the GUI):
  - **EAC-compatible companion log** now records *which softwares produced the rip* — `Generated by Platterpus <version> (build <fp>)`, `FLAC encoder: flac <v>, metaflac <v>`, and `Derived-format encoder: ffmpeg <v>` (only when a transcode ran) — appended between the genuine cyanrip source line and the not-an-EAC-log disclaimer. Absent values are omitted, never invented; the no-EAC-checksum / NOT-signed honesty guardrails are untouched.
  - **Window title** now shows `Platterpus <version>` (was just "Platterpus") — the running version at a glance, not only in Help → About.
  - **`--doctor` / preflight header** now stamps the Platterpus version + build.
  - **EAC results column.** The per-track AccurateRip table gained an **EAC** column: the track's EAC-format CRC32 (the value to diff against a real EAC rip) + a ✓ when it meets the archival bar we can verify (AccurateRip-verified + Copy OK; the rip is offset-corrected, error-free). Offset-variant-only matches show `~`; not-in-AccurateRip shows the value with no mark — never a false ✓. Tooltip is explicit it is NOT a claim of EAC-checksum equivalence (maintainer approved "both value + verdict"). One honesty fork was surfaced via AskUserQuestion rather than guessed: a literal pass/fail "EAC compliance" verdict is impossible without a reference EAC rip, so the ✓ means "meets the criteria we can actually check."
  - **Left alone (honesty):** cyanrip's own `.log` and `.cue` — we parse them but don't author them, so stamping a Platterpus/encoder line there would forge bytes in another tool's file. The JSON report and About dialog were already complete and untouched.
  - **CTDB offset-range fix (KDD-16) — the *real* calibration bug.** A source-grounded agent (cloned `gchudov/cuetools.net`, read the C#, proved the CRC equivalence in Python over all offsets) found v0.4.17's CRC/trim/combine were **already bit-for-bit correct**; the one wrong constant was the **offset search range**: we swept AccurateRip's `±2939` (`5·588−1`) but CTDB's `CDRepair.FindOffset` sweeps `±(stride/2 − 1) = ±5879`. The maintainer's disc pressing sat outside ±2939, so two hardware calibrations returned no-match. Fixed `CTDB_OFFSET_RANGE = CTDB_STRIDE_WORDS//2 − 1 = 5879` (the fixed stride/2 front trim exists precisely to give this room), pinned it in a test, refreshed the ±2939 doc mentions. Still `CRC_VALIDATED=False` until the maintainer's next `--ctdb-calibrate` confirms offset-0. **Lesson:** two off-by-a-constant bugs in a row (trim, then range) — memory-based reconstruction of an external algorithm is unreliable; read the actual source and prove equivalence before claiming a fix.
  - **cyanrip upstream survey (cloned the repo + fetched PR #115).** cyanrip has **zero** CTDB code (AccurateRip-only) → CTDB is 100% Platterpus-side, no PR. Master **already** does TOC-based pregap/`INDEX 00` handling (+ merged cdrdao-TOC PR #127); the accurate subchannel pregap/**HTOA** detection is **PR #115** (UltraFuzzy, open, surgical: `pregap.c` + a one-line call-site swap; blockers = a leftover debug-assert block + a macOS private-struct hack). So the honest move is **help land #115, not open a competing PR and not build a parallel Platterpus cdrdao path** (recorded in `cyanrip-upstream.md`). Test&Copy is covered by `-Z`; cache-defeat/C2 live below cyanrip in libcdio-paranoia. **License note:** cyanrip is LGPL-2.1+, so any code contributed upstream must be LGPL-2.1+, never GPL-3-only Platterpus code.
  - **License/provenance audit (maintainer asked "did we take anything we shouldn't have?").** Clean: Platterpus GPL-3.0-only (PEP-639 SPDX); runtime deps PySide6 (LGPL-3, used under the LGPL option), musicbrainzngs (BSD-2), tomli-w (MIT) — all GPL-3-compatible; every external tool (cyanrip, flac/metaflac, ffmpeg, cdrdao, Picard) is a **subprocess, never linked** (KDD-10 aggregation), so its license doesn't reach in. The CTDB CRC is a clean-room reimplementation of a *factual* algorithm from the **LGPL** CueTools source (LGPL is GPL-3-compatible regardless), original Python, **never** the GPL-2.0-only `python-cuetoolsdb`; the research clones lived only in ephemeral agent sandboxes and **no foreign source is in the repo** (verified). No PR is *required* anywhere — everything ships through the existing cyanrip binary. **One flag:** `docs/archive/archival-extraction-guide-2026-06.md` is a verbatim third-party doc in a public repo — confirm its provenance/permission or replace with a cited summary.
  - **README:** added a top-of-file capability & EAC-parity matrix + a point-by-point comparison to the EAC "perfect rip" checklist. Corrected the C2 framing — the archival guide *disables* C2 deliberately, so "no C2" is best-practice-aligned, not a gap.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) provenance belongs in Platterpus-authored artifacts (JSON, EAC companion log, About, title, doctor), sourced from ONE seam, never written into cyanrip-authored bytes; (2) when a requested label (a per-track "EAC compliance" verdict) can't be produced honestly, surface the fork to the maintainer and record the verifiable-only meaning in the tooltip rather than overclaiming; (3) reverse-engineering an external algorithm from memory is unreliable — clone/read the real source and *prove* equivalence before shipping a "fix"; (4) reading an open-licensed repo for facts is fine, but keep the clone out of our tree and never port GPL-incompatible code.

- **v0.4.17 — CTDB CRC corrected + honest gap/OSS-strategy docs (2026-07-07).** Follow-up to v0.4.16, driven by the maintainer's "do it all, but be honest — no cramming, no faking" direction.
  - **CTDB CRC trim fixed (KDD-16).** Reconstructed the CUETools DB CRC bit-for-bit from the LGPL source: it IS a plain `zlib.crc32`; the placeholder only got the **trim** wrong (no trim vs the real fixed 5880-frame front + length-dependent `laststride/2` back). `crc.py` now computes `CTDBCRC(offset)` correctly (with a memory-safe streamed offset-0 variant for verify), and `calibrate.py` sweeps the ±2939 offset window via a `crc32_combine` (GF(2)) fast path so the whole sweep is a few big CRCs, not 5 879 re-CRCs of a ~600 MB disc. `CRC_VALIDATED` stays False (fails safe) until the maintainer's `--ctdb-calibrate` run on a real in-DB disc confirms it (expected: match at offset 0 vs the confidence-1347 pressing). Rewrote the ctdb crc/verify/calibrate/diagnose tests for the offset model + a hypothesis never-raises test; documented in `docs/ctdb-crc-algorithm.md`. This is why the maintainer's earlier Police calibration returned ❌ — the symmetric candidate sweep never generated the asymmetric `(5880, 9996)` trim.
  - **Research: tracker path + cache-defeat + parity (4-agent workflow).** Decisive finding on trackers: acceptance is blocked ABOVE the audio layer — the gazelle logcheckers gate on **ripper identity** (only EAC/XLD/whipper>=0.7.3 recognized; cyanrip → hard 0), on top of the EAC-checksum forgery wall we refuse to cross. So there is no honest partial score, only rejection; the landscape doc's "whipper+eaclogger = 100%" is wrong (eaclogger can't emit the EAC checksum, upstream #7) and "logchecker-go" is unverified. Cache-defeat: cyanrip/libcdio-paranoia only *attempts* it, never reports it → keep "(unknown)", never forge "Yes"; real guarantee is AR/CTDB consensus + `-Z`.
  - **Doc-currency refresh (whipper-removal debt).** KDD-18 had left stale/false claims across the docs. Corrected README, PLANNING (KDD-13 Matches table), dependency-contracts, log-format-comparison, eac-parity-investigation (added an extraction-vector scorecard), eac-log-and-repair-feasibility (ripper-identity finding), TASKS, DEPENDENCIES. Recorded two decisions: **KDD-24** (tracker acceptance out of scope by design) and **KDD-25** (cache-defeat = attempted-not-measured).
  - **`cyanrip-fork.md` Part A §10 — gap-closing OSS option menu.** Per-gap, PR-first (fork = fallback), every gap EXCEPT the signed EAC checksum (permanently refused), with a verify-or-explain honesty gate. Key honest results: a cyanrip fork can close the technical gaps (cache-defeat verdict, Test&Copy, gap/INDEX-00, HTOA) but CANNOT unlock tracker acceptance (identity wall) and CANNOT reach C2 (below cyanrip, in libcdio); the honest tracker answer is re-adding whipper (a recognized ripper) or a PR onto the logchecker allow-list. Licensing latitude: GPL-3 + subprocess-invocation (not linking) ⇒ even GPL-2-only tools like cdrdao are usable.
  - **`docs/cyanrip-upstream.md` — ordered PRs + contributor instructions (delivered).** A 4-agent research sweep (cyanrip/whipper/Logchecker/libcdio-paranoia/cdrdao) turned §10 into a ranked action list. Honest results: **DO-NOW is a Platterpus-side `cdrdao read-toc` subprocess integration** for pregap/INDEX-00 + HTOA — *no upstream PR* (GPL-2-only is fine as a subprocess; exactly how whipper got gaps); cyanrip's **live PR #115** (UltraFuzzy, adds `pregap.c`) already tackles the same two gaps → *support it, don't compete*; the tracker chain (a new stable cyanrip log schema → a Logchecker allow-list PR) is **skip-for-now** (two-repo effort against a maintainer who ignored the analogous CUERipper request #13 for ~5 years; cyanrip can't score competitively regardless); C2 is **skip** (libcdio-paranoia #3 dead ~11 years, and the BDR-209D reports C2 unsupported anyway); **re-adding whipper** is the honest tracker fallback (its native YAML log is *already* logchecker-recognized) but is gated on the >587-offset cd-paranoia bug that drove KDD-18. **Collaborator access is needed nowhere — fork + PR is the flow.** Also filed the maintainer's GitHub SOP as `docs/github-workflow-sop.md` (preface reconciles it to this repo's CLI/MCP + ff-only-proxy + `claude/…`-branch + squash-merge policy; CLAUDE.md wins on conflict).
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) the block on a capability can sit ABOVE the layer you're working in — tracker acceptance fails on ripper *identity* before audio quality is ever scored; verify the actual gate before "fixing" the wrong thing. (2) Removing a backend (whipper, KDD-18) creates silent doc debt — claims that were true of the old engine become false; a currency sweep is part of "done" for a backend swap.

- **v0.4.16 — QoL + reporting batch; CTDB algorithm cracked (deferred to next release) (2026-07-06).** Maintainer asked for missing quality-of-life features ("autodetect a new disc", "track 12 of 17", "look at what EAC/others have, no workarounds"). A 9-agent research workflow mapped the current code against EAC/dbPoweramp/XLD/whipper/cyanrip and adversarially critiqued the plan; two findings reshaped it: **disc auto-detect already ships** (v0.4.12 polling) — the real gap was removal; and **"open rip folder" already ships** — a false gap in the plan. Shipped eight features, each a small test-green commit:
  - **"Track N of M"** in the status line — the worker already held both numbers (`len(_track_ms)` / the disc banner); threaded the total into `_describe_activity`, omitted when unknown.
  - **Eject/removal reset** — `MediaWatcher` gained `observe_event()` (INSERTED/REMOVED/NO_CHANGE; `observe()` kept as the insert-only bool); the media poll clears the disc-identity view on a disc→empty transition via `_reset_disc_view()`, leaving the results pane intact.
  - **Completion notification** — a `QSystemTrayIcon` message on finish/failure (pure PySide6, **no notify-send** → no ad-hoc dep check, Critical rule #6; the workflow critique caught the original notify-send plan as a rule-#6 slip). Gated by `notify_on_completion`; a user cancel is not announced; `notify.build_completion_message` is a pure tested function.
  - **Live per-track Status column** — `RipWorker.track_completed(int)` (mirrors `current_track`) advances a Status column (⟳ Ripping / ✓ Done, symbol+text) added to `TrackTableModel` (last column so indices are stable).
  - **Auto-write EAC-layout log** (`write_eac_log_after_rip`, off by default) — renders the existing attributed-never-signed `eac_log_export` output as `… (EAC-compatible).log` beside cyanrip's `.log`.
  - **UPC/barcode + catalog + label** — `barcode` added to `ReleaseSummary`; `catalog_number`/`barcode`/`label` threaded through `RipMetadata` → Picard-style `BARCODE`/`CATALOGNUMBER`/`LABEL` tags (via the existing colon-safe `-a` path) + the report's `disc` block.
  - **Back/booklet art + load-from-file** — `save_additional_covers()` reads the CAA typed-image manifest and saves `back.jpg`/`booklet-NN.jpg`; `apply_local_cover_art()` + a *Tools → Set cover art from file…* action let the user override the front cover per-disc.
  - **Report completeness** (maintainer directive "report as much as logically possible") — `CoverArtResult.additional_saved` + `disc.{catalog_number,barcode,label}` so the one-file JSON reflects the whole package.
  - Config schema **v7→v8** (three new bools), all registered in `settings_validation._BOOL_FIELDS` and both completeness meta-tests (`test_validated_field_names_matches_config_exactly`, `test_bad_value_map_covers_every_config_field`) — the meta-tests caught the missing registrations, exactly their job.
  - **Answered the maintainer's logging questions:** `log.txt` self-purges (RotatingFileHandler, 1 MiB × 5 backups); the two-file split is intentional — one per-album `.platterpus.json` (embeds that album's session log; no `.platterpus.log` sidecar) + one app-wide `log.txt`; the new EAC log is opt-in so it isn't part of the default set.
  - **CTDB CRC — cracked, but deferred to its own release.** A source-grounded agent (github.com/gchudov/cuetools.net) proved the placeholder was wrong only in the **trim**, not the CRC: `CTDBCRC(0) = zlib.crc32(pcm[front*4 : len−back*4])`, front = stride/2 = 5880 frames (stride = 10·588·2 = 11760 words), back = laststride/2, laststride = 11760 + (2·total_frames mod 11760); offset tolerance is a constant-length window slid over ±2939; `total_frames = (leadout−150)·588`. This explains why the maintainer's Police calibration returned ❌ (the symmetric sweep never generated the asymmetric `(5880, 9996)` trim). Deferred to a focused follow-up (rewrites `crc.py`/`calibrate.py`/`verify.py`/`diagnose.py` + their tests; keep `CRC_VALIDATED=False` until the maintainer re-runs `--ctdb-calibrate` on the shipped build to confirm match@offset0/conf-1347, then flip the flag + bake the fixture). **Maintainer: don't re-run `--ctdb-calibrate` until that ships — the shipped v0.4.16 still has the old algorithm.**
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) research-before-plan pays — the workflow caught two false gaps (auto-detect, open-folder already shipped) and one rule-#6 slip (notify-send) before any code. (2) The offscreen-Qt test flake swallows pytest's summary at teardown; `--junit-xml` reads through it (real pass/fail), unlike the truncated stdout — the reliable way to trust a Qt-suite run in this environment.

- **v0.4.15 — offset-variant tracks no longer read as "bad rip" (2026-07-06).** Real-hardware validation of v0.4.13/0.4.14 on the Roots disc-1: the maintainer re-ripped, and the **stall detection worked exactly as designed** — track 18 (damaged outer edge) triggered repeated `WARNING rip stalled … at 90.8%/91.2%/…/93.6% (track 18)` + `INFO rip recovered from stall` cycles in log.txt, the status line showed *"Ripping track 18… 72% · stalled 6m 1s — the drive is stuck on a hard-to-read spot"*, no false positives on tracks 1–17, and the force-stop kept 1–17 cleanly (incremental report intact). The disc, not the software, is the limit (advised clean-the-outer-edge + retry). The one UX gap the results screen exposed: tracks 11–17 matched only the **+450 offset-variant** pressing (a partially-accurate, perfectly-good result) but the per-track AR v1/v2 cells showed cyanrip's raw *"not found, either a new pressing, or bad rip"* — alarming, and contradicting the reassuring banner. Fixed `_ar_cell` to take the track's `accuraterip_offset` and render **"offset-variant match (N)"** when the standard checksum missed but the offset variant matched, and to render a plain **"not in DB"** for genuine no-matches instead of the *"…or bad rip"* phrasing. Also switched the plain-match branch to key on `accuraterip_is_match` (confidence ≥ 1) so cyanrip's "accurately ripped, confidence N" renders the clean "OK (N)" too (the old `"exact match"` substring check only caught whipper). Same trust-first principle as the CTDB honesty fix (an unvalidated/benign result must never read as a failure). Tests cover the offset-variant cell, the cyanrip not-found wording, plain-match-wins-over-offset, and the end-to-end table row.

- **v0.4.14 — duration-weighted ETA (2026-07-05).** After the v0.4.13 re-rip of the Police disc confirmed the fixes live (banner + "Ripping" label + verbose report all present; 13/14 AccurateRip exact this time, track 3 improved from partial→exact on the re-read, track 5 a consistent +450 offset-variant pressing), the one remaining ETA issue was the ±~10-minute oscillation: the overall progress bar gave every track an equal 1/N slice regardless of length, so a long track made the bar crawl (ETA spikes up) and a short one made it race (ETA dips). Fixed by weighting each track's slice of the 5–95% read band by its real **MusicBrainz duration** (`length_ms`), so `overall_percent` advances with *audio position* — which at a steady read speed is ~linear with wall-clock, so the elapsed÷fraction ETA stops swinging. Plumbing: `TrackSummary.length_ms` (already parsed by the MB client) → new `TrackTag.length_ms` passthrough → `_start_rip_worker` number-keyed map (mirrors the existing ISRC passthrough) → `RipWorker` precomputes a validated per-track prefix-sum table in `__init__` and consumes it in the new `_overall_from_track`. **Strict fallback:** the weighting is used ONLY when every track has a positive length and the numbers are a contiguous 1..N matching the disc's track count; any gap/None/mismatch (unknown disc, partial metadata) drops cleanly to today's equal-slice math, so there's zero regression risk. Not recorded in the report schema (the eta_trace's `overall_percent` already reflects it); a `progress_model` field is a possible future add. Tests cover weighted values, equal-slice fallback, and the incomplete-lengths refusal.

- **v0.4.13 — stall-aware ETA, always-verbose rip report, build fingerprint in `--version` (2026-07-05).** Driven by two maintainer questions after the Roots/Police real-hardware rips: *"have you done the ETA update to make timing more accurate?"* and *"do we need a json AND a txt log, or will one do? are they verbose enough to be a useful record?"* Answered both, then acted on what the answers surfaced (three reversible, in-scope improvements batched into one release):
  - **ETA — the accuracy fix was already shipped (v0.4.10: trailing-90 s rate window + EMA smoothing + coarse buckets); the *stall-awareness* half was missing, and that's the half that mattered for the track-18 hang.** When a damaged sector flat-lines progress, `_album_eta_text` fell back to a cumulative projection that just creeps a misleading — eventually absurd — countdown upward (the maintainer watched it show "~4h left" on a read that was stuck for hours). Added stall detection keyed on **meaningful forward progress** (not zero movement, so a hairline crawl like 72.00→72.02% over minutes is still caught): track the album fraction at the last ≥0.5 %-of-album step and the time it was reached; once no step clears for 3 min the status line reads *"stalled Nm — the drive is stuck on a hard-to-read spot (a scratch or smudge)"* instead of a countdown. A merely-slow-but-advancing read is never mislabelled; recovery is automatic when the bar jumps past the next step. Reset per pass; regression tests cover detect / recover / no-false-positive.
  - **Logs — three artifacts, not redundant; but the JSON wasn't verbose by default.** Confirmed the design: cyanrip's own `.log` (EAC-equivalent, ripper-owned), our per-album `.platterpus.json` (which already *embeds* that rip's session log under `debug.lines` — there is deliberately no `.platterpus.log` sidecar), and the global cross-session `~/.local/share/platterpus/log.txt` (the catch-all for failures with no rip folder — a startup crash, a drive that won't enumerate). One file can't do the job: different scope and lifetime. **The real gap was verbosity** — both the JSON's embedded log and log.txt defaulted to INFO because `debug_logging` defaults to `False`, so a report sent for a problem rip was missing the subprocess/probe/parse DEBUG detail. Fix: the in-memory `SessionLogBuffer` (the JSON's sole source) is now held at **DEBUG always**, decoupled from the toggle — it's in-memory and bounded (50 k records / 10 k embedded), so DEBUG is free; the "Debug logging" setting now governs only log.txt's on-disk verbosity.
  - **Follow-ups (same batch, maintainer's ask "all of this can show up in either the log or json file" + "the logs files should note the application and version"):** (1) the stall is now **recorded**, not just shown — a `WARNING` on entry (so it's in `log.txt` at the default INFO level *and* the report's DEBUG-always embedded log) and an `INFO` recovery line, fired once per stall via an `_eta_stalled` latch (no per-tick flood). (2) Every `log.txt` file is stamped with a `──── Platterpus <version> (build <fp>) ────` banner at session start **and on each rotation** (a small `_BannerRotatingFileHandler` that re-stamps in `doRollover`), so even a rotated backup names its build; the JSON already carried version + fingerprint in `generator`.
  - **Two real bugs the uploaded Police rip (v0.4.12, archival-grade: 12/14 AccurateRip exact @ conf 200, 2/14 offset-variant, 0 errors, all FLAC clean, cover embedded, ~1× realtime) surfaced — both folded into v0.4.13:** (1) **the status said "Encoding track N" for the entire 59-minute rip** (the `eta_trace` showed it on every one of 329 samples). cyanrip reads+encodes in one pass ("Ripping and encoding track N"), and `_describe_activity` mapped the `and encoding` group to the label "Encoding" — so the slow *disc read* looked like slow *encoding* (which is instant). Now both cyanrip progress forms → "Ripping track N…" (one honest verb; also kills the "Reading"/"Encoding" flicker when the two forms interleave). (2) **the cancelled Roots rip logged a full `FileNotFoundError` traceback at WARNING** when the report write found the album folder gone (cancelled + cleaned) — `write_report` now detects a vanished parent dir and logs a concise INFO instead of a crash-looking stack trace, keeping full detail for genuine write errors.
  - **Not bugs (confirmed healthy in the report):** CTDB `no_match` is the KDD-16 placeholder behaving correctly — `crc_validated:false` with the honest "not hardware-validated; AccurateRip is the authority; run --ctdb-calibrate" message and all 102 `db_crcs` recorded for offline calibration; recompress correctly "backend already maxes compression". **Noted-not-fixed:** the ETA oscillates ±~10m because the overall-progress model treats all tracks as equal 1/14 slices regardless of duration — a duration-weighted model would tighten it (future refinement; the stall/rate/scan-band fixes already landed).
  - **Version-control audit tail:** the app version is already cleanly single-sourced (`__init__.__version__`, read dynamically by pyproject) and reported everywhere (`--version`, About, MB user-agent, report `generator.version`, CI guard); the one gap was the **build fingerprint** (git short-SHA / `source`) living only in the JSON report. Now surfaced in `--version`, Help → About, and the startup log line so a bug report carries the exact build.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) an ETA is only honest if it can say *"I can't estimate this — it's stuck"*; a projection that keeps counting down through a hang is worse than no number. Detect a stall by **meaningful progress over time**, not zero movement, so a crawl counts. (2) The single self-contained debug artifact must be **verbose by default** — gating its detail behind a setting the user has to find first means the one report they send is the one missing the evidence. An in-memory, bounded buffer can afford DEBUG unconditionally; only the on-disk log needs a verbosity dial.

- **v0.4.12 — incremental rip report, auto-detect a newly-inserted disc, timestamped status (2026-07-05).** Driven by the maintainer's real 4-disc box-set session (Roots Music — "An American Journey", disc 1/4). The disc-1 rip ran ~real-time for tracks 1–17 (17 tracks in ~47 min for ~54 min of audio — healthy, EAC-class) then **stalled for hours on track 18** (a damaged outer-edge sector; cyanrip at max paranoia grinding retries) and was force-stopped at 1h55m. That surfaced three asks:
  - **Incremental `.platterpus.json`.** A clean cancel already wrote a partial report (BUG-5's `finally`), but only ONCE at finish — a hard kill/power-loss wrote nothing, and the partial lacked post-rip verification. Now `RipWorker._write_incremental_report` re-parses the growing cyanrip `.log` after each `_CYANRIP_TRACK_DONE` and re-writes a partial report (`outcome.status: "in_progress"`) beside it — on the worker thread, atomic (`write_report`'s temp+replace), best-effort, main-passes-only (never the auto-fix temp rip). The GUI still writes the full report at finish, superseding. cyanrip's `.log` and the app `log.txt` were already incremental; this closes the JSON gap.
  - **Auto-detect a new disc.** Cancel force-stops AND *ejects* the drive, and there was no media-change detection, so "put a new CD in" did nothing until a manual Rescan. New Qt-free `drive_media.py`: `probe_disc_status` (a best-effort `CDROM_DRIVE_STATUS` ioctl — never spins the disc, never raises, degrades to `"unavailable"`) + a pure `MediaWatcher` state machine (fire only on a real empty→disc transition; the startup disc and `unavailable` blips never trigger). A 2.5 s `_media_poll_timer` in `MainWindow` runs `_poll_disc_media` while idle (skips during a rip/scan — the drive is busy) and auto-runs the existing rescan when a disc appears. The ioctl is hardware-gated; the transition logic + poll wiring are unit-tested with a fake probe.
  - **Timestamped status.** `RipProgress.set_status` now prefixes `HH:MM:SS · ` (injectable clock) so a screenshot/glance shows *when* the current phase was reached.
  - **Also clarified for the maintainer:** the hours-long rip was the **disc** (track 18), not the software — the 17 clean tracks prove near-real-time EAC-class speed; and the CTDB "no match" on the Police disc is the known placeholder-CRC (KDD-16), fixed-wording in 0.4.11, still awaiting the `--ctdb-calibrate` trim. Noted that the big pre-session "portable brief" the maintainer pasted was a stale 0.4.9 snapshot — its §A–§G already shipped in 0.4.10/0.4.11.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) a report meant to survive a crash must be written *incrementally by the producer* (the worker), not once by the consumer (the GUI finish handler) — the finish handler never runs on a hard kill. (2) Isolate a hardware syscall (the CDROM ioctl) behind a pure, tested transition state machine + a best-effort probe, so the decision logic is validated in the cloud and the un-runnable ioctl degrades to a no-op.

- **v0.4.10 shipped, then a real-hardware rip cracked the CTDB mystery (2026-07-05).** Cut **v0.4.10** (the handoff work): pre-flight-audited the release path with a workflow, bumped `__version__`, rolled the CHANGELOG, squash-merged PR #43, dispatched `release.yml` — AppImage + `.sha256` + PyPI all green (github-actions bot, pre-release). Then the maintainer ran a real rip (The Police — "Every Breath You Take: The Classics") on the Bazzite/Pioneer rig and sent the four artifacts (GUI log, cue, cyanrip log, `.platterpus.json`) + a finished-GUI screenshot.
