  - **The rip was archival-grade:** AccurateRip 12/14 exact (conf up to 200) + 2/14 offset-variant (conf 200), 0 read errors, FLAC decodes clean, cover embedded, SHA-256s recorded. The 2 "partial" tracks are a pressing-offset variant, not damage; the hand-entered `+667` offset is *proven* correct by the 12 exact matches (a wrong offset fails all tracks).
  - **The one real finding — CTDB "no match" was a false alarm, and the report proved it.** The `ctdb` block showed `confidence: 1347, crc_validated: false, our_crc: 9C4045CE, matched_crc: null` — i.e. the disc **is** in CTDB (thousands of submissions) but our CRC matched none, against an AccurateRip-confidence-200 rip. A workflow investigation confirmed this is **not a bug hunt**: the CTDB CRC is a *deliberate placeholder* (`ctdb/crc.py`, a zlib CRC-32 at offset 0, `CRC_VALIDATED=False`) that **fails safe** to `no_match` — the real CueTools `CTDBCRC` needs a ~2940-frame guard-band trim + ±2939 offset sweep that was left un-implemented pending exactly this hardware data (KDD-16). **The rip un-gated it:** the lookup succeeding means the `toc=` wire format is now hardware-CONFIRMED; only the CRC trim remains, and `ctdb/calibrate.py` already exists to *discover* the trim by sweeping until it reproduces a DB CRC.
  - **Shipped toward v0.4.11 (CTDB honesty + self-diagnosing + calibration-from-the-AppImage):** (1) the results/report wording no longer asserts "your rip differs" on a placeholder `no_match` — `ctdb_verdict_line` + `verify._match_verdict` now branch on `crc_validated` (unvalidated → "not confirmed / experimental; a non-match doesn't mean your rip is wrong; AccurateRip is the authority"); (2) report schema **v7→v8**: the `ctdb` block now carries `db_crcs` + `entry_count` alongside `our_crc`, so a saved report is calibratable offline; (3) extracted `ctdb/diagnose.py` (shared by `scripts/ctdb_verify.py` and the new **`platterpus --ctdb-calibrate <folder>`** flag) so the maintainer can pin the CRC straight from the AppImage — no dev checkout, no re-rip — against the existing Police folder.
  - **The Mutter idea was a dead end (good catch by the maintainer):** their Rammstein — Mutter disc is a **burned CD-R**, which AccurateRip/CTDB don't index like a pressing, so it can't disambiguate our CRC bug — it'd `no_match` for legitimate reasons. The clean disambiguator is a pressed popular disc; the Police disc already is one.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) an *unvalidated* check must never render a definitive negative verdict — a `no_match` from a known-placeholder CRC reads as "couldn't confirm", not "your rip is wrong" (graduated into the CTDB verdict guards + KDD-16 note). (2) When a subsystem is hardware-gated, the *report* is the validation vehicle: carry enough diagnostic data (`db_crcs` vs `our_crc`) that a single real-disc artifact un-gates the fix, and expose the calibration from the shipped binary so the non-dev rig can run it. (3) A stale editable install silently fails `test_installed_metadata_matches_canonical_version` after a version bump — `pip install -e .` to refresh; CI is unaffected (fresh install).

- **Handoff execution — rip-report v7 enrichment, the confirmed bug batch, the KDD-18 doc sweep, tech-debt + the in-app viewer (2026-07-03).** Picked up the `handoff20260703.md` work on a fresh session branch (`claude/new-session-6z0up0`). Confirmed scope with the maintainer first (they'd asked for the report before implementing): they chose **everything, in priority order**, **merge via PR (no release)**, and to **fold the prior session's unmerged branch in themselves** — but with no PR on it they said "merge and go", so I fast-forwarded `claude/platterpus-full-audit-82dnt4`'s clean delta (ETA trailing-window, the v6→v7 config migration, the `%%` naming fix, the offscreen-GC test tweaks) into this branch and built on top. Landed in test-green, individually-committed units.
  - **A/B — rip report `.platterpus.json` v7 (the maintainer's #1 ask: "put as much errors, gate checks of all kinds into the json").** `REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 6→7 with all-additive sections so one file explains a rip: `outcome` (process success/cancelled/failed + failure hint + whether the auto-heal fired — the actual run result, distinct from the AccurateRip `verdict`); `settings` (what the GUI *asked for*, incl. the read offset as `{configured, applied, effective}` — the log shows `0` either way); `disc` provenance; `environment` (Python/OS/PySide6/install-channel + per-dependency versions **and paths** from the launch-time probe, never a fresh GUI-thread re-probe); `generator.build_fingerprint` (git short-SHA stamped by `build_appimage.sh` into a generated `_build.py`, `"source"` fallback); `verification.gates` (turns an ambiguous null into ran/disabled/backend-self-verifies/flac-only) + `verification.recompress`; `cover_art` (a real `CoverArtResult` — found / why-not 404-vs-network / embedded_count / bytes); `read_speed.secure_rerip` (why the dynamic re-rip did/didn't run); `log_parse`; and a consolidated severity-tagged `issues[]`. A hard failure with **no** log now writes a minimal `platterpus-rip-failure.platterpus.json` (the most-broken rips were the least diagnosable). New `build_info.py` holds the fingerprint/channel/env facts (Qt-free at import so the pure report builder can use it). **Section B**: a report-completeness meta-test pins the exact top-level + `verification` key sets, so a new section can't be added without being surfaced (or a section silently dropped) — the maintainer's "make sure there is a test so we don't miss that."
  - **C — the confirmed bug batch (11), each with a same-change regression test.** BUG-1 off-thread QWidget read in post-rip tagging (snapshot the track table on the GUI thread + a thread-identity assert); BUG-2 TOCTOU in `_find_log_path` that could leave `finished` un-emitted and the rip lock stuck (guard the stat + belt-wrap `start_rip`); BUG-5 finish-handler state-clear not in `finally`; BUG-3 unbounded `.sha256` read; BUG-4 CTDB `TimeoutExpired` escaping the never-raise path; BUG-6 tool-path validator missing the raw-value control-char check; BUG-7 Picard not detached; BUG-8 parser param shadow; BUG-9 deps re-probe on the GUI thread after the setup wizard (now a worker + nested event loop); BUG-10 dialog-centering dedup by `id()` (→ WeakSet); BUG-11 `.desktop` `Exec=` control-char injection. All three "suspected" (9/10/11) verified real.
  - **D — the KDD-18 doc-currency sweep (the dominant remaining theme).** The prior audit's "all whipper drift fixed" was scoped too narrowly. Reconciled code as source-of-truth, then reworded whipper-as-current across help/README (renamed the re-rip control to "Max reads to confirm a shaky track", described the dynamic model, added a "How ripping works" section), the drive-setup wizard ("saves to whipper.conf" was false — the offset goes to Platterpus config, fed to cyanrip via `-s`; removed the dead `DriveSetupResult.backup_path`), architecture.md/PLANNING.md (cyanrip is the sole backend; the report has several async re-writers, not just CTDB), and ~12 code files' comments — **labelling genuinely-inert seams** (the whipper-format progress/heal parsers, `_WHIPPER_CLI`) rather than pretending they're live, and keeping the legitimate references (the whipper-format log parser, the legacy `whipper.conf` offset reader). Removed the dead `scripts/preflight.py --backend whipper` flag (+ CLAUDE.md). Fixed the Python matrix (3.11–3.14), DEPENDENCIES pytest pin, PEP-639 classifier note, and the CLAUDE companion-doc list.
  - **E — tech debt (behaviour-preserving).** TD-2: one `_launch_post_rip_daemon(compute, signal, thread_attr)` now owns the rip-generation staleness guard, with the four verify/checksum launchers routed through it (a 6th can't forget the guard). TD-5: the album `rip_dir` computed once (was 5×). TD-3/TD-4/TD-6: corrected now-false docstrings (the deps registry doesn't cascade on `tier`/`fallback_tiers`; the resolver dialogs that "don't exist yet" do) and reframed the whipper progress classifiers as inert.
  - **F — IMP-1: an in-app read-only viewer for the log/report.** "View log"/"View report" no longer call `openUrl` (a `.log`/`.platterpus.json` has no default app on a fresh KDE → the "Open With" chooser the maintainer reported); they open a self-contained monospace `FileViewerDialog` (bounded, never-raises read) with an "Open externally…" escape hatch. "Open rip folder" keeps `openUrl` (a folder has a handler).
  - **Deferred, with rationale (not silently dropped):** **TD-1** (extract a `PostRipVerifyMixin` from the ~1700-line `main_window_rip.py`) — a large behaviour-preserving refactor of a heavily-tested god-object that is genuinely its own focused PR; rushing it at the tail of a long session risked a regression against "momentum with *safety*". **IMP-2** (bound the quit-during-rip drive-stop) — [low], hardware-gated, and the current path is already bounded (every subprocess `timeout=20`), just potentially slow on a cold container. The **medium-priority report additions** (verification.tagging counts, read_speed.stop_reason, per-attempt reason, checksums_skipped_reason) — the v7 core + Tier-S landed; these are additive follow-ups.
  - **Test-running under the offscreen flake:** PySide6 + `offscreen` + worker threads SIGSEGV/SIGBUS *mid-run* on the full single-process suite (the conftest `os._exit` hook only masks *teardown* crashes). Ran a **per-file isolated runner** (each test file in its own process, crashers retried) for an authoritative green signal — all 91 files green throughout.
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) a report/JSON that's meant to be the single debug artifact needs a **completeness meta-test** — the value is that a consumer can rely on every section being present-or-null, and that guarantee has to be enforced, not trusted. (2) When a report field's source lives on a worker/adapter thread or in `params` that the finish handler clears, **snapshot it into `self._last_*` while the producer is alive** — never re-derive it on a debounced re-write (the worker is gone) and never re-probe it on the GUI thread (the freeze). (3) A doc-currency sweep after a backend swap is not "delete every mention of the old tool": distinguish *stale current-behavior claims* (fix) from *legitimate legacy references and inert seams* (keep, and label the seams) — a blind grep-replace would have broken the legacy offset reader and the old-log parser.

- **Proactive follow-ups after the 0.4.9 update-restart report: expectation-setting + Phase-6 backfill that found a real bug (2026-07-03).** The maintainer merged PR #41 (the relaunch failure-mode locks), then said *"do all proactive fixes you can, i will update my logs when i can too."* Worked the two highest-value proactive threads:
  - **Update-restart UX (closes the "did not restart" loop).** The relaunch code is *correct* (Popen with scrubbed env + `start_new_session`, keeps the window open on `OSError`; `closeEvent` has no veto, so `self.close()` reliably exits) — so the report was the ~20–30s AppImage cold-extract gap being read as a failure. Since we can't shrink the extract, the "Restart Platterpus now?" prompt now **sets the expectation** ("the window can take 20–30 seconds to reappear — that's normal"). Regression test locks the heads-up text so it can't be silently dropped.
  - **Phase 6 test backfill (audit §D) — and a real bug the fuzzing surfaced.** Added hypothesis property tests for the two never-raise/security surfaces the audit flagged: `naming.render_preview` (never raises on any typed template) and the Settings path validators (`..` traversal + control chars rejected at *every* position, not just the hand-picked example spots). The control-char property test **failed on `/tmp/\x1c`** → real bug: `_validate_dir` ran `_has_control_char` on the whitespace-**stripped** value, but Python classifies the C0 information separators `\x1c`–`\x1f` (and `\t\n\r\v\f`) as whitespace, so a leading/trailing one was trimmed before the check and slipped through — yet stayed in the saved config and reached the rip. A mid-string control char *was* caught; the ends weren't. Fixed by checking the **raw** value (matching what `_validate_template` already did). Also capped `update_check._default_fetch`'s response read (4 MiB, matching the cover-art/CTDB/updater fetchers) with fault-injection tests, and added fault-injection tests for the host `SubprocessRunner`'s `127`/`124` sentinels (previously only the injected fake runner was exercised). Suite **1497 green**, 93% coverage, ruff clean; naming/update_check now 100%, step_engine 100%.
  - **CI flake fixed at the root (not just re-run).** PR #42's py3.12 cell failed the offscreen SEGV (exit 139) **three runs straight** while 3.11/3.13/3.14 stayed green — re-running wasn't converging. The faulthandler dump pinned it: a cyclic-GC pass fired **on the cover-art worker thread** during `apply_cover_art`'s file write and finalized a QObject off the Qt thread → segfault *mid-run* (so the `os._exit` shutdown-hook can't mask it). Fixed by pausing the cyclic collector (`gc.disable()`) for the life of the one E2E test that runs real Qt work on worker threads, restored in teardown — refcount freeing still runs, so no assertion changes. 6/6 green locally after. Graduated to `docs/testing.md`. (This turns the red check legitimately green — no admin-override needed.)
  - **Lesson (durable):** property/fuzz tests earn their keep at *position-dependent* invariants — a security check written against a few hand-picked example positions (`..`/control char in the middle) can silently not hold at the edges. And beware `str.strip()`: it removes more than the obvious whitespace (the C0 separators `\x1c`–`\x1f` too), so "validate the stripped value" quietly narrows a character-class check. Validate the **raw** input for forbidden characters; strip only for the empty/format checks.

- **0.4.9 released, then a real-hardware update-restart report → regression-locked the relaunch flow (2026-07-03).** After 0.4.9 was merged (PR #40) and published (GitHub Release + PyPI, all CI green incl. the new Python-3.14 cell), the maintainer updated in-app and reported *"updated to 0.4.9 but did not restart"* — then started a real-disc rip. Two things came out of it:
  - **Diagnosis:** the auto-relaunch is performed by the version being updated *from* (0.4.8's code), and a freshly-downloaded 237 MB AppImage cold-extracts (~20–30s) on first launch, which reads as "didn't restart." The definitive tell is one log line (`relaunching into the new version:` vs `relaunch failed`) — requested from the maintainer, non-urgent.
  - **The ask ("so we don't keep battling it") → regression tests.** `main_window_update.py` (the `UpdateMixin` relaunch orchestration) had NO dedicated coverage of the *failure* modes — only the happy path + `_relaunch_env` in isolation. Added three locks to `test_ui_main_window.py`: (1) a relaunch spawn that raises must show the fallback message and **NOT close the window** (never "closed into nothing"); (2) No-to-restart neither spawns nor closes; (3) the spawn is asserted to pass `start_new_session=True` **and** an `env=` with the old-mount vars (`APPDIR`/`LD_LIBRARY_PATH`) scrubbed — locking the *call site*, not just the `_relaunch_env` helper (a refactor dropping `env=_relaunch_env()` would reintroduce the "closed but didn't reopen" bug and now fails a test). Test-only; merged to main so it guards every future PR (no release needed).
  - **Lesson (durable):** the AppImage relaunch/restart flow is a proven recurring battleground (env whack-a-mole 2026-06-27 & 0.4.6; this restart report on 0.4.9). Lock the **call site + every failure branch**, not just the pure helper — the bugs live in the "what happens when the spawn fails / the user says no / the env isn't passed" seams, which unit-testing the helper alone never exercises.

- **Post-0.4.8 (on branch `claude/platterpus-full-audit-82dnt4`, unreleased) — full end-to-end audit: 15-agent adversarial review → staged fix execution (2026-07-02).** The maintainer asked for an exhaustive, full-power review of code/docs/rules/dependencies before cutting 0.4.9, with a prioritized plan for approval first. Ran a 15-agent adversarial audit (8 review dimensions) + 3 web-research agents, synthesized **58 findings** into `docs/audit-2026-07-02.md` (now `docs/archive/audit-2026-07-02.md`) (the durable deliverable: exec summary, findings table with CONFIRMED/SUSPECTED marks, dependency KEEP/UPGRADE decisions, test plan, staged Phase 0–7 plan). Maintainer approved: *"move forward, and do your recommendations, updated the claude.md file, and do the PEP 639 license migration."* Executing the plan in small, test-green, individually-committed units (suite 1456→1488+ tests, ruff clean at every step). What shipped this session:
  - **Phase 0 (doc hygiene):** whipper→cyanrip drift across CLAUDE.md/PLANNING/DEPENDENCIES/README/architecture, KDD range fix, leaked `</content>`/`</invoke>` tags removed, module-map + dependency-contracts HTTP fixes.
  - **Phase 1 (GUI-thread — the sacred rule):** #1 MB queries genuinely off-thread (a `moveToThread` worker still runs on the **caller's** thread when a slot is *called* directly, not emitted — root cause) + MB socket timeout; #9 non-blocking Cancel; #10 update-dialog Cancel; #8 check-deps/Refresh off-thread; #7 wizard/uninstaller close teardown; #13 closeEvent thread teardown; the `stop_thread` helper (cancel→quit→brief wait→**detach** a still-running QThread rather than block/destroy — destroying a running QThread SIGABRTs).
  - **Phase 2 (rip integrity):** #4 UTF-8 log read + finally-guard; #5 rip-generation token drops a stale async verify; #6 MB result staleness — every lookup echoes the **disc-id** it was fired for, mismatch dropped (wrong-album guard; mirrors DiscInfoWorker's device echo); #3 not-in-AccurateRip-DB skips the wasteful whole-disc re-rip; #18 atomic FLAC swap; #19 swap addendum keeps the album `.log` CRCs honest; #17 subprocess reap on stream error; #20 log discovery scoped to *this* rip's start (shared music root would otherwise adopt a previous album's log); #30 checksums skipped (not faked) when post-rip work hasn't settled; #37 Start locked during a scan; #22/#38 probe races + unknown-mode latch.
  - **Phase 3 (dynamic-rerip default, maintainer-approved Option A):** `secure_rerip_matches` default 0→2 in config + all presets, so the 0.4.9 headline feature actually runs out of the box (guarded by the Phase-2 not-in-DB fix so it's safe on CD-Rs).
  - **Phase 4/5 (validation, security, packaging):** #11 never-raise config load (+ back up a bad file); #12 validate a hand-edited config at load; #14 capture flac/ffmpeg stderr to the log; #24 CTDB response size cap; #25 CAA release-id URL-encoded; #26 `.desktop` Exec escaped per freedesktop spec; #27 watchdog bounds a stalled ffmpeg decode; #28 MB timeout; #29 colon-restore now covers **every** field fed to cyanrip (year/genre/isrc/albumid, not just title/artist); #23 force-stop prefers device-scoped `fuser -k` before the broad by-name `pkill` (never kills an unrelated rip on another drive); #32 EAC-style export stops fabricating "Read mode: Secure"/"C2 pointers"; #39 CTDB CRC streamed track-by-track (~1.5 GB → one track); #40 verify workers enumerate only the album's direct FLACs (non-recursive) — nested folders were corrupting the CTDB TOC; #34 uninstall removes the exported `flac` wrapper too; #15 release workflow asserts version==tag and force-publishes on a retry; #16 no-shell guard extended to `scripts/`+`build/` + a `.sh` errexit guard. **PEP 639 license migration** (SPDX `license = "GPL-3.0-only"` + `license-files`, setuptools≥77; KDD-10 rationale amended) — maintainer sign-off obtained.
  - **CLAUDE.md locked-text edits** (approved): the whipper→cyanrip "Project"/Stack lines and subprocess-parsing wording updated to reflect the cyanrip-only reality (KDD-18).
  - **Every shipped fix carries its regression test in the same commit** (institutional rule). Each bug also got a `CHANGELOG.md [Unreleased]` bullet; CI-only/test-only commits use `[skip changelog]`.
  - **Also fixed after the first pass:** #35 (unknown-distro Distrobox install now uses the injected `elevate`/`pkexec`, not a hardcoded `sudo` that has no GUI TTY), #36 (`_host_stack_ready` delegates to `cyanrip_on_host`, so a PATH-native cyanrip isn't nagged — Critical rule #6), and #21 (album ETA re-baselined per pass, so it no longer balloons on a read-speed retry / secure re-rip). Suite at **1492 green**.
  - **Then, on the maintainer's "yes, do that": #33, the CI changes, and the low-sev batch.**
    - **#33 (removed the dead resolution cascade).** Traced it fully: `DependencyManager.resolve_missing` (+ `_dispatch`/`_next_tier`/`_clone_with_tier`) was a *second* implementation of install-tier routing, dead in production — the GUI always routes via `_resolve_missing_unified`, and preflight only calls `check_all`. Deleted the cascade + the manager's now-vestigial `auto`/`queued`/`manual` params, the GUI-only `_DialogQueuedResolver` (it merely wrapped `PendingInstallsDialog`, which the UI already drives directly), its re-export, and the dead `_gui_auto_consent`; kept the three resolver classes in `deps/resolvers.py` as the tier vocabulary (`AutoInstaller` is reused by the GUI). No behaviour change (the removed path had no callers). Resolution: `deps/` probes, the UI routes — one place each. Docs (architecture.md §3.5 + map, PLANNING.md) updated. This is the *right* reading of Critical rule #6: the rule centralizes *presence/version* logic (still in `deps/`); resolution routing is unavoidably Qt-coupled and belongs in the UI.
    - **CI/deps:** widened the dev pytest pin to `>=8,<10` and added **Python 3.14** to the CI matrix + classifiers (validate-in-CI — I run 3.11/pytest 8 locally, so CI is the gate; a local `-W error::pytest.PytestRemovedIn9Warning` run showed no removal warnings on the sampled modules).
    - **Low-sev batch (#42–58): did the ones with clear, confirmable value; left the rest with rationale.** Done: the in-app updater's **download-size cap** (a hostile server can't stream the disk full before the SHA-256 gate — bounded by header AND running count), and the whipper→cyanrip **install-script desktop-entry** currency (menu Comment/Keywords, uninstall label). Deliberately NOT changed: `%%Y` (its `%%Y`→`%%Y` output is a *tested, intentional* contract — the audit note was ambiguous, and changing tested behaviour on a guess is wrong); `run_capture` buffer cap (a local trusted-tool probe already bounded by its timeout — not a hostile source like CTDB/#39); temp-file "TOCTOU" (every atomic-write is in a user-owned dir — config, the music output root — not world-writable `/tmp`, so the classic race doesn't apply); and the packaging-cosmetic nits (orphaned metainfo.xml, appimage.yml zsync, make_icon doc). Suite **1487 green**.
    - **Lesson:** "delete the dead second implementation" is the correct resolution of a Critical-rule-#6 "single strategy" violation only after proving the deleted path has zero production callers (traced: GUI → `_resolve_missing_unified`; preflight → `check_all`) — and only the *routing* is duplicated; the tier *building blocks* stay. And a terse audit note is not a mandate to change **tested** behaviour (`%%Y`): verify the intent against the test first.
  - **Still open (lower priority):** the skipped low-sev nits above, Phase 6 property/fault-injection backfill + hardware `test-plan.md` checkboxes, Phase 7 (optional architecture split). `docs/archive/audit-2026-07-02.md` §E tracks the rest (graduated to the TASKS.md Documentation backlog 2026-07-21).
  - **Lessons (durable):** (1) the *echo-the-context* staleness pattern generalizes beyond DiscInfoWorker — any async worker whose result can outlive its request should echo an identity token the GUI re-checks (MB now does; graduated as a note in the audit doc). (2) When the output dir is a *shared* root, "find the most recent artifact" must be time-scoped to the operation, or it silently adopts a neighbour's. (3) A destructive system action (kill/pkill) must be scoped as narrowly as the intent — device-scoped before name-scoped — so it can't hit an unrelated process. (4) Don't fabricate a record field you didn't measure (EAC log), same bar as "parsers never invent."

- **Post-0.4.8 (on branch → main, unreleased; intended 0.4.9) — dynamic-always, numbers-as-max, input/output validation + enforcement, and the drive-won't-stop fix (2026-07-02).** A large live-testing batch. The maintainer ripped the Police disc on v0.4.8 and gave a run of directives; all **merged to main** but the **release was deferred** at the maintainer's call — they want a full Fable-5-driven review of code/docs/rules/dependencies first. The CHANGELOG entries sit under `[Unreleased]`; version stays 0.4.8 until the release is cut.
  - **Dynamic secure re-rip is now the behaviour, not a checkbox.** The maintainer: "the dialog boxes of dynamic are a waste of time." Removed the opt-in checkbox; `secure_rerip_dynamic` defaults **True** and is TOML-only for power users. Ripping is always: one fast pass (`-Z 0`), then secure only the AccurateRip-failing tracks.
  - **The user's number is the *ceiling*, not a tax.** "the numbers a user picks should be the max, do not hardcode a max, unless it's like 10." The `-Z` spinner is relabelled "Max reads to confirm a shaky track"; the worker uses the configured value as the re-rip ceiling. **Subtlety that bit:** the auto_ladder *read-error recovery* still needs *some* `-Z` when the user left it at 0 — so `next_step`/`_auto_fix_tracks` fall back to the small internal `MAX_SECURE_REREP` (3) only when the user set nothing (`secure_rerip_matches or MAX_SECURE_REREP`), which is the "like 10" sanity cap the maintainer explicitly allowed. Two auto_ladder tests caught the conflation when I first wired the user's value as the sole ceiling.
  - **The drive kept ripping after the app closed (real-user report).** `closeEvent` *did* call `_rip_worker.cancel()`, but that only killpg's the **host-side** wrapper; on rootless podman the in-container cyanrip is a separate tree podman won't forward the signal into, and the in-app Cancel's force-stop QTimer never fires once the window is gone. Fix: `_stop_rip_on_shutdown()` runs `drive_control.free_drive()` (kill the reader, no eject) **synchronously** in `closeEvent` — a daemon thread would be killed mid-`pkill` as the interpreter exits, so it *must* be synchronous. **Graduated** to `docs/dependency-contracts.md` (shutdown contract). The Force-stop/Cancel paths already reached the in-container reader; only window-close didn't.
  - **Input validation on every setting + visible error + logging (the governance headline).** The maintainer asked: "is none of this input/output validation logged as a requirement in claude.md?" — **it wasn't**, anywhere, which is precisely why Settings had only ad-hoc per-widget ranges. Added it as a CLAUDE.md Code convention (*Validate every input and every dependency output*) and a `docs/testing.md §5` rule. Built the pure `settings_validation` module (type/range/chars/format for every `Config` field), a live red banner in the dialog that marks the offending field *as you type*, **OK blocked** on errors, and `log_issues` to the log file. metaflac availability deliberately **defers to the dependency subsystem** (Critical rule #6 — not a scattered PATH check).
  - **Security + enforcement (the maintainer: "we don't need exploits from inputs… make sure this happens every time").** Rejected exploit-shaped inputs: path traversal (`..`), control chars/NUL, absolute templates. Made the rules **self-enforcing in CI**: a completeness meta-test (`validated_field_names()` must equal the `Config` fields), a reacts-to-a-bad-value test (corrupt each field → must raise an issue — so a new setting can't ship unvalidated), and `test_security_no_shell` (AST guard: no `shell=True`/`os.system`/`os.popen` anywhere — confirmed clean). Output side: ffmpeg's discarded stderr is now captured to the log on failure.
  - **Richer JSON (schema v6) + the dependency-contract reference.** Surfaced `speed_changeable` (rip block) + per-track `extraction_speed`/`extraction_quality`/`pre_emphasis`/`peak_level` (all already parsed) so the re-rip's report reports back more. New `docs/dependency-contracts.md` — the single reference for the exact args/syntax/output of every dependency (the code-side half of the validation rule).
  - **Deferred (needs a decision — flagged in the final report):** actually injecting *experimental/questionable cyanrip rip flags* on the hardware run. I declined to auto-add them to the archival rip — I can't validate them here and a bad flag could taint the master. The enriched report already captures far more of what the *real* rip reveals; a safe read-only probe pass is the right follow-up once we pick which flags to trial.
  - **Lessons (graduated):** input/output validation is now a written, CI-enforced convention (was a silent gap); the "user's number is the ceiling" rule coexists with a tiny internal recovery cap for the no-`-Z`-set case; window-close is a distinct teardown path from Cancel/Force-stop and needs its own synchronous in-container kill. 1456 tests green, coverage 93%.

- **Post-0.4.8 (on branch, unreleased) — dynamic secure re-rip, dialog surfacing, ETA-trace causes (2026-07-01).** Live-testing follow-ups from the maintainer while ripping on v0.4.8:
  - **Dialog on the wrong monitor / behind windows (the "move to ~/Applications" prompt).** It *was* being centred (parented to the main window), but two gaps: no clamp to the visible screen, and it was never raised. `center_on_anchor` now clamps the dialog fully onto whichever screen the centred point lands on (pure `_clamp_to`, unit-tested) **and** `raise_()`s + `activateWindow()`s it so it comes to the front. Real-user 2-monitor report.
  - **ETA trace: cause + actual.** The maintainer asked to "timestamp estimations vs actual + the events that caused the jumps." Each `eta_trace` sample now carries the `track` + `activity` in effect (so a jump — e.g. a fast track 1 giving way to a slow, re-read-heavy track 2 — ties to its cause), and at report time each sample is backfilled with `actual_remaining_seconds` (real finish − sample time) via a pure `_eta_trace_block()`. Direct predicted-vs-actual record.
  - **Dynamic secure re-rip (headline of this batch; maintainer chose opt-in default-off).** Root cause of the "20 min → 1 h" ETA: the maintainer's `-Z 2` re-reads *every* track ≥2× (clean or not) — ~2.8× real-time. A track that matches AccurateRip on the first read is already proven, so re-reading it is wasted. New opt-in "Only when needed" checkbox: pass 1 rips **fast** (`-Z 0`), then secure-re-rips **only** the AccurateRip-failing tracks (via the v0.4.8 `-l` per-track path), at the configured `-Z`. Clean disc → one fast pass; marginal tracks still fully secured. Plumbing: config `secure_rerip_dynamic` (schema v5→v6, defaults-fill); `read_speed_ladder.tracks_failing_accuraterip()` (proven = v1/v2 or offset-variant match); worker `dynamic_secure` gate + generalized `_auto_fix_tracks(tracks, rerip_z, trigger)` (was `_auto_fix_unstable_tracks`); Settings checkbox (enabled only when `-Z > 0`, a11y + tooltip); `retried_tracks[].trigger`. All on the branch, **no release** (maintainer: "no new release until I upload final logs").
  - **Lessons:** the dynamic-vs-always `-Z` distinction is the same "fast, careful only when needed" spine as the read-speed ladder — AccurateRip match *is* the proof, so trust it and secure only the rest. Still hardware-gated: the per-track re-rip-and-swap (and now dynamic's reliance on it) hasn't run on a real drive; the maintainer's in-flight v0.4.8 rip is the first test.

- **0.4.8 — per-track auto-fix: re-rip the unstable track alone, keep the better read (2026-07-01).** The maintainer's call after the gate research: "do it, and auto-fix." Wired cyanrip's `-l` (confirmed subset-rip) into the ladder:
  - **Behavior.** When a pass reads clean overall but a track's `-Z` secure re-read never *converged* (instability), Platterpus now re-rips **just that track** (`-l N`) with a harder `-Z` into a throwaway temp dir. If it now converges, the improved FLAC replaces the original (matched by the re-rip log's per-track filename); if not, the original is kept and the track stays flagged. Cheap (one track, no `-S`, so it works on a speed-locked drive) and **safe by construction** — a non-converged read is only ever replaced by a converged one, and any failure/uncertainty leaves the originals untouched. Supersedes 0.4.7's "flag, don't re-rip" for instability.
  - **Why temp-dir + swap (not in-place):** cyanrip's log model is whole-disc — an `-l 3` run would clobber the album's `<Album>.log`/`.cue` with a track-3-only version. Re-ripping into a temp dir keeps the album's whole-disc archival log/cue intact; the JSON (`read_speed.retried_tracks`, schema v5) is the authoritative record of what the auto-fix did, consistent with the maintainer's "JSON is the single source of truth" logging model.
  - **Plumbing.** `RipBackend.rip(only_tracks=…)` → cyanrip `-l`; `_rip_once(output_dir=…, only_tracks=…)` re-usable for the temp re-rip; `_auto_fix_unstable_tracks()` orchestrates re-rip → parse → conditional swap; `clean` decoupled from instability (so a rescued track isn't still marked `unresolved`); results pane reports fixed (win) vs still-unstable (caveat). Report schema v4→v5 (`retried_tracks`). 1362 tests green, coverage 93.0%.
  - **Remaining hardware gate (flagged honestly):** the re-rip-and-swap path itself hasn't run on a real drive — safe by construction, but validate on the Bazzite + BDR-209D rig. Gate 3 (subset re-rip) is otherwise closed; gate 1's `-S` abort is fixed; only "does a *changeable* drive actually read slower" is untestable here.
  - **Doc fix:** the `## [0.4.7]` CHANGELOG heading had been accidentally clobbered by the prior `-S`-abort commit (its content merged into `[Unreleased]`); restored, and 0.4.8 content separated out.

- **Post-0.4.7 (on branch, unreleased) — research closed a hardware gate and exposed a latent `-S`-abort bug (2026-07-01).** Follow-up to the user's question "will the next batch of logs answer all the hardware-gated questions?". Ran a small research workflow (parallel readers over cyanrip source/docs + Linux drive-speed, with an adversarial verify pass on the key claim). Two findings:
  - **Gate 3 (subset re-rip) — ANSWERED without hardware: YES.** cyanrip supports `-l <comma-list>` (e.g. `-l 3,5`) to rip only selected tracks — source-verified twice (`rip_indices[]` gates which tracks call `cyanrip_rip_track()`; distinct from `-t` tag metadata). This was never a logs question — it's a capability lookup. Makes per-track re-rip cheap and speed-free.
  - **Gate 1 (`-S`) — the design assumption was WRONG, and it was a latent bug.** We assumed a speed-locked drive would *ignore* `-S` (graceful degrade). Source review: cyanrip **aborts the rip** ("Device does not support changing speeds!" → `EINVAL`) if `-S` is given and the drive lacks `CDIO_DRIVE_CAP_MISC_SELECT_SPEED`. The BDR-209D logs `Speed: default (unchangeable)` — so an error-triggered escalation would have crashed the re-rip. Latent (never hit — no disc on the rig has triggered *error*-based escalation yet), but real.
  - **Fix (this commit, on branch):** parse cyanrip's `Speed:` banner → `RippingInfo.speed_changeable`; `next_step(speed_locked=True)` skips the speed rungs and escalates `-Z` only; the worker sets the lock from pass 1's log (always max/no `-S`), so `-S` is never sent to an unchangeable drive. Speed-changeable drives unchanged. +3 regression tests (parser banner, ladder speed-lock, worker escalates-Z-never-`-S`). 1355 green.
  - **Held for the maintainer:** whether to also adopt `-l` per-track re-rip (which would reopen the just-made "flag, don't auto-re-rip" instability decision — a per-track re-rip is cheap enough that auto-fixing a flagged track becomes attractive), and release timing (v0.4.8). Not done unilaterally — it redefines the headline feature.
  - **Lessons (graduated to strategy §8.1):** gate 1 assumption corrected + gate 3 answered. Meta: a research pass over the *tool's own source* (not just rip logs) closed a gate that "collect more logs" never could — the right instrument depends on the question (capability → source; drive behavior → hardware; parser correctness → real logs).

- **0.4.7 — real-hardware read-stability finding: flag, don't auto-re-rip (2026-07-01).** The maintainer ran a full disc on 0.4.6 (The Police, *Every Breath You Take: The Classics*, BDR-209D) and uploaded the four artifacts. Analyzing them surfaced a real gap in the **headline** read-speed ladder that a green suite never would:
  - **The bug.** cyanrip reported `Ripping errors: 0` (whole-disc) yet track 3's `-Z` secure re-read never converged — 5 reads, no two agreed (`Done; (no matches found, but hit repeat limit of 5)`) — genuine read instability. The ladder's `clean`/`unresolved` decision keys on that whole-disc error count, so `.platterpus.json` claimed the rip was clean. This is exactly the hardware-gated unknown the ladder's own docstring flagged ("we trigger on the whole-disc count"). **Real hardware answered it: that signal is insufficient.**
  - **The subtlety (track 3 vs track 5).** Track 5 was ALSO "partially accurate" — but it *converged* (stable read) and matched only an offset-variant pressing. That's a **pressing difference, not instability** → re-reading can't change it. So "partially accurate" is NOT a read-error signal; the reliable per-track tell is `-Z` **convergence**. A naive fix keying on partial-accuracy would wrongly re-rip track 5 forever.
  - **The fix.** Parse cyanrip's per-track `Done; (…)` verdict into `TrackResult.secure_rerip_converged`; `read_speed_ladder.unstable_tracks()` lists the non-converged tracks. A pass with an unstable track is now honestly marked not-clean / `unresolved`, with the track named in `read_speed.unstable_tracks` and a loud results-pane caveat. **Escalation is unchanged** — still gated only on unrecoverable errors.
  - **The policy call (maintainer, via AskUserQuestion): "flag it, don't auto re-rip."** A whole-disc re-rip to retry one track costs hours (this album = 2h49m/pass) with no guarantee a scratch resolves, and subset re-rip is still hardware-gated (strategy §8.1 check 3). So instability is surfaced, not auto-escalated; the user cleans the disc and re-rips manually if they want a verified copy.
  - Report schema v3→**v4** (also formalizes the `eta_trace` added earlier this branch). Regression tests: parser convergence (incl. the track-3-vs-5 distinction), `unstable_tracks`, worker "flag-not-re-rip", report serialization, UI caveat. 1352 tests green, coverage 93.0%.
  - Also folded in this release: the 3 earlier post-0.4.6 fixes (value-based relaunch env-scrub; one smoothed ETA with cyanrip's stripped from the log; the labeled `eta_trace`).
  - **Lessons (graduated):** the two-signals distinction (unrecoverable error = trigger; instability = flag) → `read_speed_ladder.py` docstring + strategy §8.1 (check 2 now ANSWERED). Meta: this is the *third* time a single real-hardware rip found a defect the suite couldn't — "real hardware is ground truth" keeps earning its place in CLAUDE.md.

- **0.4.6 — adaptive read-speed ladder + derived-file verification, plus polish (2026-07-01).** Post-0.4.5 continuation session. Built five work items and a focused set of self-improvement iterations, all test-green (1330+ tests, coverage ~92.9%, ≥ the 91% gate):
  - **Headline — adaptive read-speed ladder.** Rips start at the drive's max speed and only slow down / re-read harder when a disc *actually* reads with errors (quality only goes up). New pure `read_speed_ladder.py` policy engine (never-raises, hypothesis-tested): step `max→8×→4×→2×` (cyanrip `-S`), then escalate `-Z` at the floor, then stop and **FLAG** an unresolved disc; bounded by `MAX_ATTEMPTS`. The escalation loop lives in `rip_worker` (re-rips the whole disc on a completed pass with read errors — subset re-rip is a future optimisation), records a per-pass `SpeedAttempt` history folded into the report (`read_speed`) and surfaced in the results pane. `-S` plumbing added to the `RipBackend` ABC + cyanrip backend; Config `read_speed_mode`/`read_speed` (schema v4→v5); goal presets all use the ladder; Settings adds an advanced fixed-speed control. **Three pieces are HARDWARE-GATED (flag for the BDR-209D rig): does the drive honour `-S`; is cyanrip's per-track read-error signal reliable; can a track subset be re-ripped.** Graceful fallback if `-S` is ignored → no regression. See `docs/cyanrip-fork.md Part A §8.1`.
  - **Derived-file verification (Task #19).** After the FLAC master (already AccurateRip+CTDB+`flac -t` verified), the derived MP3/WavPack/WAV are now proven too — honestly per format: WAV/WavPack get a **bit-identity** PCM byte-compare against the master; MP3 (lossy) gets **decode-clean + complete** and the report says explicitly it's NOT bit-identity. New `adapters/derived_verify.py` (never-raises + hypothesis) + `workers/derived_verify_worker.py`; routes through the ffmpeg that already did the transcode → **no new dependency** (Critical Rule #6). Report schema v2→v3 (`verification.derived`).
  - **`%Y` year-only naming token.** cyanrip's `%y` is the full date; added a Platterpus-only `%Y` pre-expanded to the 4-digit year in `scheme_from_template` before cyanrip sees it (so `%%Y` stays literal). Year presets switched to `%Y`; migration v3→v4.
  - **Polish:** rip-report writes debounced (was ~5×/rip → coalesced, flush on close) and made **atomic** (temp+rename, crash-safe — it.12); results pane surfaces album loudness + partial-match count and the read-speed outcome; `safe_path_segment` hardened across locales (NUL/control strip, `.`/`..` refusal, 255-byte NAME_MAX cap — it.11); `docs/eac-parity.md` refreshed whipper→cyanrip.
  - **Logging consolidated (maintainer's mid-session call).** Dropped the standalone per-album `.platterpus.log` sidecar. The album folder now holds only: audio, cover, cyanrip's EAC `<Album>.log`/`.cue` (for humans), and `<Album>.platterpus.json` — the single machine/LLM debug artifact, which now embeds this rip's full session log (`debug.lines`, cap raised to 10k). The global `~/.local/share/platterpus/log.txt` stays *outside* the album folder as the cross-session catch-all for program-level failures. (This reversed the task brief's "keep both logs" note — explicit user direction wins.) Documented in architecture.md §3.7.
  - **Self-review caught 3 real bugs in this cycle's own code** (adversarial verify workflow): the ladder marked a hard-failed rip `clean=True` (short-circuit on `success`) → `unresolved` read wrong; `derived_verify` killed the decode subprocess on timeout/read-error without reaping it (zombie leak). All three fixed with regression tests in the same cycle. **Lesson:** run the adversarial self-review over new code *before* declaring done — a green suite missed all three.
  - **Lessons (graduated):** the logging model → architecture.md §3.7; the read-speed ladder + its three hardware gates → cyanrip-fork.md Part A §8.1; both are institutional now, not just log entries. Meta: the derived-lossless proof is a PCM byte-compare (definitive) rather than trusting a format's own checksum — the same "prove it, don't assert it" bar as the FLAC master.

- **0.4.5 (in progress) — real-rip-driven fixes on branch `claude/verification-suite` (2026-07-01).** The maintainer ran a full 14-track disc (The Police, *Every Breath You Take: The Classics*) on the Bazzite + BDR-209D rig and uploaded the `.log` / `.cue` / `.platterpus.json` / `log.txt`. Analyzing the *real* artifacts (plus two subagent workups) surfaced defects a green test suite never would:
  - **CTDB never worked.** We sent `toc=` with the 150-sector lead-in included; `lookup2.php` wants offsets from 0, so every lookup 404'd (surfacing as "timed out" on the user's net). The XML parser also read `crc`/`hasParity` on a non-namespaced tag when the live server returns a namespaced doc with `crc32`/`hasparity`. Fixed both (verified against the live server: `0:…` TOC returns a real match), added retry+typed errors. **Graduated:** KDD-16's "confirm on hardware" caveat is now resolved for the TOC/endpoint. The `toc_string()` lead-in subtraction is the fix; regression test pins offsets start at 0.
  - **822h time estimate.** We captured cyanrip's *first* ETA tick (at 0.01% → nonsense). Replaced with a self-computed album ETA (elapsed ÷ fraction) + a realtime multiplier in the report; stop echoing cyanrip's per-op ETA; label read vs encode phase. **Lesson (graduated to `rip_timing` docstring):** never trust a ripper's early ETA; compute your own from observed rate.
  - **Verdict under-claimed** offset-variant (Accurip 450) partial matches — now surfaced honestly (stays amber, never claims bit-perfect).
  - **Parser dropped** the drive model (cyanrip prints `Device model:`, not `Drive used:`), all loudness/ReplayGain, and cyanrip's `Log FUN512` log signature — all now captured in the JSON.
  - **Debug-log noise:** ~40 `musicbrainzngs` INFO lines/rip silenced; a readable per-rip `<Album>.platterpus.log` now lives in the album folder (session-scoped, other albums filtered — same rule as the JSON embed). Global `log.txt` kept as the no-rip/cross-session catch-all (it spanned multiple launches in the upload — proof it's not redundant).
  - **Meta-lesson (reinforces CLAUDE.md "real hardware is ground truth"):** one real rip found ~6 defects unit tests couldn't. Each fix shipped with a regression test built from the real log.

- **0.4.4 — freeze fix + a 10-iteration self-improvement run on UX/testing (2026-06-30).** Started from two real-user 0.4.2 reports: (1) the window froze while installing an optional dependency, and (2) a first-run modal that "seemed frozen and looked bad" plus a "0 dependencies, then it gave me this option" prompt. Root causes and fixes:
  - **Freeze #1 (the dependency install):** the Picard Flatpak install ran on the GUI thread inside a modal dialog. Moved it to a worker thread (`_InstallWorker`), gated the dialog's close (incl. the title-bar ✕) during install, widened it. **This was the 3rd occurrence of the never-block-the-GUI-thread bug** → graduated to a CLAUDE.md Critical-rule note + an architecture.md "dialogs must not block" pattern + a runtime/AST guard sweep (testing.md rule #10).
  - **Freeze #2 (the off-monitor modal):** on multi-monitor the modal opened on a *different* screen; being application-modal it correctly refused input on the main screen, so the app *looked* frozen. New `CenteredDialog` base centres every modal on its parent window on first show (no-op under native Wayland). (it.6)
  - **Accessibility pass (gap #4, it.7):** accessible names on the album metadata fields + disc-info values; standard keyboard shortcuts on Quit/Settings/User Guide. The status/verdict surfaces already named themselves and never signal trust by colour alone.
  - **Optional-deps prompt (it.8):** the "0 missing" summary immediately followed by "install optional?" read as a contradiction. When everything required is present, collapse to one outcome-first dialog ("✓ Everything required is installed…") that lists each extra with *what it does for you*.
  - **Disabled-Start affordance (it.9):** Start now carries a state-aware tooltip explaining *why* it's greyed out (no drive / disc not identified) and what a click does once ready — a greyed control with no explanation reads as broken.
  - **Testing infra (it.1–5):** debug-log embedding cap, worker-thread naming + install logging, centralized bounded-pump helper, robust thread-leak net, hardened GUI-thread AST guards, and a mitigation for the PySide interpreter-shutdown abort (CI-flake guard). Coverage holds at 93% (gate 91, ratchets up).
  - Docs: ux-design-principles statuses refreshed (gap #2 JSON log ✅, gap #4 accessibility 🟡); stale "Whipper GUI" headers in CHANGELOG + this log corrected.

- **whipper removed entirely — cyanrip is the sole ripping backend (2026-06-30, branch `claude/ui-polish-experimental-resize`).** The maintainer asked: what's missing from cyanrip for EAC parity, do we need to fork it / mine its branches, and remove whipper if there's no reason for it. **Research first:** compared cyanrip vs EAC (Hydrogenaudio ripper table + cyanrip README/releases + the two impls). Findings — cyanrip needs **nothing structural** for parity: it already hits AccurateRip confidence 200 / bit-perfect on the BDR-209D, and the only EAC↔cyanrip differences (FUA cache-defeat, C2 pointers) are *method* not *correctness* (cdparanoia reaches bit-perfection by re-read consensus, which `-Z` leverages). The remaining 14/14 gap is runtime (`-Z 2`, already shipped) + a physical disc defect EAC also fails. cyanrip's only non-master branches are stale 2023 experiments (one already merged), so **no fork / no branch-mining is warranted** (KDD-18 §5 gates not met). whipper's only edges (cdrdao gap detection, --keep-going, CD-R safety) are niche and feature-not-drive-dependent; its one drive-dependent factor (the >587 offset bug) only ever favours cyanrip. **Verdict: no functional reason to keep whipper → removed it** (maintainer chose "remove completely", incl. amending the locked rules). **Execution (mapped the full surface with an Explore agent first):** moved the `RipBackend` ABC + `RipHandle`/`RipMetadata`/`TrackTag`/`run_capture` into a neutral `adapters/rip_backend.py`, renamed `WhipperError`→`RipError`, deleted `whipper_backend.py` + `WhipperHostExportedImpl`; composition/preflight always build cyanrip; dropped `Config.ripper_backend`/`whipper_path` + the whipper-only rip flags (`continue_on_cdr`/`force_overread`/`keep_going`); Settings lost the backend dropdown + per-backend gating (re-compress now permanently disabled — cyanrip maxes compression); host_setup + registry are cyanrip-centric (`check_cyanrip` via `-V`, COPR install always-on, exports cyanrip/metaflac/flac); `setup-host.sh`/`install.sh` match. **Offset wrinkle fixed:** cyanrip reads no config file, so the drive-setup wizard now persists the detected offset to the GUI's own config (applied as `-s`); `whipper.conf` is kept read-only as a legacy reference (offset_config.py still reads it for the trust display). **Docs:** amended locked Critical Rule #3 (routing is now `~/.local/bin/cyanrip`; pattern unchanged), Rules #1/#5, the Stack note, KDD-18 (amendment block), DEPENDENCIES (struck-through whipper row), README top/how-it-works/manual-Step-3, and the user guide; surfaced the **logo** in the About dialog header (`app_icon.logo_pixmap`). Test suite stayed green and ≥91% throughout the large test rewrite (final 93.02%), ruff clean; three commits, pushed. **Known follow-up (flagged, not done):** the README's deep *from-scratch manual* section (Step 4 export / Step 5 `whipper offset find` / `whipper.conf` hand-edit) still has whipper wording — the supported paths (one-liner / wizard) are correct, so this is advanced-doc polish, not a functional gap. **LESSONS:** (1) "is tool A better than tool B everywhere, or is it drive-dependent?" is the right question to force before defaulting/removing — here the one drive-dependent factor was *monotonic* (only ever favoured cyanrip), which collapses "auto-select per drive" into "just remove the loser." (2) Removing a backend named into a *locked* rule needs the rule amended in the same change, not silently contradicted — and the offset *store* (whipper.conf) is calibration data, a separate concern from the *backend*, so it can stay even when the tool that named it is gone. (3) Map the full reference surface (Explore agent) before a wide deletion: the ABC/shared-types living *inside* the deleted module was the trap that a naive `rm` would have sprung.
- **Self-contained rip report + output buttons, released as 0.4.1 then 0.4.2 (2026-06-30, branch `claude/ui-polish-experimental-resize` → `main`).** After the timing/enrichment/audit work below shipped as **0.4.1** (folding in the never-tagged 0.4.1a1 alpha — the CTDB http fix etc.), the maintainer upgraded on real hardware (0.4.0→0.4.1 update worked; the Plasma-6 repaint fix held) and asked a design question that drove this batch: the three output files. **Decision (maintainer):** `<Album>.log` = EAC-parity human archival (stays beside the FLACs); the `.platterpus.json` + the app `log.txt` are both "debug this app" artifacts and should be combined "if possible." **What shipped:** the `.platterpus.json` (kept in the album folder, per the maintainer's override) became a *self-contained* debug record — it now embeds the session log (everything since this launch) **minus the time-windows of other albums' rips**, so each album's report has the full environmental picture without other rips' noise. New `log_buffer.SessionLogBuffer` (in-memory logging handler installed by `logging_setup` alongside the file handler, same format/level, re-leveled by the debug toggle, 50k-line cap with truncation flag); `main_window` tracks per-rip epoch windows and keeps the *current* rip's own window out of the exclusion set (so a report never filters its own lines), recomputed on each write so the CTDB re-write captures later lines; `rip_report` gained `build_debug_log` + a `debug` section (sorts last, bulky). Plus two post-rip buttons — **"View report"** (opens the JSON) and **"Open rip folder"** — next to "View log", all three greyed until a rip finishes. **The `log.txt`-elimination question, answered with a reason (not just kept):** `log.txt` stays as the always-on persistent safety net, because the JSON only exists *after a completed rip* — a startup crash, a failed setup, or a rip that dies before writing its JSON would otherwise leave zero debug trail, and that's exactly when you most need one. So: for a rip, one file (the JSON); for "broke before a rip," `log.txt`. Cut **0.4.2** for it. Also fixed two inline whipper-vestige comments the audit's docstring-sweep missed in `rip_progress`. Gate green throughout (93.05%), ruff clean. **LESSONS:** (1) "combine the files" has a hard floor — a per-event persistent log can't be replaced by a per-*rip* artifact, because the artifact doesn't exist for the failure modes that occur before/without a rip; embed for convenience, but keep the always-on log as the safety net. (2) When excluding "other rips" from an embedded log, identify the *current* rip's window by object identity and exclude only the others — appending the current window to the same list it's filtered against (without the identity guard) would make a report filter out its own lines.
- **Rip-timing record + richer cyanrip-log surfacing + a full post-whipper-removal audit (2026-06-30, branch `claude/ui-polish-experimental-resize`).** Triggered by a real `-Z 2` rip the maintainer uploaded (Police "Classics", 14 tracks): logs + cue + `.platterpus.json` + app `log.txt`. Three threads. **(1) Actual elapsed vs estimate.** The rip took **2h45m** wall-clock while cyanrip's ETA sat at "~35m" — its ETA is computed from the current read pass only and is blind to `-Z` secure re-reads. cyanrip's log records the disc's *audio* length ("Total time") and a finish timestamp but **never its own run time**, so only the GUI can measure it. New pure never-raises `rip_timing.py` (`parse_eta_to_seconds` for cyanrip's "3m"/"1h2m" form, `format_duration`); `RipWorker` captures the *first* ETA as a whole-run estimate (`estimated_seconds`); `main_window_rip` stamps the start at the user-perceived Start (so an auto-heal retry is included), logs `rip elapsed (actual): … — cyanrip's ETA estimate was …` at finish, and threads a `timing` block (elapsed/started/finished + estimate) into the `.platterpus.json` (CTDB re-write preserves it). **(2) Richer cyanrip-log fields.** The parser was dropping exactly the detail the parity analysis read by hand: per-track **rip count** ("after N rips"), the **+450-frame offset variant** match ("Accurip 450" → `TrackResult.accuraterip_offset`, sentinel version 450, *recorded as data, NOT folded into the verified rule* so the verdict can't over-claim a partial as exact), the **"Tracks ripped partially accurately"** summary, the disc **audio duration**, and the **Paranoia status counts** (READ/VERIFY/FIXUP_ATOM/OVERLAP). All surfaced in the JSON; the status-line fidelity summary now notes partially-accurate tracks so a "12/14 verified" reads as a pressing-offset quirk, not a bad rip. The Paranoia block parser closes cleanly so it can't swallow the finish-report lines after it. **(3) Full code-vs-docs-vs-intent audit** (maintainer: "nothing vestigial, all options/buttons work, fix all, document patterns"). Ran 4 read-only audit agents (backend / UI / deps-config-paths / docs) + 2 editing agents (peripheral docstrings; the README rewrite). Findings: **0 dead controls** (every Settings option/menu action/button is wired and consumed; the FLAC re-compress toggle is intentionally disabled). Fixed false *current-behaviour* claims: a setup-dialog success message ("whipper is installed"), `composition.py`/`app.py`/`adapters/__init__.py`/`registry.py` docstrings asserting a removed `Config.ripper_backend`/`whipper_backend` module, a stale `.desktop` `whipper` keyword, ~25 README spots (the **manual-install offset section still told users to run `whipper offset find` and hand-edit `whipper.conf`** — fully dead; rewrote to the GUI drive-setup-wizard flow + "cyanrip uses no config file"), and a user-facing `failure_hint` carrying a **false ">587 cd-paranoia bug" claim *and* a reference to the removed "Keep going" setting**. Also stale tests from the whipper-removal commits (setup-host.sh still asserted whipper install lines). `WHIPPER_BINARY_DEFAULT` confirmed *not* vestigial (host_teardown uses it to clean old installs). Gate green throughout (93.13%), ruff clean. **LESSONS:** (1) **comment hygiene after a backend swap** — removing a backend leaves dozens of docstrings claiming "whipper does X" as *current* behaviour, which is a doc bug for the next reader; the convention (now in architecture.md) is comments describe the *current* backend and the old tool appears only as accurate history, and a swap must grep the whole tree and re-audit every hit. (2) A self-reported metric the user can't otherwise see (actual elapsed) belongs in the durable record when the tool's own estimate is unreliable — measure it where you can (the GUI), don't trust the subprocess's ETA. (3) The richest failure evidence was already in cyanrip's log text (rip counts, offset-variant, paranoia counts) — surfacing what the tool *already emits* beat inventing new instrumentation.
- **Unified dependency dialog + cyanrip as the default backend (2026-06-29, branch `claude/ui-polish-experimental-resize`).** Two maintainer asks. **(1) One dependency popup, not several (items 2+6).** A fresh install used to open a separate dialog per missing piece — whipper *and* metaflac each fired their own `ManualInstallDialog` (both offering the same setup wizard) = the "two popups" the maintainer hit. New `DependencyMixin._resolve_missing_unified` collects every *installable* missing dep into ONE `PendingInstallsDialog` (checkbox per row, ticked by default), drives the installs inline with per-row progress, and — per the maintainer's "grey out close until install completes" — `set_install_phase_active` now also disables Cancel, so the only dismiss path (Close, via `show_close_button`) appears after the loop. `_make_unified_install_one` dispatches by *registry metadata*, reusing existing install machinery (Rule #6, no new install code): `from_setup_wizard` tools (whipper/cyanrip/flac/metaflac) open the one container wizard — guarded by a `wizard_ran` flag so it opens **at most once** no matter how many such rows are ticked, then re-probes each to mark its result; `install_command` deps (Picard) install via `AutoInstaller`; anything neither (a broken bundled package) falls back to the per-item manual dialog. Routed both the launch-time check (`_apply_dependency_report`) and the optional-install offer through it. The setup wizard now installs **both** backends unconditionally (`_build_host_setup` → `include_cyanrip=True`), so switching backends in Settings never needs a re-run. **(2) cyanrip is now the default backend** (`Config.ripper_backend` `"whipper"`→`"cyanrip"`). Researched the maintainer's exact question — is the better backend drive-dependent, or is cyanrip just better everywhere? Verdict from `docs/cyanrip-fork.md` + the two impls: cyanrip wins on every axis that matters (active 2024 vs whipper's stalled 2021, no >587 read-offset bug — the only *drive*-dependent factor, and it only ever favours cyanrip — max FLAC compression, and it's the backend behind `-Z` "re-rip until reads match"); whipper's only edges are niche EAC-parity knobs (cdrdao gap/pregap detection, `--keep-going`, `--cdr` safety) that are *feature*-dependent, not drive-dependent. So the default is **universal, not auto-selected per drive** — there's no drive on which whipper rips better, making per-drive switching pure complexity for zero benefit. Updated the Settings combo/labels/tooltip, help, and the four tests that assumed the implicit whipper default (made each explicit). Gate green throughout (93.04%), ruff clean; one commit, pushed. **LESSONS:** (1) when consolidating N per-tier resolver popups into one dialog, keep the *classification* in the subsystem (registry tiers / `from_setup_wizard` / `install_command`) and only move the *presentation* — the unified dialog reuses `AutoInstaller` + the wizard, so Rule #6's "one install path" holds. (2) A heavy idempotent installer invoked from a per-item loop needs a run-once guard in the closure (`wizard_ran`) + a re-probe to attribute success per row — don't run the container wizard once per tool it installs. (3) "Make the better thing the default" deserves the *drive-dependent vs universal* question asked explicitly: here the one drive-dependent factor was monotonic (only ever favours cyanrip), which collapses "auto-select per drive" into "just default to it."
- **Icons committed, `WhipperBackend`→`RipBackend` rename, and UX gaps #5 + #6 shipped (2026-06-29, branch `claude/platterpus-icons-ux-backlog-iph0gn`).** Three maintainer asks, in order. **(1) Icons.** Installed `librsvg2-bin` and ran `build/make_icon.py` to rasterize `assets/platterpus-logo.svg` → the 512px `build/python-appimage/io.github.rmccann_hub.Platterpus.png` (bundled by the AppImage) + `assets/icons/*` (16–512px + favicon). These were missing on `main` because they couldn't be pushed from the renamed-mid-session sandbox; verified the render visually (a platypus whose body is a CD). **(2) `WhipperBackend` ABC → `RipBackend` — DECISION: yes, renamed.** The ABC is implemented by *both* `WhipperHostExportedImpl` and `CyanripImpl`, so naming it after one of its two implementations was misleading legacy (the open judgment call from the rename session). Whole-token rename across the backend layer, workers, UI, composition, preflight, tests + the live design docs; **deliberately untouched** (the sacred distinction): `WhipperHostExportedImpl`, `WhipperError`, the `whipper_backend.py` module name, `whipper.conf`, `~/.local/bin/whipper`. Dated history (`session-log.md`, `docs/archive/**`, completed-task notes in `TASKS.md`) left as written — the class genuinely *was* `WhipperBackend` then. **(3) UX backlog (TASKS item 15).** **Gap #5 (outcome-first wording):** the goal-presets/format work already covered most labels; reworded the two genuine jargon-first surfaces — the overread toggle ("Read past the last track to catch any final samples (overread)" under a "Disc lead-out" label) and the drive-setup cache verdict ("this drive caches audio, so Platterpus will read around the cache…" / "doesn't cache audio, so its reads are already trustworthy") — effect-first per principle 4. **Gap #6 (drive profiles):** ran a 3-lens design panel + synthesis Workflow first (the design had a real fork: record-only vs. make-it-authoritative). **Verdict + KDD-23: built the record/display/guard ledger, NOT a second offset authority** — `whipper.conf` + the `--offset` override stay the only authorities; per-drive offset *application* is deferred as hardware-gated (it changes the offset a rip uses → needs a two-drive rig to prove). New pure subsystem `drive_profiles.py` (StrEnum `OffsetSource`/`Confidence`, `OffsetRecord`/`DriveProfile`, `compute_fingerprint` with `wwn:`→`sn:`→`vm:` tiers and firmware **excluded** from the key, `read_drive_identity` sysfs seam, `find_fingerprint_collisions`, `should_replace_offset` upgrade rule, `evaluate_drive_state` guard) + `drive_profile_store.py` (JSON, never-raises load, atomic save, `schema_version`/`_migrate`, path resolved live so tests sandbox it). Refactored a shared `canonical_token`/`normalize_combined` out of `accuraterip_offsets` so the fingerprint key, the AR lookup key, and whipper.conf's section name can't drift. Wired one recorder (`DriveMixin._record_drive_fact`) into the three discovery points (AccurateRip auto-apply, manual save, a new wizard `detection_recorded` signal) + a disc-panel "Read offset" **trust line** (provenance + confidence, with ⚠/ⓘ guard warnings — text never colour alone) refreshed on drive change. An autouse conftest fixture redirects `DRIVE_PROFILES_PATH` to a tmp file so the suite never writes the real config. Five test-green commits (icons / rename / #5 / #6-core / #6-wiring), full suite green throughout (92.8%→93.07%), ruff clean. **Gap #3 (timestamp-localized anomalies + playback) assessed and left for hardware:** whipper's `.log` is track-level (no per-position C2/re-read data), so a position-level parser can't be written or validated without real anomaly-bearing rip output, and playback needs a real FLAC + a CC0 sample — genuinely hardware-gated, flagged honestly rather than stubbed. **LESSONS:** (1) when a gap names a *correctness* bug ("silent wrong-offset rips") but the fix is hardware-gated, ship the *trust-visibility* layer (provenance + confidence + a loud guard) first — it delivers the gap's intent at zero blast radius on the bit-perfect value and lays the substrate for the deferred fix (→ KDD-23). (2) Renaming a backend-neutral ABC named after one implementation is safe when the name isn't a substring of the implementation's (`WhipperBackend` ⊄ `WhipperHostExportedImpl`/`WhipperError`), so a whole-token replace can't touch the sacred whipper strings — but split live-doc updates from dated-history rewrites (don't falsify the log). (3) A store that `save()`s internally needs its path resolved *live* (through the module, not a bound import) so an autouse fixture can sandbox it — bound default args can't be monkeypatched after import.
- **Project renamed to Platterpus + UX trust-research applied (2026-06-28, same session).** Two maintainer asks. **(1) Rename** `Whipper GUI` / `Whipper-GUI-Frontend---CD-Rip` → **Platterpus** on branch `rename/platterpus`, with the critical distinction held throughout: rename the PROJECT, never the `whipper` *backend tool*. Surgical insight that made it safe — the strings `whipper_gui` / `whipper-gui` / `Whipper GUI` / `WhipperGUI` / `Whipper-GUI-Frontend---CD-Rip` / `WHIPPER_GUI` are **always** the project (the backend never carries a `-gui`/`_gui`/` GUI` suffix), so an ordered `perl -pi` over the tracked text files (excluding `docs/session-log.md` + `docs/archive/**` as historical, and the data blob) renamed everything without touching `WhipperBackend`, `whipper_backend.py`, `~/.local/bin/whipper`, `whipper.conf`, `WHIPPER_CONFIG_PATH`, `output_reference/whipper_*`, or the real-whipper log fixture. `git mv src/whipper_gui src/platterpus` (84 files); config/cache dir → `~/.config/platterpus` with a one-time `migrate_legacy_config_dir()` that copies an existing `~/.config/whipper-gui`; **freedesktop app-id `io.github.rmccann_hub.Platterpus`** decoupled into `paths.APP_ID` and used for the `.desktop` filename + bundled-icon name + new AppStream metainfo (the uninstall helper/script keep the `platterpus` slug; teardown/install scripts aligned); new `assets/platterpus-logo.svg` shipped in-package and wired as the window icon (`app_icon.py`) + `.desktop` Icon, with `make_icon.py` rewritten to rasterize the SVG (no converter here → PNG generation flagged as manual). **No Flatpak manifest created** — the project rejected Flatpak (Critical Rule #3 / KDD-17); that's a distribution-model change for the maintainer. 207 files, ruff clean, full suite green at 92.7%; **not pushed** (per the brief) — GitHub repo-rename commands handed to the maintainer. The one judgment call flagged for confirmation: the `WhipperBackend` ABC name (kept; renaming touches the whole backend layer). **(2) UX deep-research applied** (three uploaded reports on *why EAC made bit-perfect ripping usable*) on branch `feat/ux-trust-improvements` (stacked on the rename). **(A)** distilled them into `docs/ux-design-principles.md` — the trust-first thesis ("the user's goal isn't to finish the rip, it's to know it can be trusted"), 10 principles with how Platterpus measures up, and a ranked 6-gap backlog (TASKS item 15). **(B)** implemented the top two gaps: **accessibility** (accessible names on every rip-screen status surface + the drive combo + track list; verdict now conveyed by symbol+text on all three levels — added `ⓘ` to neutral — never colour alone) and **goal presets** (`goal_presets.py` + a Settings "Goal" combo: *Fast verified* (= shipping defaults, recommended) / *Archival exact* (+CTDB +recompress) / *Portable* (MP3); picking one snaps the controls, editing a control flips to Custom — progressive disclosure, no behaviour change by default). Three more commits, all test-green (cov 92.8%). **LESSONS:** (1) for a rename with a homonym trap (project `whipper-gui` vs tool `whipper`), find the string pattern that is *unambiguously* one side and replace only that — never a blanket `whipper`→x. (2) Decouple the freedesktop app-id from the CLI/config slug: they have different rules (no hyphens in app-id components) and different jobs. (3) The research's #1 modern gap was *accessibility*, which the app had least of — and the verdict banner was already 90% there (the ✓/⚠ text), needing only the neutral symbol + accessible names to stop being colour-dependent.
- **EAC-parity follow-up, phase 2: audit-driven trust fixes + honest EAC-log renderer (2026-06-28, same session, after the maintainer said "you are getting stuck").** I had stalled into asking decision questions; the maintainer's nudge + ultracode meant *stop deferring, keep shipping reversible/honest work*. Ran a 5-agent read-only Workflow to map everything genuinely buildable now (no hardware, no maintainer decision) and find completeness gaps in the just-shipped items 1+2, then synthesized a worklist and executed it. **The audit found a REAL correctness bug:** `disc_info_panel._ar_track_matched()` decided "verified" by string-matching `"exact match"` with no confidence check, while the new verdict banner used `confidence >= 1`. Two consequences: (a) the two surfaces could disagree on the same screen, and (b) — because cyanrip writes `"accurately ripped, confidence N"` with **no** "exact match" substring — the disc panel showed "not in database" for a disc cyanrip had *fully verified* (cyanrip is the active backend on the rig). **Fix:** one shared rule in `parsers/rip_log.py` (`accuraterip_is_match` / `track_accuraterip_verified`, confidence ≥ 1, can only under-claim) now used by the banner, the disc panel, the status-line `fidelity_summary`, and the new EAC renderer — so no surface can disagree, and cyanrip matches count honestly. **Also shipped:** (1) a committed-baseline **parity regression guard** (`tests/test_parity.py` pins the real 12/14 cyanrip-vs-EAC state + the full 14-track EAC CRC vector — a no-hardware guard against parser drift/artifact corruption); (2) the **honest, unsigned, attributed EAC-layout log renderer** (`eac_log_export.py` + `scripts/render_eac_log.py`) — option 2 from the feasibility doc — which renders our real RipLog into EAC's layout with a conspicuous "generated by Whipper GUI / NOT a genuine EAC log / not signed" header+footer and **never** a fabricated checksum (forging EAC's signature is the no-go); (3) help_content "Understanding the results" section (green/amber/grey banner meaning + the confidence≥1 rule in plain language) and the missing Settings docs (`-Z N`, CTDB verify); (4) the supporting-docs currency batch (KDD-13 addendum, architecture.md backend-param recipe + Verification&parity subsection, TASKS item 14, test-plan Test 8 `-Z 2` convergence steps). Five test-green commits; gate stayed green throughout (cov ~92.8%, ruff clean). **LESSONS:** (1) "verified/trustworthy" must have **exactly one definition** in the codebase — duplicating it (a string check here, a confidence check there) silently diverges, and for the active backend it *over*-claimed nothing but *under*-claimed everything (showed a perfect cyanrip rip as unverified). Centralize trust predicates. (2) When stuck deferring to decisions, a read-only fan-out audit converts "what's even buildable?" hand-wringing into a concrete worklist — and it caught a real bug I'd have missed. (3) The honest version of a "forbidden" feature often ships fine: we can't sign an EAC log (forgery), but we *can* render an EAC-layout, clearly-attributed, unsigned log — same diffing value, zero dishonesty.
- **EAC-parity items 1+2 shipped (code) and the log-trust path investigated (2026-06-28, continuing the EAC-parity brief; audio parity = priority 1).** Picked up the `claude/whipper-eac-parity-al1sq6` branch after the prior session's investigation doc. The plan-confirmation `AskUserQuestion` failed (tool stream closed), so — given the brief already locks items 1/2/3 with audio parity first and the "momentum with safety" value — proceeded on safe, brief-endorsed defaults (plan as written; tracker-log path = *investigate first, no code*; hardware-gated work = build code-side now + flag it) and said so up front for the maintainer to redirect. **Item 1 (P2a) — cyanrip `-Z N` "re-rip until reads match" for marginal discs.** Verified the flag against cyanrip's README first (`-Z <int>`: "Rips tracks until their checksums match `<int>` number of times. For very damaged CDs.") rather than trusting the brief's shorthand. Threaded a new `config.secure_rerip_matches` (0=off, the default) → `RipParameters` → backend ABC `rip()` → cyanrip `_build_rip_argv` (emits `-Z N` only when >0); whipper accepts+ignores it (no equivalent flag). Settings gained a "Re-rip until reads match" spin box with `setSpecialValueText("Off")`, added to `_cyanrip_only` so it greys out (value kept) for whipper — same pattern as the verify/re-compress toggles. **Item 2 — at-a-glance verification verdict banner.** The results pane already had an AR table + a CTDB line + a `fidelity_summary` status line, but trust wasn't *at a glance*. Added a bold, colour-coded `_verdict_banner` above the AR table driven by a pure `accuraterip_verdict(rip_log)` → `(message, level)`: green "✓ Bit-perfect: all N tracks verified against AccurateRip (confidence X+)", amber "M of N verified", grey "no tracks matched… the per-track Copy CRCs still prove a secure read" (the CD-R case). Honesty rule baked into `_is_ar_match`: a match requires `confidence >= 1`, so a "not present"/confidence-0 track can never count as verified (the banner can only under-claim). Colour-coded the CTDB line the same way via `ctdb_verdict_level` (green only for a hardware-validated match; experimental stays amber). Both helpers are pure + never-raise + accept both whipper and cyanrip `RipLog` shapes. **Item 3 (investigate-only) — `docs/eac-parity.md`.** Researched the two deferred questions. **(A) Tracker-accepted EAC logs:** the checksum is reverse-engineered and public (`puddly/eac_logsigner`, MIT, Python; `OPSnet/eac_logchecker.py` — Rijndael-256, UTF-16-LE, XOR the cipher blocks), so we *could* sign an EAC-format log — but doing so attests EAC produced a rip cyanrip did = **forgery of provenance**, which gazelle treats as a bannable faked log and which collides head-on with the brief's "never fake a log/checksum." → **Recommend NOT forging**; trust the open AccurateRip+CTDB path, optionally emit an *unsigned, attributed* EAC-format log for humans; maintainer decides. **(B) In-app CTDB repair:** feasible on Linux (`Masterisk-F/ctdb-cli`, .NET 10; or CUETools/Mono) but a heavy runtime dep that emits a single untagged WAV and *rewrites the master* — and our own CTDB CRC isn't hardware-validated yet (KDD-16), so repair (unlike verify) can't fail safe → **defer**, ship `-Z N` as the lighter first line, document the manual workflow, gate integration behind sign-off + validation. Cross-linked from `docs/README.md` + `eac-parity.md` (P2a marked ✅ code-landed / ⚠ hardware-gated). Two test-green commits (cov 92.7%→92.8%, ruff clean): +2 cyanrip-argv tests, +2 settings round-trip/grey-out, +1 rip-controls flow, +1 worker mock kwarg; +~10 verdict-banner/CTDB-level tests. **Caveat — all hardware-gated:** `-Z N` builds the right argv and flows through the stack in tests, but whether `-Z 2` actually converges a Track-3-class near-miss to the AccurateRip consensus can only be proven on the BDR-209D with the real disc (re-rip with it on, re-run `scripts/eac_parity.py` vs the EAC baseline). **LESSON:** when an interactive confirmation can't reach the user, fall back to the *brief's own* stated priorities + the project's momentum value rather than stalling — state the assumptions explicitly and keep the reversible work moving. And the EAC-log question is a trap: "technically possible" (the signer is public, MIT, in our language) is not "legitimate" — a signed EAC log is an attestation about the *tool*, so signing cyanrip's output is forgery regardless of the audio being genuine; the honest lever for community trust is AccurateRip/CTDB, not a manufactured checksum.
- **THE WIN: first end-to-end archival-quality rip on the Bazzite/BDR-209D rig — plus the cyanrip colon-in-tag restore completing KDD-22 approach A (v0.3.10, 2026-06-27).** The maintainer let a cyanrip rip run uninterrupted on 0.3.8 and it produced a flawless result: all 14 FLACs full-size and decode-clean, **AccurateRip confidence 200** (12/14 exact, 2/2 partial-accurate via the 450 offset variant), 0 read errors, cover art embedded in all 14, correct tags. This finally settled the "0-byte files" scare: **cyanrip finalizes/writes the output only at the END of the whole disc** (the empty `.cue` mid-rip was the tell) — there was never a write bug; every prior run had been canceled/killed before the finish (including once on my bad "kill it" advice, owned and corrected). The diagnostic marathon (host `ls` → container `ls` → `find / -size +1M` → the cyanrip `.log`) was only conclusive because of the new Open-logs-folder button + the user's patience. **Shipped to complete the chosen "correct tags + clean folders" fix:** `cyanrip_backend.restore_substituted_colons(metaflac, flac_files)` reverses the `:`→`∶` substitution in the *written tags* post-rip (reads each FLAC, rewrites only tags containing `∶`, leaves genre/MBID/ISRC/art untouched), wired as step 0 of `_start_post_rip_processing` (before cover/transcode so derived formats inherit the fix). Gated by `_metadata_has_colon()` (reads the track table) so colon-free albums never spin up the post-rip thread for nothing — which also fixed two tests that asserted "no post-rip thread when cover art is off."
