The folder keeps cyanrip's own `∶` path-sanitization (a literal colon in a path is best avoided cross-tool). +4 tests (restore reverses/​no-ops, metadata-has-colon true/false); 1067→1071; gate green, 92.78%. Gave the maintainer a one-liner to fix the album tag on the already-ripped files immediately. **LESSON:** don't infer a write bug from a mid-operation snapshot — some rippers buffer/finalize at the end, so 0-byte outputs during the run are normal; the conclusive test is one *uninterrupted* completion (and never advise killing a process whose own log reports success). And the colon round-trip (substitute for the CLI, restore in the tags, leave the path sanitized) is the clean shape when a tool's arg parser can't carry a character its output can.
- **Black-window-during-rip is a Plasma 6 *Wayland* repaint bug → prefer XWayland (v0.3.9, 2026-06-27, BDR-209D rig).** The maintainer reported the black-out STILL happening on 0.3.8 — and the screenshot proved my 0.3.8 throttle was the wrong fix here: the progress was updating only ~once/3s (1.34→1.65→1.96%), nowhere near a flood, so the rate-limit never engaged. The real signature — the app is alive (progress ticks) but a window region covered then re-exposed stays black — is the classic Qt-on-Plasma-6-Wayland repaint failure (on Wayland the compositor re-shows the client's last-committed buffer; this Qt build mis-manages it during partial-damage updates). Fix: `app._prefer_xwayland_on_wayland()` sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=xcb;wayland` BEFORE QApplication on a Wayland session (XWayland repaints via X11 expose, which works) — a FALLBACK LIST so a missing xcb plugin drops back to native Wayland and can never block launch; respects an explicit user override; no-op off Wayland. Belt: a 500ms `QTimer` (`_repaint_timer`) forces a full-window `update()` while a rip runs (started/stopped with `set_rip_active`), so any stray black region self-heals even if the fallback lands on native Wayland. The maintainer (rightly) pushed back on being asked to test platform fixes, so I shipped the high-confidence fix + belt rather than a diagnostic round-trip. +5 tests (xwayland set/override/noop/WAYLAND_DISPLAY, repaint-belt idle/interval); 1062→1067; gate green, 92.86%. **Also resolved this session via the user's own logs (no code): the cyanrip rip is bit-perfect** — `Every Breath You Take.log` showed tracks AccurateRip-verified at confidence 200; the alarming "0-byte files" are cyanrip's normal mid-rip state (it writes/finalizes at the END; the empty `.cue` is the tell) — and I had wrongly told the user to KILL a working rip after misreading 0-byte as broken (owned it; corrected to "let it finish, never cancel"). Still unconfirmed: whether a *completed* cyanrip rip materializes the files on disk (every run so far was canceled/killed before the end) — the open test is one uninterrupted rip. **LESSON:** match the fix to the *evidence*, not the first plausible cause — a black window with progress still ticking at 1/3s is a Wayland *repaint* bug, not an event-loop flood (the throttle was a real improvement for a different case, but not this one). And for a Qt/Wayland repaint bug on the primary target, preferring XWayland with a fallback list is the reliable, launch-safe fix.
- **Four hardware-found bugs fixed in one pass: cyanrip cancel, repaint black-out, update relaunch, scan-after-install (v0.3.8, 2026-06-27, BDR-209D rig, mid-rip feedback).** The maintainer, ripping on cyanrip, reported a batch and said "work on these in the meantime." **(1) Cancel didn't stop cyanrip.** `drive_control`'s kill patterns matched only `whipper (cd|…)` (-f) and the readers `cdparanoia|cd-paranoia|cdrdao` (by name) — cyanrip is neither (it's its own libcdio reader), so cancelling a cyanrip rip killed only the host wrapper and the in-container cyanrip kept ripping. Added `cyanrip` to the by-name kill set (kept it in the same pkill arglist so the call-count tests hold). **(2) UI blacked out when another window was dragged over it during a rip.** cyanrip redraws progress many times/sec (each `\r`→`\n` is its own line), and `RipWorker` forwarded EVERY redraw to the log pane (`appendPlainText` = text layout + repaint per line) → event-loop saturation → repaint starvation. Rate-limited the *log-pane* forwarding of progress redraws to ~10/s (`_PROGRESS_MIN_INTERVAL_S=0.1`, `time.monotonic`); crucially left the progress-bar/status/track signals UNthrottled (cheap) so the bar still moves smoothly AND the existing exact-sequence progress tests still pass — throttling those would have broken them. Non-progress lines (errors, markers) never throttled. (The log widget was already capped at 10k blocks.) **(3) "It closed but didn't reopen" after an update.** Confirmed not diagnosable from the old log (it didn't record the relaunch — corrected my earlier overclaim). Root cause: the relaunch `Popen` started the new AppImage with THIS process's AppImage-injected env (`LD_LIBRARY_PATH`/`PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONPATH`/`APPDIR`…), so the new bundled Python loaded the old mount and crashed silently. Fixed: `_relaunch_env()` strips those vars; also log the relaunch + show a message (don't silently close) if the spawn fails. **(4) "After every install it asked about scanning the disk."** `_on_host_setup_finished` called `refresh_drives()` after ANY wizard run, re-firing the disc scan; now it only refreshes when no drive is selected yet (first-time setup). +7 tests (cyanrip-in-kill-set, progress-throttle + non-progress-not-throttled, relaunch-env-strips, host-setup-skip/refresh ×2); 1057→1062; gate green, 92.85%. **Also confirmed from the log:** the maintainer worked around the colon bug by editing the album title (so that rip had clean metadata + was progressing, just slow — BDR-209D ~55min/track). **LESSON:** a kill/force-stop pattern must enumerate EVERY backend's process names — adding a backend (cyanrip) silently left cancel broken for it. And throttle at the RIGHT layer: the expensive flood was the text-widget append, not the bar update — narrowing the rate-limit to the log pane fixed the black-out without dropping the data the progress tests (and the user's eyes) rely on.
- **cyanrip colon-in-title fix (foundation) + post-update relaunch logging (v0.3.7, 2026-06-27, BDR-209D rig).** Acted on the v0.3.6 root-cause. The maintainer chose "correct tags + clean folders." **Shipped the safe foundation:** `_escape_meta_value` now substitutes `:`→`∶` (U+2236, the char cyanrip itself uses for a colon in a path) instead of backslash-escaping it — because cyanrip's `append_missing_keys` splits on `:` before any escape is honored, so a literal/escaped colon is unpassable. Folders are now clean ("Every Breath You Take∶ The Classics") and the parser can't choke (this likely also unblocks the rip, which had failed with no log — a strong sign cyanrip choked on the malformed `-a`). **Deferred (the last 10% of the chosen approach):** restoring the literal `:` in the FLAC *tags* via a post-rip metaflac merge (`write_tags` is a per-key remove+set, so it can overwrite ALBUM/ALBUMARTIST/TITLE/ARTIST with real colons without dropping cyanrip's genre/MBID/ISRC). Held because it needs cyanrip's EXACT Vorbis key casing (ALBUM vs album, ALBUMARTIST vs album_artist) to avoid duplicate tags — and that's only knowable from a real rip's `metaflac --export-tags` dump (real-hardware-is-ground-truth). Requested that dump from the maintainer. **Also:** corrected an earlier overstatement — the log shows a 0.3.5 process started 31s after the update install, but the log does NOT record whether the app auto-relaunched it or the user did; I claimed the former without evidence. Added explicit relaunch logging in `_on_update_install_finished` (+ graceful handling if the spawn fails — show a message, keep the window open, instead of silently closing into nothing). +0 net new test files (updated the cyanrip escaping test for the `∶` substitution); gate green, 92.77%, 1057 passing. **LESSON:** when a fix has a safe, self-contained foundation and a risky tail that needs a fact only real hardware can supply (exact tag-key casing), ship the foundation and gate the tail on the hardware artifact — don't guess the casing and risk duplicate tags. And: never claim a log proves something it doesn't record (auto-restart vs manual open look identical in the log without an explicit relaunch line — so add the line).
- **In-app install for optional deps + cyanrip colon-in-title bug ROOT-CAUSED from a real rip log (v0.3.6, 2026-06-27, BDR-209D rig).** Two things from the maintainer's debug log (now easy to grab via the new Open-logs-folder button). **(A) Shipped: install option for optional components.** The maintainer (rightly) expected the dependency check to let them *install* the "optional, not installed" deps (Picard, flac), not just list them. Added `_offer_optional_install`: on a user-initiated `Tools → Check dependencies`, it offers to install the optional missing deps and routes them through the SAME tier machinery (Critical Rule #6 — no second install path): Picard auto-installs (Flatpak, with consent), flac (a `from_setup_wizard` tool) opens the one-click container wizard via the manual dialog. Launch-time checks stay quiet (`show_summary` gate) so optional deps never nag. +2 tests; 1055→1057; gate green, 92.79%. **(B) Root-caused (NOT yet fixed): the `Every Breath You Take∶album_artist= The Classics` folder.** The log's actual cyanrip command + the maintainer's Settings proved the template is innocent (`%A/%d/...` → `-D {album_artist}/{album}`, standard). The real cause is a cyanrip CLI flaw: cyanrip preprocesses `-a`/`-t` with `append_missing_keys()`, which `av_strtok(src, ":")`-splits the string **naively (no `\`/quote handling)** and prepends `album=`/`album_artist=` to the first two "keyless" (`=`-less) fragments — its shorthand for `-a "Title:Artist"`. The album "Every Breath You Take: The Classics" has a colon, so our `_escape_meta_value` backslash-escapes it (`\:`) — but `append_missing_keys` ignores the `\`, sees ` The Classics` as a keyless fragment, and injects `album_artist=` before it; then `av_dict_parse_string` (which DOES honor `\:`) reads the album value straight through the injected text → `album = "Every Breath You Take:album_artist= The Classics"`. Confirmed via the cyanrip source (cyanreg/cyanrip `cyanrip_main.c`). **Conclusion: you cannot safely put a colon in a cyanrip `-a`/`-t` value via the CLI** — neither backslash nor quotes survive `append_missing_keys`'s naive split. The rip also came back `success=False`. **Fix options (pending maintainer call):** (1) feed cyanrip colon-sanitized values (`:`→`∶` U+2236, which cyanrip uses for paths anyway) so folders are clean and the parser can't choke, then authoritatively **retag the FLACs post-rip with metaflac** using the real colon (correct tags + clean folders) — the fully-correct fix, needs the cyanrip known-disc path to run the metaflac retag; (2) accept `∶` in tags too (simpler, slightly-wrong tag). **LESSON:** when feeding an external CLI a delimited mini-language, the *documented* parser (av_dict_parse_string honors `\`) may be fronted by an *undocumented* preprocessor (append_missing_keys, naive split) — escaping that satisfies one can be defeated by the other; verify against the tool's actual source, and prefer not routing user data through a fragile delimiter format at all (retag out-of-band).
- **Bazzite hardware session round 2: flac never exported, no in-app recovery for a wedged scan, and logs hard to reach — fixed all three (v0.3.5, 2026-06-27, BDR-209D rig).** The maintainer, now on 0.3.4 and steering toward the cyanrip backend (the documented answer for the BDR-209D's cdrdao-stalls + +667 offset), surfaced three issues. **(1) `flac` decoder "optional, not installed":** the setup wizard's tools step installs the `flac` package in the container (titled "whipper + flac") and exports metaflac, but the **export step never exported `flac` itself** — so `flac --test` integrity verification (which runs for backends that DON'T self-verify, i.e. cyanrip — whipper self-verifies) and the CTDB audio cross-check couldn't find it. Fixed: add `/usr/bin/flac` to the export list + a `flac_path`/`flac_exported()` and require it in `_export_done`/`is_ready`, so a wizard re-run repairs an existing install. **(2) A stuck disc *scan* wedged the drive with no in-app recovery:** Force-stop was enabled only during a rip, but a stalled `whipper cd info` (cdrdao holding the drive; the in-container reader survives the host-side timeout because podman doesn't forward the kill) left the user dropping to a terminal. Fixed: `RipControls.set_scan_active()` enables Force-stop during a scan; a new `drive_control.free_drive()` kills the reader **without ejecting** (disc stays in for a Rescan); `_on_force_stop_button` branches scan-vs-rip; a scan timeout auto-frees; a manual scan Force-stop shows a clean "freed the drive" message via a `_scan_force_stopped` flag. **(3) Logs hard to reach:** added **Help → Open logs folder…** (QDesktopServices opens `LOG_DIR` in the file manager — non-blocking) so the maintainer can grab `log.txt` without a terminal (and it makes every future debugging round easier). +10 tests (free_drive no-eject ×2, scan-active enable, host_setup flac export + is_ready, force-stop scan-vs-rip branch ×2, scan-timeout auto-free, non-timeout no-free, force-stopped clean message, logs-folder menu + openUrl); existing host_setup tests updated to register flac as exported. 1045→1055; gate green, coverage 92.80%. help_content updated (Force-stop for scans, Open-logs-folder). **Still open — the `Every Breath You Take:album_artist=The Classics` folder name:** the album is the real Police comp "Every Breath You Take: The Classics" (title contains a colon); cyanrip parses `-a` pairs with `:` and `_escape_meta_value` DOES backslash-escape `:`, so a clean flow shouldn't leak `album_artist=` into `{album}`. The maintainer said they "need to remove the album_artist argument," implying a config/template value — needs their disc/track template + the actual cyanrip `-a` line from the new logs button to pin down before fixing. **LESSON:** a dependency the setup *installs* isn't usable until it's also *exported* to the host PATH — the install step and the export step must agree on the full tool list (they'd silently drifted: tools installs flac, export forgot it). And recovery affordances (Force-stop) must cover every operation that can hold the drive, not just the obvious one (a scan wedges it as surely as a rip).
- **First disc scan of a session failing with "whipper timed out after 30s" — fixed the cold-container timeout (2026-06-27, maintainer's Bazzite + Pioneer BDR-209D rig).** Confirmed v0.3.3 published clean (AppImage + `.sha256` + `.zsync`, built from the freeze-fix commit) and gave the maintainer the one-time manual-install command to escape the 0.3.1 updater-freeze loop. Then diagnosed the *separate* disc-scan timeout they reported. **Root cause:** the `~/.local/bin/whipper` wrapper transparently enters the Distrobox `ripping` container; the **first** whipper call of a session pays podman's container cold-start (tens of seconds on first use after a boot). But both timeouts gating that first call were calibrated for a *warm* system — `_INFO_TIMEOUT_S=30s` (the scan, `whipper cd info`) and `_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S=10s` (the launch dependency probe, `whipper --version`); their own comments said "within seconds"/"milliseconds". So the cold start tripped the scan ("timed out after 30s") and could also make a cold container look like a *missing* whipper at launch. Confirmed this is the scan path (TOC read + MB disc-id), **not** the BDR-209D's +667 read-offset bug — that bites at rip time (overread), not `cd info`. **Fix:** raised `_INFO_TIMEOUT_S`→120s and `_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S`→60s with full reasoning in the comments (backstop against a wedged process, not a latency target — warm case still returns in ~1-2s; both run off the GUI thread so the window stays responsive). Side effect by design: the launch probe now *waits for* the container to come up, which **warms it**, so the disc scan that follows runs warm and fast. Added a `friendly_disc_scan_error` branch so a residual timeout shows plain language pointing at **Rescan disc** (a retry against the now-warm container almost always succeeds) instead of the raw line. +3 regression tests (friendly timeout message; two constant-floor guards so a future contributor can't quietly shrink the timeouts back below a cold-container budget). Cut as **v0.3.4**. **Caveat:** code-correct + unit-tested but **hardware-gated** — final proof is a real cold-container scan on the Bazzite rig (no distrobox/optical drive here); flagged honestly. **LESSON (graduated into `architecture.md` §3.3 subprocess rules):** a timeout on a *container-entering* command must budget for the container cold-start, not just the warm-path latency — a warm-calibrated cap turns a legitimately-slow first call into a false failure (here, two of them: a false "timed out" scan and a false "missing whipper" at launch). Pin such timeouts with a regression test.
- **THE real updater-freeze root cause: worker-thread widget access in the in-app installer (2026-06-27, maintainer hit "Not Responding" at 100% on 0.3.1).** I twice mis-diagnosed an updater "freeze" from screenshots (first the pre-0.2.6 synchronous-`kbuildsycoca` freeze — real but already fixed; then "it's just a slow download"). The About dialog settled it: the user was on **0.3.1** (the supposedly-fixed updater) running **Python 3.14**, window titled **"Whipper GUI (Not Responding)"** — a genuine GUI-thread deadlock, not a slow download (downloads are off-thread). Stopped guessing and **read the threading code**: `_begin_update_install` connected the install worker's `progress`/`status`/`finished` signals to **local closures + a lambda**. A callable with no `QObject` receiver is connected by Qt as a **DIRECT** connection, so those handlers ran on the **worker thread** — and they call `dialog.setValue/setLabelText/close()` and pop a `QMessageBox` (widgets). Touching widgets off the GUI thread is illegal in Qt and **deadlocks the main thread** → "Not Responding". This is the *exact* bug class fixed once before for the launch dep-check (lambda→bound-method), but the install path was missed. **Audited every `worker.<signal>.connect(...)` in `ui/`** (grep): all others (rip, dep-check, drive-list, MusicBrainz, disc-info, host-setup, uninstall, drive-setup, update-*check*) already use bound methods — only the update *install* path was wrong. **Fix:** stash the dialog + a phase flag on `self` (`_install_dialog`/`_install_post_download`, init in `MainWindow.__init__`) and connect `progress`/`status`/`finished` to **bound methods** (`_on_install_progress`/`_on_install_status`/`_on_update_install_finished`) → Qt queues them to the GUI thread. Folded in the progress-bar improvement (determinate % during download → moving busy bar for the quick verify/install, so it never sits at a frozen-looking static 100%) and a pure `_is_download_phase` predicate (tested). +3 tests (handlers drive the dialog; phase predicate; finished now reads `self._install_dialog`), 2 updated. 1040→1042; gate green, coverage ~92.7%. Cut as **v0.3.3**. **Crucial caveat:** the *running* version drives its own update, so 0.3.1/0.3.2's buggy updater still freezes — the user must install 0.3.3 **manually** (download from releases) once to escape the loop; in-app updates work from 0.3.3 on. **LESSON (graduated — this is now twice):** in PySide, a worker signal connected to a **closure/lambda runs on the *emitting* (worker) thread** (no receiver context → DirectConnection); any handler that touches widgets MUST be a **bound method of a GUI-thread QObject**. Grep `worker.*\.connect\((lambda|<local closure>)` as a standing check. And: don't diagnose a freeze from a screenshot — "(Not Responding)" in the title means GUI-thread block; read the threading, don't guess.
- **Three real onboarding bugs found from a Bazzite hardware report — fixed (2026-06-26, maintainer: wizard "seemed to freeze," "uninstall didnt do all," "why do i have to copy links?").** The maintainer hit all three on a real uninstall→reinstall cycle. Diagnosed each from the code (hardware-gated, so unit-tested not hardware-verified — flagged honestly). **(A) Wizard "freeze":** after `distrobox create`, the FIRST `distrobox enter` — triggered by the `whipper_in_container` idempotency probe for the "tools" step — runs distrobox's multi-minute one-time container init, and that probe happens *after* the container step records RAN but *before* the next step's RUNNING ping, so the status line sat stale on "'ripping' container — working…" the whole time (the screenshot's tell: container ✓ installed, status still on the container step). Not a true hang (`SubprocessRunner` uses `stdin=DEVNULL` + a 30-min timeout) — slow + zero feedback. **Fix:** emit a "checking the container — first start can take a minute…" ping BEFORE the container-entering probes (`_SLOW_PROBE_STEPS = {tools, cyanrip}`). **(B) "uninstall didnt do all":** `HostTeardown.run` set `stop=True` on the first failed step → all later steps CANCELLED. But teardown steps are *independent* (unlike setup, where later steps depend on earlier), so one failure (commonly `distrobox rm` on a busy container) left the AppImage/whipper.conf/shortcuts behind. **Fix:** best-effort — record FAILED and continue; only an explicit cancel stops it; the final settings/logs step is *kept* if anything failed (so the log survives to debug) and removed on a fully-clean run. **(C) "why copy links?":** whipper/metaflac are `tier=MANUAL, install_command=None` in the registry (they're container-provided, not host-`dnf`-able), so when missing they fell to the tier-(c) `ManualInstallDialog` = a copyable Google search string — the wrong fallback for tools the app installs itself via the wizard. **Fix:** new `DependencySpec.from_setup_wizard` flag (whipper/metaflac/flac=True); the manual dialog now shows a primary **"Set it up automatically…"** button (opens the host-setup wizard) for those, with the search string demoted to a labelled last resort; `_gui_manual_dialog` wires `on_setup_wizard=self.open_host_setup_dialog`. +6 tests, 2 updated (teardown best-effort, setup checking-ping); 1034→1040; gate green, coverage 92.67%. **LESSON:** setup and teardown look symmetric but have OPPOSITE failure semantics — setup stops on failure (later steps depend on earlier), teardown must NOT (steps are independent; stopping strands the user with a half-removed system). And a "manual install" tier-(c) fallback is wrong for any dep the app can install itself — those deserve a one-click path to the installer, not a search string. **Caveat recorded:** these are code-correct + unit-tested but NOT hardware-verified (no distrobox/KDE here) — the wizard log (`~/.local/share/whipper-gui/log.txt`, which logs each `host-setup:` command) is what would confirm slow-vs-hung definitively.
- **Real-binary verification caught a WavPack bug the stubbed tests couldn't (2026-06-26, right after merging the multi-format feature to main; maintainer: "merge it, then see if anything else can be done").** Ran the **shipped** `transcode_files()` through the **real** ffmpeg (not the unit-test stub) on self-generated audio (sine + generated PNG, Rule #8-safe) to verify the feature actually works — and the WavPack path **wrote nothing**: the argv passed `-f wavpack`, but ffmpeg's WavPack *muxer* is named **`wv`** (only the *encoder* is `wavpack`), so ffmpeg aborted with "Requested output format 'wavpack' is not known." The unit tests stub the runner, so they pinned the argv shape but never learned ffmpeg rejects it; my earlier design spike missed it too because it relied on the `.wv` *extension* to infer the muxer, whereas the production code writes a `.transcode.tmp` and **must** pass `-f` explicitly. **Fix:** `-f wavpack` → `-f wv` (one line), plus a real-ffmpeg **integration test** (`tests/test_transcode.py`, skipped when ffmpeg/flac absent) that decodes each lossless output back to PCM and asserts it's bit-identical to the source FLAC — the regression guard that would have caught it, per the institutional "every shipped bug gets a regression test" rule. Re-verified: WavPack/WAV bit-identical PCM, MP3 carries tags + embedded cover, all through the real adapter. +3 tests (1031→1034); gate green, coverage 92.65%. Pushed to main as the fix. **LESSON (the KDD-21 discipline, re-earned):** a stubbed unit test pins the *shape* of an external command but never proves the external tool *accepts* it — run the real binary on a real artifact before calling a subprocess-driving feature done, and make the spike use the **exact** production argv (the `-f` the temp-file path forces), not a convenience shortcut. ffmpeg's encoder name ≠ its muxer name was the specific trap. **Second catch, same pass — WavPack rips would have had NO cover at all:** tracing `cover_art.apply_cover_art` showed the default "embed" mode embeds in the FLAC then **deletes** the folder image (`save_file=False`), and ffmpeg can't embed art in `.wv` — so a default-config WavPack rip would have ended with no visible cover anywhere (the FLAC master has it; the `.wv` the user wanted does not). Fixed by **forcing the folder `cover.<ext>` save** for non-embedding output formats (WavPack/WAV) when art is wanted + the disc is identified — driven by a new `transcode.EMBEDS_COVER_ART` set (`{"mp3"}`), wired in `_on_rip_finished`, covering both the GUI-fetch (cyanrip/unknown) and whipper-known paths. +3 more tests (→1037). Graduated into KDD-22. **LESSON:** "tags + images for all if possible" has to be traced *through* the existing cover-art code, not assumed — the embed-then-delete behavior plus the muxer limit combined into a silent "no cover" that neither piece showed alone.
- **Multi-format output SHIPPED — FLAC/WavPack/MP3/WAV, flipping Critical Rule #4 (2026-06-26, maintainer re-sent the directive: "flac and wv lossless, best practices for mp3… tags/images for all if possible… ripped or transcoded correctly… best effort highest quality, verifiable by other users").** Read the re-sent directive ("it should still be either ripped or *transcoded correctly*" — documents don't transcode) as "stop asking, build it," and built the user-facing feature the prior session had held. **Empirical spike first** (tools here: ffmpeg 6.1.1 + flac 1.4.3; **no wavpack/lame installed**): generated a tagged FLAC-with-cover in the scratchpad and proved (1) ffmpeg FLAC→WavPack is **bit-identical PCM** (lossless ✓) and carries text tags, but (2) ffmpeg's WavPack muxer **rejects a second stream** ("only supports a single WavPack stream") so it **can't embed cover art in the `.wv`**; (3) MP3 carries tags + APIC cover. That settled the design: **single-binary ffmpeg path**, embed art in FLAC/MP3, fall back to the album-folder `cover.<ext>` for WavPack (the universal image `cover_art.py` already writes), document the `.wv`-embed gap as a future standalone-`wavpack` enhancement. **Architecture decision — transcode-always (KDD-22):** rather than branch on `native_output_formats()` (cyanrip native `-o` vs whipper transcode), both backends rip FLAC (cyanrip is already `-o flac`) and a non-FLAC choice is a post-rip ffmpeg transcode — because cyanrip's native MP3 is only CBR, so best-practice VBR `-V0` needs the transcode path anyway, and this keeps the FLAC master + one tested code path for both backends. **Built:** `transcode.py` gained WavPack (`-c:a wavpack`) + a `_FORMAT_EXT` map (`.wv` ≠ `.wavpack`) + public `SUPPORTED_FORMATS`; the transcode folded into the post-rip daemon thread **last** (after tag→cover→re-compress, reading the final FLACs, sibling files only so it can't race metaflac) via a new `transcode_done` signal + `_on_transcoded` slot; the **Settings → Output format** combo (FLAC/WavPack/MP3/WAV) + a live WAV no-tags/art warning. **Latent bug caught by exposing the field:** `SettingsDialog.to_config()` didn't round-trip `output_format`/`mp3_vbr_quality`, so saving Settings would have silently reset them — fixed + regression test (graduated into KDD-22). +11 tests (1020→1031), coverage 92.62%, ruff clean. **Docs:** flipped CLAUDE.md Critical Rule #4 (FLAC-only → FLAC-default/master + derived formats; preserved the durable "route every encoder through the one dep subsystem, no bespoke install code" principle), updated `docs/mp3-wav-support.md` (status→shipped, §4(b)→transcode-always, §6→built + the `.wv`-cover limitation), README (the Output-format table + the per-format ffmpeg commands, replacing the stale "MP3/WAV — P1, not in v1" section), TASKS (encoder item `[~]`→`[x]`), PLANNING (transcode.py entry + KDD-22), DEPENDENCIES (ffmpeg now the shipped encoder dep + a `wavpack` future-enhancement row), CHANGELOG (Added). **LESSON:** spike the *toolchain limit* before committing to a design — "tags + images for all if possible" resolved to "embed for FLAC/MP3, folder-image for WavPack" only because the 5-minute ffmpeg test exposed the single-stream muxer limit; guessing would have either over-promised embedded `.wv` art or needlessly added a `wavpack` dependency. And: exposing a config field is incomplete until `to_config` carries it.
- **Docs brought current + encoder design locked from fresh upstream research (2026-06-26, maintainer: "update documents to current… flac and wv lossless, best practices for mp3… tags/images for all if possible… best effort highest quality, verifiable by other users").** Two halves. **(A) Doc-currency fix-batch** — cleared every confirmed drift from the prior session's code-vs-docs audit (the audit had held everything for input; this lifted the hold): the **FLAC `-e -p` flag drift** (the shipped set is `-8 -e -p --verify --silent -f -o`, but `TASKS.md`, `docs/test-plan.md` Test 10, `PLANNING.md` ×2, *and* a self-contradicting line inside the CHANGELOG re-compress bullet still said `-8 --verify` with "`-e/-p` deliberately omitted" — all corrected, with the encode-time-only/zero-decode-cost reasoning recorded); **README cyanrip caveats** (progress bars + cover art shipped — verified in `rip_worker.py` progress regexes + `adapters/cover_art.py` — so the "don't move yet / whipper-only" caveats were stale; left only the restart-after-switch one); **`main_window.py` line count** (`~460`→`~620` in `PLANNING.md` + `architecture.md`, annotated that the original split landed ~460 and it has grown; the KDD-19 `1707→460` historical snapshot left as dated fact); **`PLANNING.md` docs-tree** (added `mp3-wav-support.md`, `cyanrip-fork.md`, `archive/README.md`, `archive/archival-extraction-guide-2026-06.md`); **`checks.py` description** (added `check_flac()`/`check_ffmpeg()`, fixed `version: str`→`tuple[int, ...] | None` to match `ProbeResult`); **KDD-18** (`read by app.py`→`composition.py`'s `build_backend`, per KDD-21); **`CLAUDE.md` `--doctor`** (clarified `--no-network`/`--backend` are `scripts/preflight.py`-only — `whipper-gui`'s argparse only has `--version`/`--uninstall`/`--doctor`); **`DEPENDENCIES.md`** (cyanrip is provisioned by the host-setup wizard, not a dep probe — softened the section heading + added a note); **`pyproject.toml`** classifiers (`Development Status :: 1 - Planning`→`4 - Beta`, added Python 3.13). **(B) Encoder design locked** — rewrote `docs/mp3-wav-support.md` with **current** args from the maintainer-supplied upstream docs (xiph FLAC, HydrogenAudio Recommended-LAME via lame.sourceforge.io, wavpack.com): **FLAC** `-8 -e -p --verify` (+ version-gated `-j` on 1.5.0; decode cost rides on LPC order `-l`, not `-e`/`-p`); **MP3** best-practice VBR `-V0` (= ffmpeg `-q:a 0`, joint-stereo on, #516 is CBR-only so VBR sidesteps it, matches the maintainer's EAC rip); **WavPack** lossless-default + `-hh -m -v -x` (very-high, source-MD5, verify pass, extra effort — the WavPack analogues of FLAC `--verify`/STREAMINFO-MD5) with APEv2 tags + binary `Cover Art (Front)` via the standalone `wavpack` tool; **WAV** kept raw-interchange-only (RIFF can't carry tags/art → warn, WavPack is the lossless-with-metadata answer). Converted the doc's §5 "open questions" into **locked decisions** per the directive: formats = FLAC(default/master) + WavPack + MP3 first-class, WAV as a warned extra; lossless = provably lossless (verify pass + re-checkable MD5, the "verified by other users" bar); ffmpeg approved + `wavpack` to be registered with its encode path; FLAC stays the archival master; one-format-at-a-time first; tags+art for all three containers that allow it. **Did NOT build** the user-facing feature — the rip-flow wiring + Settings selector + the WavPack encoder in `transcode.py` are what flip the locked Critical Rule #4 (FLAC-only for v1), so they stay held for an explicit go/no-go (presented to the maintainer this session). The packaging-metadata change is the one non-doc edit → real CHANGELOG `Changed` entry; the rest is doc-currency. **LESSON:** a held audit's value is only realized when the hold is lifted *and* the same sweep catches new instances of the same class — fixing the four known `-e -p` sites surfaced a fifth (the CHANGELOG bullet contradicting itself); grep the *pattern*, not just the listed line numbers.
- **Enforced-safety layer added from the "project bootstrap" upload (2026-06-25, maintainer chose "add enforced safety only").** The maintainer uploaded a generic one-time Claude-Code bootstrap; Phase-0 detection = this is a mature existing project whose CLAUDE.md/docs already exceed what the bootstrap generates, so running it wholesale would be redundant/risky. The one genuine gap it surfaced: **we had no `.claude/` enforced layer** — all safety was *guidance* (Critical rules) + the `.gitignore` backstop, none of it a hard guarantee (which the bootstrap correctly notes CLAUDE.md can't provide). Adopted only that, tailored: **(1) `.githooks/pre-commit`** — the canonical hard stop for Critical Rule #8 (blocks committing any audio/copyrighted-media extension, incl. via `git add -f`; runs on every commit path). **Tested for real** (staged a fake `.flac`, commit blocked exit 1, cleaned up — never committed). Activated here via `core.hooksPath` + wired into `dev-setup.sh` for contributors; `--no-verify` is the documented escape for a verified CC0 sample. **(2) `.claude/settings.json`** (committed/shared) — permission `deny` for destructive commands (`rm -rf`, force-push) + secret reads, plus a `PreToolUse` belt that blocks a Bash call while audio is staged (auto-active in Claude sessions, the cloud-session case where `core.hooksPath` may be unset). Deliberately **no `ask` on normal `git push`** so the merge-and-keep-going workflow is unaffected. `.gitignore` now excludes `.claude/settings.local.json` (personal overrides). Documented in CLAUDE.md Project operations ("Enforced safety"), CHANGELOG, and here; did **not** touch the locked Critical rules. Offered to delete the uploaded bootstrap file. **LESSON:** for a hard guarantee, guidance isn't enough — pair the rule (CLAUDE.md) with an *enforced* mechanism (git hook / settings deny); and a "bootstrap/setup" tool aimed at empty projects should be cherry-picked, not run wholesale, on a mature one (its own Phase-0 rule says exactly that when a CLAUDE.md already exists).
- **Doc/repo consistency audit (2026-06-25, maintainer chose it from the "what next" menu).** Swept the docs that grew this session for drift vs. reality. **Fixed (living-doc drift):** (1) `DEPENDENCIES.md` coverage gate `88`→`91`; (2) `docs/architecture.md` coverage `~90`→`91` (both stale — the gate ratcheted to 91 on 2026-06-23); (3) `PLANNING.md` — added `adapters/transcode.py` to the module tree + a module-list entry (it existed in code but wasn't in the tree), and enriched the `parity.py` entry with `decode_log_bytes`/UTF-16; (4) `README.md` test count `950+`→`1,000+` (actual 1020). **Verified clean (no action):** coverage in `CLAUDE.md`/`testing.md` already 91; `docs/README` index complete (all 11 docs incl. the brief + `mp3-wav-support.md`); **0 broken internal `.md` links** across the 12 key docs (scripted check); the residual "converted to UTF-8" mentions are accurate *historical* context (the CHANGELOG fix entry + a dated session-log entry), not drift; the new ABC capability methods are intentionally not enumerated in architecture.md. Docs only → `[skip changelog]`. **LESSON:** distinguish living-doc drift (fix) from historical record (leave) — the CHANGELOG/session-log "88→90→91" and "UTF-16→UTF-8" lines are *chronology*, correct as written; only the present-tense claims in CLAUDE/DEPENDENCIES/architecture/README must track current reality.
- **Store EAC logs as native UTF-16 (not converted) + accept the WavPack rip (2026-06-25, maintainer: "shouldn't the parity checker be UTF-16?" + "use the wavpack").** The maintainer was right that converting the committed EAC logs to UTF-8 repeated the exact normalization that hid the decoder bug. Reversed it: the three EAC logs (`EAC_flac` baseline, `EAC_mp3`, `EAC_wav`) are now stored **verbatim in EAC's native UTF-16** — MP3/WAV copied byte-for-byte from the uploads, the FLAC baseline re-encoded UTF-8→UTF-16LE+BOM+CRLF (content untouched). **`.gitattributes` gained `output_reference/**/*.log -text`** so `text=auto` LF-normalization can't corrupt the UTF-16 (verified: staged blob == disk bytes, BOM intact, numstat shows binary; `.cue` stays ASCII text). **Tests now read the baseline via `decode_log_bytes(read_bytes())`** (test_parity ×3, test_parsers_eac_log ×2) instead of `read_text("utf-8")` — so they exercise the real production decode path, and a future re-normalization can't silently pass. Checker re-verified on the UTF-16 files: MP3 12/14, WAV 13/14, baseline self 14/14; full suite green. **WavPack:** the maintainer confirmed the "WAV" rip being WavPack (`wavpack -h -m`) is fine ("sorry I didn't realize I made it different") — softened the EAC_wav README from "wrong format, replace with plain WAV" to "WavPack kept intentionally; equivalent for extraction parity; whether to *offer* WavPack as a GUI output is an open §5 question." Docs only (READMEs, fixtures/README, output_reference/README encoding note) → `[skip changelog]`. **LESSON (graduated into the output_reference/README + fixtures/README):** store reference artifacts in their **authentic encoding** and decode at read time — never normalize-on-store; the decoder is the right layer, and normalized fixtures hide format bugs (this is the second time the UTF-8 conversion bit us).
- **EAC "WAV" rip stored + a format gotcha caught (2026-06-25, maintainer supplied the EAC "wav" log/cue).** Ran it through `scripts/eac_parity.py` → **13/14 vs the FLAC baseline** (best session yet): only **T3** differs (Copy `329DC760` ≠ `59D352DD`) — the disc's persistent marginal spot; **T4 came back clean** this time (it had errored in the MP3 session, confirming the trouble is transient disc-surface, not systematic); T5 matches the baseline (the known quirk). **Gotcha flagged:** despite being called "wav," EAC's User Defined Encoder for this rip is **`wavpack.exe -h -m`** → the output is **WavPack (.wv)**, a *lossless-compressed, tagged* format (APEv2 via `-w`), **not** the plain uncompressed PCM WAV our design's WAV path produces (`ffmpeg -c:a pcm_s16le`). For *extraction parity* it's equivalent (lossless → same Copy CRC); as a *format reference* it documents WavPack, not plain WAV. Stored it anyway per the maintainer's standing "store the files, not perfect but valuable" call → `output_reference/EAC_wav/eac_wav_police_classics.{log,cue}` (UTF-16→UTF-8, no audio) with a README that leads with the WavPack distinction + the 13/14 caveat + how to replace; updated the parity matrix (TASKS) and `output_reference/README` layout. Cue nit: this session's cue dropped T10's ISRC. **LESSON:** read what the rip *actually* did, not what the folder's named — "WAV" was WavPack; surfacing that (the maintainer likely set EAC's WAV slot to wavpack) matters more than silently filing it under EAC_wav.

- **EAC MP3 rip analyzed → found + fixed a real UTF-16 bug in the parity checker (2026-06-25, maintainer supplied the EAC MP3 log/cue).** Ran the maintainer's EAC MP3 log through our own `scripts/eac_parity.py` vs the FLAC baseline — it reported **0/14 (every track "missing")**, which was wrong. **Root cause (real shipped bug):** EAC writes logs as **UTF-16-with-BOM**; the checker read them as UTF-8 → every char became a replacement char → parser found no CRCs → false "NOT parity". The committed baseline had been converted to UTF-8, which had hidden this the whole time. **Fix:** `whipper_gui.parity.decode_log_bytes(raw)` byte-sniffs the encoding (UTF-16 LE/BE BOM, UTF-8 BOM, a NUL-heavy heuristic for BOM-less UTF-16, else UTF-8; never raises); the CLI now `read_bytes()` + decodes. +7 regression tests (every encoding + the exact "UTF-16 → parities after decode" case). **Re-run result: 12/14 match** — T3 (`418F6CF8`≠`59D352DD`) and T4 (`1D0079A1`≠`60D796AE`) differ; both are **read errors this session on the marginal disc's known trouble zone** (T4's *Test* CRC `60D796AE` matched the baseline but its *Copy* pass didn't — a transient copy-pass error), not an MP3-format issue. T5 matches the baseline (the consistent disc quirk). **So this MP3 rip is NOT a clean extraction.** **The real value it delivered: it empirically confirms EAC's MP3 encoder config = `lame3.100.1 -V 0` + ID3 tags on** — exactly our design's recommendation, and == our `ffmpeg -q:a 0` transcode. Recorded in `docs/mp3-wav-support.md` §3. Cue nit: this session's cue dropped T12's ISRC (`GBAAM8300009`), minor. **Then (maintainer: "store the files anyway, not perfect but valuable"):** committed log+cue to `output_reference/EAC_mp3/eac_mp3_police_classics.{log,cue}` (UTF-16→UTF-8, LF-normalised, no audio — Rule #8) with a README flagging the 12/14 imperfection + how to replace it and recording the encoder-config value; updated the parity matrix (TASKS) + `output_reference/README` layout (`EAC_mp3` 🟡 imperfect). **Adversarial review of the MP3/WAV foundation (background workflow, 22 agents): 25 raw → 18 verified → 0 confirmed real bugs, 7 nits — foundation validated.** Applied two worthwhile nits: explicit `-map 0:a` on the WAV transcode (audio-only — excludes an embedded cover RIFF can't hold) + a custom-binary test; **empirically settled the review's one substantive risk** by transcoding a real cover-LESS FLAC→MP3 with ffmpeg (works; `-c:v copy` is harmless with no video stream). **LESSON:** dogfooding the tool on a *real* artifact (not the cleaned-up committed fixture) is what surfaced the encoding bug — golden fixtures that have been normalized can hide format bugs the real input would trip; test against the raw thing.

- **MP3/WAV foundation built — the §5 gates, started (2026-06-23, maintainer: "start those please").** Built the unambiguous, well-specified layer the design doc's §5 unblocks, **all unreachable and default-FLAC so v1 stays FLAC-only (Critical Rule #4 honoured at runtime)** while the encoder groundwork lands. **(1) ffmpeg dependency** registered through the *single* subsystem (Critical Rule #6): `check_ffmpeg` in `deps/checks.py` (uses `-version`, single-dash, unlike the GNU probes), an `ffmpeg` spec in `deps/registry.py` (optional, MANUAL tier), a `DEPENDENCIES.md` row. **(2) Config** `output_format="flac"` + `mp3_vbr_quality=0`. **(3) Capability flag** `WhipperBackend.native_output_formats()` (ABC default `{flac}`; cyanrip adds `wav`/`mp3`) so the GUI can pick native-encode vs transcode. **(4) `adapters/transcode.py`** — the whipper-path FLAC→MP3/WAV transcoder, modelled on `flac_recompress.py`: per-file ffmpeg re-encode to a sibling, **atomic swap-in, FLAC kept as the master**, never-raises, MP3 = libmp3lame VBR `-q:a` + tags/cover (`-map_metadata 0`/`-c:v copy` → ID3 APIC), WAV = `pcm_s16le` (no tags — RIFF limit). +~30 tests (transcode all-paths, check_ffmpeg present/absent, registry spec, config round-trip, both backends' capability). 990→~1020 tests, coverage 92.64%. **Deliberately NOT built (held for sign-off):** the rip-flow integration (cyanrip `-o` argv + whipper post-rip transcode call folded into the post-rip thread) and the Settings UI selector + WAV-no-tags warning — that's where Critical Rule #4 *exposure* and the §5 product decisions (keep-FLAC-master, multi-format, default `-V0`, surface WAV?) bite. Then **adversarially reviewed** the foundation via a workflow before merge. **LESSON:** when a feature has a clear buildable core and a decision-laden surface, build the core unreachable/default-off so progress is real but ships nothing — the Critical-Rule-#4 line is "is it reachable by a user?", not "is the code present?".

- **MP3/WAV interim prep while the EAC mp3/wav rips are pending (2026-06-23, maintainer: "anything we can do until then?" → picked all three: parity-tooling readiness + verify-and-write-up + design draft).** Three pieces, no audio touched, no app behaviour changed. **(1) Parity tooling proven format-agnostic:** the per-track Copy CRC is computed on the extracted PCM, so one check covers FLAC/WAV/MP3 — pinned two invariants in `tests/test_parity.py` (WAV rip → parity vs the committed *FLAC* baseline, since lossless → same CRC; MP3 rip → extraction-CRC parity only, audio not bit-compared) and documented the per-format semantics in `whipper_gui.parity`. **Key practical finding for the maintainer: WAV needs no separate EAC baseline** — the existing FLAC baseline IS the WAV target. **(2) Verified the banked encoder gotchas** (background research agent, cited): whipper is **FLAC-only** (v0.5.0 removed profiles) → MP3/WAV on the whipper path is a post-rip re-encode; cyanrip is natively multi-format via `-o` (incl. wav/mp3, bitrate `-b`); the LAME `noise_shaping_amp`/`-q4` bug (#516) is real + still open **but CBR/ABR-only, NOT VBR** → use `-V0`, joint-stereo on, `-q4` only matters if we ever ship CBR; FFmpeg/libmp3lame `-q:a N`=VBR=`-V N` (the standalone-`-q` bug is a different path); WAV needs no RF64 at CD scope and **carries no rich tags/cover art** (collides with the north star → must warn). **(3) Wrote the design** → new `docs/mp3-wav-support.md`: routes MP3/WAV through the existing seams (Config `output_format`, a `native_output_formats()` capability flag, a post-rip `transcode.py` adapter modelled on `flac_recompress.py`, `ffmpeg` as the single transcode dep — Critical Rule #6), keeps FLAC the archival master, and lists the §5 decision gates the **build is gated on** (a new dep needs maintainer sign-off per the Deviation policy). Wired into TASKS (encoder item `[~]`, parity matrix note), `docs/README.md` index. Critical Rule #4 (FLAC-only for v1) untouched. Docs/tests only → `[skip changelog]`. **LESSON:** "what can we do while blocked?" → make everything *downstream* of the blocker ready (the parity checker already consumes the logs; the design already knows the encoder facts), so when the hardware artifact lands the remaining work is mechanical — and surface findings that reduce the maintainer's blocked work (WAV needs no separate rip).

- **Codified "no copyrighted media in the repo, ever" as a Critical rule + `.gitignore` backstop (2026-06-23, maintainer: "we should not store any copyrighted files, especially music, even if testing temporarily").** Prompted by the EAC-log comparison: the maintainer wants to hand me audio to compare but (rightly) won't upload it to the public repo. Graduated the existing directory-scoped policy (`output_reference/README.md`: "never commit commercial audio") into a **project-wide CLAUDE.md Critical rule #8** — no music file or other copyrighted media committed *anywhere*, including temporary test files; test real audio **outside** the repo (scratchpad / `/tmp`), commit only the **text** proof (logs + per-track CRCs). Edited the locked rules section per the maintainer's explicit request. Backstops + cross-refs added: **`.gitignore`** now denies audio extensions (`*.flac/wav/mp3/m4a/aac/ogg/opus/wv/ape/wma/aiff/alac/dsf/dff` — with a `git add -f` escape hatch noted for a verified CC0/self-generated sample); **`docs/testing.md`** gained institutional rule #9 + a Definition-of-Done checkbox; **`docs/test-plan.md`** §0 gained a ⚠️ banner (these tests rip real commercial CDs — keep output out of the tree); **`output_reference/README.md`** copyright bullet now points at the global rule and says "even a temporary test file." Docs/policy + `.gitignore` only, no app code → `[skip changelog]`. **LESSON:** a safety rule needs the always-loaded anchor (CLAUDE.md) *and* a mechanical backstop (`.gitignore`) — the rule is the line of defense, the ignore is the seatbelt; neither alone is enough.

- **Re-compress now uses `-8 -e -p` (maximum effort), since the maintainer is fine spending encode time (2026-06-23, "I am always fine to add encoding time if it helps").** Reversed the earlier "stop at `-8`" call now that the only objection (encode time) is gone: `-e/--exhaustive-model-search` + `-p/--qlp-coeff-precision-search` keep `-l` at 12, so they add **no decode cost** (the dimension that matters for mobile playback) — they cost only (much) slower *encoding* for a small extra size win (typically <1%). Validated on flac 1.4.3: `flac -8 -e -p --verify` exits clean, the file is smaller than `-5`, and the STREAMINFO decoded-PCM MD5 is **identical** (lossless confirmed). Wired via a new `_EXTRA_FLAGS = ("-e", "-p")` constant (drop to `()` to revert); per-file timeout bumped 300→600s (exhaustive search on a long track can run a while). Updated the argv test assertion, the comment, the Settings tooltip ("maximum effort… slow, but encode time only"), the README equivalent-command snippet, and the CHANGELOG bullet. **LESSON:** a "compression is a bonus" decision can flip cleanly once you've separated the cost *dimensions* — here, encode time (maintainer doesn't mind) vs decode cost (the real constraint, unchanged by `-e`/`-p`); naming the mechanism in the prior entry is what made this a one-line, well-reasoned reversal.
- **Recorded the real *why* behind "compression is a bonus": decode cost on portable players (2026-06-23, maintainer).** The maintainer supplied the rationale that high FLAC compression is bad for **mobile/low-power players** — more CPU/battery to *decode* on playback (their ~2015-era logic, "may have changed a bit since"). Verified and made precise against the xiph preset table: the driver is the **LPC prediction order** (`-l`), which the decoder applies per sample — whipper's default `-5` is `-l 8`, `-8` is `-l 12`, so `-8` is modestly heavier to decode. Both stay inside the FLAC **Subset** (max LPC order 12 at ≤48kHz), so it's a decode-*effort* difference, never a hardware-compatibility one; negligible on modern phones/PCs. Useful corollary: `-e`/`-p` keep `-l 12`, so they would add only *encode* time, not decode cost — and whipper's `-5` is genuinely the mobile-friendly default, which re-validates keeping re-compress **off by default**. Graduated this into the `flac_recompress.py` comment, the **Settings tooltip** (user sees the tradeoff), the **README** FLAC section, and the **CHANGELOG** bullet (the off-by-default reason is now the decode cost, not just "modest gain"). Doc/comment + one tooltip string; no logic change. **LESSON:** when the maintainer hands you a decades-old rule of thumb, verify it against the canonical spec and record the *mechanism* (here: LPC order → per-sample decode work), not just the rule — that's what makes the default defensible later.
- **FLAC re-compress flags verified against the xiph spec + hardware test recorded (2026-06-23, maintainer: "check the flac args vs the docs; keep bit-perfect true, high compression is a bonus").** Fetched `xiph.org/flac/documentation_tools_flac.html` and checked our shipped `flac -8 --verify --silent -f -o` arg-by-arg: **all current, nothing deprecated** — `-8` is still the max *preset* (`= -l 12 -b 4096 -m -r 6 -A "subdivide_tukey(3)"`), `--verify` is the bit-perfect guarantee (decode-in-parallel-and-compare), `--silent`/`-f`/`-o` unchanged, and FLAC-input re-encode "preserves all the metadata unless you override it" (tags/PICTURE/cuesheet/seektable) — confirming our no-extra-flags approach. So **no flag change**; the update is recording the verification. The docs surface exactly two further-compression-but-still-lossless options, `-e/--exhaustive-model-search` and `-p/--qlp-coeff-precision-search`, both "(expensive!)" — deliberately **not** adopted (typically <1% for several× the encode time; "compression is a bonus" doesn't justify it), documented as a one-line seam in `flac_recompress.py` for a future maintainer. **Recorded the hardware proof** as test-plan **Test 10**: decoded-PCM MD5 (`metaflac --show-md5sum`) identical before/after = direct bit-perfect proof independent of `--verify`; `flac --test` all-OK; PICTURE block + exported tags unchanged; files ≤ original (larger = red flag). The unit tests stub `flac`, so Test 10 is also the guard that a future `flac` hasn't changed the flags. Comment/doc + test-plan only, no behaviour change → `[skip changelog]`. **LESSON:** "the args may have changed — update" can legitimately resolve to "verified current, nothing to change"; the deliverable is the dated verification + the reasoned non-adoption of `-e`/`-p`, not a fabricated edit.
- **Optional post-rip FLAC `-8` re-compress SHIPPED (whipper-only; 2026-06-23, maintainer: "build that (opt-in, default off, with verify)").** Closed the whipper-only FLAC-compression gap (the prior entry researched it). whipper hardcodes flac `-5` (not exposable — KDD-13); the new feature re-encodes each output FLAC at `-8 --verify` *after* the rip when the user opts in. **Design, mirroring the FLAC-verify feature it's the sibling of:** `adapters/flac_recompress.py` (`recompress_flac_files()` → `RecompressResult`; per file runs `flac -8 --verify --silent -f -o <tmp> <orig>` then `os.replace(tmp, orig)` — an **atomic swap-in** so a failure/crash leaves the original untouched; never raises; same "couldn't run" vs per-file-failure split as flac_verify). A new `WhipperBackend.produces_max_compression_flac()` capability (ABC default False = whipper → runs; cyanrip override True → skipped, since it already encodes at max) gates it, exactly mirroring `self_verifies_encode()`. `Config.recompress_flac_after_rip` (default **off** — it costs CPU for a modest gain) + a Settings checkbox **greyed out for cyanrip** (added to the existing `_whipper_only` capability-gating list). **Folded into the existing post-rip daemon thread** rather than a new thread: `_start_post_rip_processing` gained a `recompress` step that runs **last** (after tag → cover), so it rewrites the final tagged-and-arted files — and `flac` preserves Vorbis tags + embedded PICTURE + cuesheet on a FLAC→FLAC re-encode (verified against the xiph docs), so nothing the rip wrote is lost. Outcome surfaced via a queued `flac_recompress_done` signal (logged, non-alarming — a failed file is still a valid rip, so it never hijacks the status line). **Mutually exclusive with FLAC-verify per backend** by construction: whipper runs re-compress (its `--verify` re-proves bit-exactness) and skips verify (self-verifies); cyanrip runs verify and skips re-compress. +16 tests (adapter all-pass/nonzero-rc/missing-temp/missing-binary/timeout-continues/OSError/atomic-swap-failure/empty/properties, both backend capabilities, config default+round-trip, Settings reflect+round-trip+gating, finish-handler runs-for-whipper/skips-cyanrip/skips-off + slot logging). 972→990 tests, coverage 92.57%. **LESSON (graduated to KDD-13 / TASKS):** "max compression" is a *backend capability*, not a global — model it as an ABC capability flag (like `self_verifies_encode`) so the GUI gates per-backend and the next backend just answers the question; and a post-rip rewrite belongs **inside** the one post-rip metaflac thread (sequenced after tag/art), never a parallel thread that could race metaflac on the same file.
- **"FLAC -8 for cyanrip" researched → resolved as a no-op for cyanrip (2026-06-23, maintainer: "continue with FLAC -8 for cyanrip").** Investigated cyanrip's option surface (upstream README + `src/cyanrip_encode.c`): **cyanrip already encodes FLAC at *maximum* compression** — the README says "always uses maximum compression", and the code sets libavcodec's `compression_level` explicitly per output format (`avctx->compression_level = cfmt->compression_level`) rather than leaving FFmpeg's default of 5 — and it exposes **no** compression-level flag. So the cyanrip path is already ≥ `-8`; there is nothing to expose or build, and writing a no-op would be wrong. The `-8` gap is therefore **whipper-only** (whipper hardcodes flac `-5`), closable only via an optional post-rip re-encode (a separate, whipper-behavior-changing feature) — narrowed the TASKS item to `[~]` and annotated KDD-13. **LESSON:** a "do X for backend Y" task can be already-satisfied upstream — verify the tool's actual behavior (README + source) before building; the honest outcome of "continue with FLAC -8 for cyanrip" was a documented finding, not code. No code/test changes; docs only.
- **cyanrip FLAC encode-verify shipped — with a Settings toggle (2026-06-23, maintainer chose "user-controllable toggle, default on").** The deferred "good everything" item, now built. whipper passes `flac --verify` during the rip (each FLAC proven to decode back to the read PCM); cyanrip (FFmpeg) doesn't, so its rips lacked that. After a successful rip the GUI runs `flac --test` on each output FLAC off the GUI thread and warns loudly on any failure. **Design, mirroring proven patterns:** `adapters/flac_verify.py` (`verify_flac_files`, never raises, "couldn't run" vs "file failed"); `workers/flac_verify_worker.py` (daemon thread + `wait_for` the metaflac thread — the exact `ctdb_worker` pattern, NOT a joined QThread, §3.2); a `WhipperBackend.self_verifies_encode()` capability (whipper True, cyanrip inherits False) so whipper never pays a redundant decode; `Config.verify_flac_after_rip` (default True) + a Settings checkbox **greyed out for whipper** via a new inverse (`_cyanrip_only`) capability-gating list mirroring the existing `_whipper_only` one; wired in `main_window_rip` after CTDB via the queued `flac_verify_done` signal (failure hijacks the status line; clean pass is logged quietly). **Test-impact handling:** the toggle defaults on, so existing rip-finish tests would newly trigger verify — fixed by making the generic test doubles self-verify (`_FakeBackend`/`_ArtifactWritingBackend` return True), keeping them unchanged, and adding focused tests for the False path. +19 tests (adapter pass/fail/missing-binary/timeout/OSError/empty, worker no-flacs/sorted/wait_for-join/never-raises, both backend capabilities, config default+round-trip, Settings reflect+round-trip+gating, finish-handler wiring runs-for-cyanrip/skips-whipper/skips-off/surfaces-failure). 953→972 tests, coverage 92.55→92.58%. This reverses the previous entry's deferral now that the maintainer made the UX call.
- **Autonomous hygiene batch (2026-06-23, "merge and keep going without asking").** After merging the metadata work to `main`: **ratcheted the CI coverage gate 90 → 91** (TOTAL 92.55%, comfortable margin; per the "ratchets up, never down" rule — ci.yml + CLAUDE.md + docs/testing.md); **de-drifted the README** test count ("950" → "950+"). **Doc audit** (all docs vs reality): tree + KDD refs consistent except architecture.md's intro still said "KDD-01 … KDD-19" → 21 (the `docs/ecosystem-audit` link "miss" was a false positive — a dated prose mention in a historical entry from before the archive reorg; living docs are correct). **Test hardening:** added `looks_like_cyanrip_log`/`looks_like_eac_log` to the never-raises property suite (they classify arbitrary external text but weren't covered with the `parse_*` fns). 951→952 tests. **Deliberately deferred (not auto-merged unreviewed):** the cyanrip FLAC encode-verify feature — real "good everything" value (whipper self-verifies via `flac --verify`; cyanrip/FFmpeg doesn't), but it threads the delicate post-rip daemon chain *and* carries a UX choice (where/whether to surface; blocking vs not), so it's left specified for a maintainer glance. **Judgment recorded:** even with broad "do anything" autonomy, threading-sensitive + UX-shaped features warrant a look before landing; safe-to-merge-unreviewed = docs, gate hygiene, test hardening, adapter-bounded passthroughs.
- **Acted on the EAC-guide gap analysis: cyanrip metadata richness shipped + fork/consolidation plan written (2026-06-23, "do the plan, but also plan to fork/combine both engines").** **Shipped (#2, feature):** the MB lookup now extracts **genre** (top folksonomy tag — musicbrainzngs 0.7.1 has no `genres` include, so we use `tags`), **disc number/total discs** (medium position/count), and **per-track ISRC** (`isrcs` include), feeding them to cyanrip's `-a`/`-t` as FFmpeg `genre`/`disc`/`isrc`. **Silent passthroughs** (maintainer's choice): read from the window's stored MB `ReleaseDetail` guarded by a matching MBID — *not* editable in the track table — so editable title/artist still come from the table while MB-only fields come from MB. `RipMetadata.tracks` became `TrackTag(number,title,artist,isrc)` (the per-track ISRC slot); `ReleaseSummary`/`TrackSummary` gained the fields. whipper unaffected (tags itself from `--release-id`). Tests: MB extraction (genre top-tag, both string/dict ISRC shapes, disc numbering) + a degenerate/odd-input case (never raises, defaults clean) + the cyanrip argv (single-disc omits `disc=`) + the GUI builder flow. 950→951 tests, coverage 92.45→92.51%. **Composer deferred** (per-recording work-relationship queries = rabbit hole). **Planned, not built (per maintainer):** a long-horizon plan to **fork/combine whipper + cyanrip into one in-house engine** — `docs/cyanrip-fork.md` got §7 "Option 3a: vendor + branch-consolidate both upstreams" (git-subtree monorepo, the mirror→enumerate→triage→per-branch-verify→sequential-merge→validate procedure, GPL-3/LGPL-2.1 attribution, the "merging unreleased branches can regress quality → verify-or-reject" risk, and a low-cost read-only first spike). Licensing research (this session): **both are forkable under our GPL-3.0** — whipper is GPL-3.0, cyanrip is LGPL-2.1 (relicensable to GPL-3, the permissive base), libcdio-paranoia is already GPL-3.0; the cost is maintenance, not legal. This **revisits KDD-18's "never fork"** (now annotated as under research; a real move amends it with a new KDD). Also banked: the LAME `-q 4`/joint-stereo + WAV-no-RF64-at-CD-scope gotchas for the future MP3/WAV PR, the cyanrip FLAC-verify gap (whipper has `--verify`, cyanrip/FFmpeg doesn't), the FLAC `-8` opportunity, P2 ideas (AcoustID/lyrics/embedded-cuesheet/WavPack), and a KDD-13 reframe (disabling C2 is best practice, not a Linux gap). **LESSON:** "enrich metadata" looked small but the data wasn't there — MB returned none of genre/composer/disc#/ISRC in our query; the work was the MB-query design (which includes, sparse `tags` vs absent `genres`, composer-as-relationship cost), which is why field scope is a maintainer call, not a guess.
- **Archived the external EAC-1.8 archival master guide + archival-parity gap analysis (2026-06-23, user-supplied reference; "deep dive + plan, implement nothing yet").** Added `docs/archive/archival-extraction-guide-2026-06.md` (verbatim, with a provenance/caveat header; registered under a new "External reference material" heading in `docs/archive/README.md`). Ran a gap analysis of the guide vs. our whipper/cyanrip pipeline — most of the extraction layer is already aligned (KDD-13: secure read, defeat-cache, offset calibration, AccurateRip; we *don't* use C2, which the guide confirms is the archival-correct choice rather than the "Linux gap" KDD-13 framed it as). Findings worth acting on, **none implemented yet, pending approval:** (1) **cyanrip metadata richness** — `RipMetadata` + the MB→table→argv chain carries only album/artist/title/year/mbid + per-track title/artist; whipper tags itself richly from `--release-id`, so cyanrip rips are tag-poorer (no genre/disc#/total-discs/composer/ISRC); a real current "good everything" gap. (2) **cyanrip FLAC encode-verify** — whipper passes `flac --verify` (decode==PCM parity); cyanrip encodes FLAC via FFmpeg with no self-verify, so the cyanrip path may lack that bit-parity guarantee (it still does AccurateRip+EAC-CRC, a *read-side* check) — **investigate before claiming a gap.** (3) **FLAC compression level** — known KDD-13 lock at whipper's `-5`; the guide reconfirms `-8` is free quality; opportunity to expose it for cyanrip and/or an optional post-rip re-encode. (4) **Future-format institutional knowledge to capture so the P1 PRs don't reship known bugs:** LAME 3.100.1 `noise_shaping_amp`/r6147 → force `-q 4` (and never force normal stereo; keep joint-stereo) *for the standalone-lame path* — flagged **verify-before-relying**, and note the FFmpeg/libmp3lame flag mapping differs; WAV is a secondary/raw format and the 4 GB/RF64 concern does **not** apply at CD scope (a full CD ≈ 850 MB). (5) P2 ideas: WavPack hybrid (.wv/.wvc), AcoustID fingerprint fallback, lyrics, embedded FLAC cuesheet. The guide also *is* the spec for configuring the EAC ground-truth that `output_reference/` + `parity.py` compare against. v1 FLAC-only scope (Critical Rule #4) stays intact; plan delivered this session for the maintainer to prioritize.
- **Whole-codebase behaviour-preserving refactor + a doctor crash fix (2026-06-22, user: "complete refactor … when you document, note what you learned so we do not repeat").** Phased, test-guarded refactor (baseline 943 tests / 92.04 % → 950 / 92.43 %, ruff clean, suite green at every commit; each refactor commit carried `[skip changelog]`). **Merged:** the duplicated composition block in `app.py` + `preflight.default_context()` → `composition.py` (`build_backend`/`build_musicbrainz_client`/`CONTACT_URL`); the two backends' `_run_capture`/`_run` → one `run_capture()` (per-backend timeout kept on purpose); `offset_config`'s two parse paths + two file-reads → one `_iter_conf_offsets` scanner + `_read_conf_text`; the version-helper tests → three parametrized cases. **Split:** the step-engine vocabulary (`StepStatus`/`StepResult`/`CommandRunner`/`SubprocessRunner`/`StepEngine`) out of `host_setup.py` → `deps/step_engine.py`, so the teardown engine stops depending on the setup engine for its core types. **Extracted:** `workers.start_worker_thread()` for the one-shot QThread lifecycle eight sites open-coded. Trivia: `cyanrip.find_offset`'s local `import re` hoisted. Decisions + lessons in **KDD-21**; `architecture.md` §2/§5.1 and PLANNING §1/§2 synced. **Lessons (graduated):** (1) a shared helper relocates where names resolve — move the monkeypatch target with it (cyanrip's `subprocess.run` patch moved to `whipper_backend`); (2) conversely, design the helper to *keep* the patchable seam at the call site — `start_worker_thread` takes the caller's `QThread` so `module.QThread` patches still bite; (3) running the real `whipper-gui --doctor` during the QA sweep exposed a **pre-existing** crash (`_routing_failure_diagnosis` built `HostSetup()` with no `runner` on the never-tested `host=None` path → uncaught `TypeError`, aborting the doctor exactly when the backend is down). Per the refactor discipline it was fixed in its **own** commit (not folded into a refactor) with a regression test + CHANGELOG entry: `HostSetup(runner=SubprocessRunner())`.
- **Doctor >587-offset advisory (2026-06-20, "do the rest" — the safe form of offset-aware backend selection).** `check_read_offset` now WARNs when the whipper backend's *effective* offset (override value or whipper.conf value) exceeds 587 — the documented cd-paranoia bug threshold (KDD-18, the bug that failed track 3 on the BDR-209D at +667) — and recommends cyanrip, which applies the offset without it. **Deliberately advisory, not a silent auto-switch:** auto-selecting cyanrip would force a backend that (a) may not be installed and (b) isn't yet hardware-parity-proven, which contradicts the "decide the default on the parity data" conclusion. So we surface the risk and let the user choose. `_PARANOIA_OFFSET_LIMIT=587`. +2 tests (override-over-587, whipper.conf-over-587); coverage 92%.
- **Live `whipper.conf` read-offset display — trust fix (2026-06-20, user: "is the offset right?" instinct).** The read offset is the one setting that silently corrupts a rip if wrong, and for the whipper backend without Override the authoritative value is in `whipper.conf` (wizard-written or hand-edited), NOT the GUI's stored `Config.read_offset` — they can drift. Extended `offset_config.py` with a never-raises per-drive parser (`read_drive_offsets` → `[WhipperConfOffset(drive, offset)]`, decodes whipper's URL-quoted `[drive:…]` section ids; handles literal-colon ids too) + `describe_conf_offsets`. Surfaced it in **Settings** (a read-only live label under the offset field — reading the tiny file on the GUI thread is fine, it's bytes not a subprocess) and in **`--doctor`** (new `check_read_offset`: cyanrip → "applies directly via -s"; whipper+Override → the override value; whipper no-Override → the `whipper.conf` value, or WARN "none set — whipper will refuse to rip"). +13 tests (parser/formatter in `test_offset_config`, the doctor check branches in `test_preflight`); coverage 92%. Connects to the whipper-vs-cyanrip discussion: makes a drifted/missing offset visible before a rip.
- **Preflight backend-routing drilldown (2026-06-20, user: "keep improving code-side").** Enriched `check_backend_routing`: when `backend.version()` fails, a new `routing_drilldown(backend_name, host)` walks the host→container→backend chain (reusing `HostSetup`'s presence checks — `distrobox_present`/`backend_present`/`container_exists`/`{whipper,cyanrip}_in_container`/`_exported`) and names the *first* broken link ("Distrobox not installed" / "no container backend" / "'ripping' container does not exist" / "not installed inside the container" / "not exported to ~/.local/bin" / "present but version command failed"), so a failed doctor is actionable rather than a bare "unreachable". The raw backend error is preserved in the detail (never hide information); the drilldown never raises; the real `HostSetup` is built lazily only on the failure path (injectable for tests). Confirmed both backends' `version()`/`list_drives()` are already timeout-bounded (whipper + cyanrip `_run` raise `WhipperError` on `_INFO_TIMEOUT_S`), so the doctor can't hang. +9 tests (every drilldown branch + the never-raises guard); preflight.py still 99%, suite 922→931, coverage 92%.
- **Preflight / "doctor" first-pass environment test SHIPPED (2026-06-20, user: "make something that can do a first pass test even without a CD … i will run it").** New `src/whipper_gui/preflight.py` runs everything the rip pipeline needs *except* the disc read, so it works with no CD inserted: `check_settings`, `check_output_dir` (writability of the nearest existing ancestor), `check_dependencies` (reuses the ONE `DependencyManager` — Critical Rule #6), **`check_backend_routing`** (calls `backend.version()` → for whipper this enters the Distrobox container, so it proves the host→container→whipper chain — Critical Rule #3, the highest-value no-disc test), `check_drives` (a drive lists fine empty; shows read offsets), `check_drive_access` (permissions via `diagnose_drive_access`), and host reachability for **MusicBrainz** (via the real adapter — empty-list/404 = reachable, `MusicBrainzQueryError` = down), **Cover Art Archive** (a plain HTTP ping — the cover_art adapter intentionally never-raises so it can't distinguish down-vs-404), and **CTDB** (via the adapter with a synthetic structurally-valid `DiscToc`). Each check **never raises** (fail-safe like the parsers); results carry status + detail + a remediation hint. Logic lives in the package (testable, reuses real adapters); `scripts/preflight.py` and a new **`whipper-gui --doctor`** flag (runs before QApplication — a terminal diagnostic, not a window) are thin CLIs over `default_context()` + `run_preflight()` with streamed per-check output and a pass/warn/blocker verdict (exit non-zero on a blocker). `--no-network` and `--backend` flags. +38 tests (every check pass+fail via injected fakes, orchestration, rendering, both composition-root branches, and the `--doctor` path); preflight.py 99% covered; suite 884→922, total coverage 92%. **KEY VALUE:** knocks out the boring environmental failure modes before a disc goes in; a bit-perfect rip still needs the real-hardware run (standing gate). Followed Rule #7: CHANGELOG bullet, this entry, run-command docs in CLAUDE.md, and a pre-hardware step in `docs/test-plan.md`.
- **Added Anthropic / Claude Code support contacts to CLAUDE.md (2026-06-20, user: "include for appropriate circumstances").** New *Project operations → Getting help (Claude Code / Anthropic)* subsection: fastest channel is the support messenger at support.anthropic.com (or signed-in via Claude.ai / Console → "Get help"), with topic-specific emails (usersafety@ / security@ / privacy@) and the Bedrock/Vertex carve-out. Scoped explicitly to **the AI tooling itself, not Whipper GUI end-user support** (app questions go to the maintainer). Pure documentation-reference addition, no behavior change → `[skip changelog]`.
- **Graduated the doc-enforcement design to KDD-20 (2026-06-20, user: "put these in their places so we know in the future if asked").** The previous entry shipped the enforcement *mechanism*; this completes the graduation by giving its *design + rationale* a canonical home a future reader will find: **KDD-20 in `PLANNING.md`** (the anchor/detail/backstop three-layer design, the "why the autoloaded file is the lever" reasoning, and the gate's own self-match bug). Added a discoverability pointer in `docs/architecture.md §6` (release/packaging, where CHANGELOG-rolling lives) and bumped the `CLAUDE.md` companion-docs reference KDD-19→KDD-20. This commit is a pure historical-record graduation (no behavior change; the feature's CHANGELOG bullet already landed), so it carries a `[skip changelog]` line — also exercising the CI gate's opt-out path for real.
- **Documentation-currency enforcement — CLAUDE.md is the autoloaded anchor (2026-06-20, user: "how do we make it part of the procedure every time?").** Root-caused why this session's docs drifted: the CHANGELOG stayed current because CLAUDE.md gives it a per-commit rule *with teeth*; the session-log + lesson-graduation had only soft "do it at milestones" framing, so they got deferred. Fix per the user's "daisy-chain off the one autoloaded file" insight: **CLAUDE.md gained Critical Rule #7 — "Documentation currency is part of Done"** (the always-loaded anchor: CHANGELOG bullet in the same commit; session-log entry before session end; graduate every durable lesson to its home; full checklist in `docs/testing.md §6`). The locked rules section was edited *with explicit user authorization* (the deviation policy requires it). `docs/testing.md §6` Definition of Done gained the matching CHANGELOG + session-log/graduation checklist items (the concrete list the anchor points at). CI gained a `changelog` job (mechanical backstop: fails a push/PR with no `CHANGELOG.md` change; opt out with `[skip changelog]` for pure historical-record commits; range-aware for both push and PR, all-zero-base safe). **KEY LESSON (now itself encoded as Rule #7):** for an agent, the highest-leverage enforcement is the always-loaded CLAUDE.md — a soft rule anywhere else gets deferred; give it the same per-change bite as the CHANGELOG rule, and chain the rest off it. Docs + CI only; no app code.
- **Off-thread dep-check cross-thread bug + cancel startup-race fix (2026-06-18→20, found by post-smoke review).** Two correctness bugs, each with a verified regression test. **(1) Launch dep-check built widgets on the worker thread.** `run_dependency_check_async` connected `worker.finished` to a **lambda** capturing the per-call `gui_manager`; a signal connected to a plain callable (no QObject context) defaults to **DirectConnection**, so `_apply_dependency_report` — which builds `ManualInstallDialog`/resolver widgets — ran on the *emitting worker thread*, off the GUI thread (Qt: "QObject::setParent: ... in a different thread"). Fix: connect a **bound method of the window** (`self._dep_check_worker.finished.connect(self._on_dependency_check_done)`) → AutoConnection → QueuedConnection → runs on the GUI thread; stash the manager on `self._dep_check_manager` for the handler to read/clear. The app-startup smoke test (below) caught this in the wild; regression test asserts `applied_on_main_thread == [True]` (fails `[False]` without the fix). **(2) Cancel during the rip-spawn window did nothing.** `RipWorker.cancel()` only stops the subprocess if `self._handle` is set; a Cancel landing *during* `backend.rip()` spawn set the flag but never killed the child → `wait()` blocked. Fix: re-check `self._cancelled` right after `self._handle` is assigned in `start_rip` and `cancel()` then if so. Regression test asserts `handle.cancel_calls == 1` after pre-start cancel + start (fails `0` without the fix). **KEY LESSONS (graduated to `docs/architecture.md` §3.2):** connect cross-thread signals to a **bound QObject method, never a lambda/free function** (lambda → DirectConnection on the emitting thread); and **cancellation must re-check the flag right after acquiring the process handle** (the spawn window is a real race).
- **App-startup smoke test made permanent (2026-06-18, user: "yes make it permanent").** `tests/test_app_smoke.py` runs the REAL `app.main([])` headless under offscreen Qt — the composition root, real adapters, one turn of the real event loop — which nothing else exercised (unit tests build `MainWindow` with fakes). Hermetic: config redirected to a tmp dir via monkeypatch (`config.CONFIG_DIR/CONFIG_PATH`), `configure_logging` no-op'd, the subprocess version-probe layer stubbed to "all missing" (so the resolver-dialog path — where the cross-thread bug lived — runs with no real whipper/flatpak/container/network), `list_drives` stubbed to `[]`, every modal neutered, and `QApplication.exec` patched to a `fake_exec` that pumps events, introspects the window (menus + the five core widgets present), then `close()`s. It asserts the app composes, the launch dep-check applies **on the main thread**, and **no cross-thread Qt warnings** fire (captured via `qInstallMessageHandler`). This test found the dep-check bug above on first run. `docs/testing.md` gained a "Startup smoke" tier row.
- **output_reference/ EAC baseline + parity checker (2026-06-18, user-specified matrix & policy).** Stood up `output_reference/` as the home for backend×format rip proofs: `EAC_flac/` holds the committed EAC ground truth (`eac_baseline_police_classics.{log,cue}`, moved out of `tests/fixtures/`); `EAC_{wav,mp3}/`, `whipper_{flac,wav,mp3}/`, `cyanrip_{flac,wav,mp3}/` are placeholder-README'd, to be filled **only when parity is reached** (user policy, now documented in `output_reference/README.md`: **the EAC baseline uploads now; other backends' logs upload only as passing examples; never commit commercial audio** — public repo + copyright + bloat, and the per-track CRCs prove bit-perfection without the audio).
