Tooling: `src/whipper_gui/parsers/eac_log.py` (`looks_like_eac_log`, `parse_eac_copy_crcs` — EAC `Track N` → `Copy CRC HHHHHHHH`; never raises; BOM-tolerant); `src/whipper_gui/parity.py` (`track_copy_crcs` sniffs cyanrip/EAC/whipper and dispatches to the existing parsers; `compare_logs` → `ParityReport`); `scripts/eac_parity.py` CLI prints a PASS/FAIL per-track table and exits non-zero on any mismatch. TASKS gained an "EAC output-parity proof matrix" section, ordered **FLAC (1) / WAV (2) / MP3 (3)**. The proofs themselves are hardware-gated (rip the Police disc with whipper/cyanrip on the BDR-209D, run the checker, commit the passing log).
- **v0.2.7 + v0.2.8 released; PyPI publish trigger fixed (2026-06-18, user: "cut 0.2.8" / "fix it").** Cut two releases via the standard cloud `workflow_dispatch` path; watched both to completion and confirmed assets. **Fixed a long-standing PyPI-publish miss (v0.2.4–v0.2.7 never published):** `gh release create` runs under `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and GitHub **suppresses workflow runs for events triggered by `GITHUB_TOKEN`** — so the `release: published` trigger on `publish-pypi.yml` never fired. `workflow_dispatch`/`repository_dispatch` are the **exception** (they always run, even via `GITHUB_TOKEN`), so `release.yml` now explicitly dispatches `publish-pypi.yml` (`gh workflow run publish-pypi.yml --ref "$TAG"`, with `actions: write` added) and `publish-pypi.yml` switched to `workflow_dispatch:`-only (+ `skip-existing: true`). Verified on v0.2.8: publish-pypi ran (event `workflow_dispatch`) and failed only at the OIDC `invalid-publisher` step — expected, because the PyPI **Trusted Publisher is the maintainer's one-time setup** (test-plan Test 7), not a code bug. **KEY FACT (now in this log + release notes):** never rely on a `release:`/`push:` trigger to chain CI from a GITHUB_TOKEN-authored action — chain it with `workflow_dispatch`.
- **flac registered as an optional dependency + test hardening, coverage floor 88→90 (2026-06-18).** Added `deps/checks.py::check_flac` (mirrors `check_metaflac`) and a `flac` `DependencySpec` (optional, `Tier.MANUAL`) in `deps/registry.py` so the CTDB PCM-decode path's `flac` need routes through the **single** dependency subsystem (Critical Rule #6), not an ad-hoc check. Did a hardening pass (failure-path + cross-thread coverage) and **ratcheted the CI coverage floor 88 → 90** (`--cov-fail-under=90`); updated `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/testing.md` to match. The gate only ever ratchets up.
- **CTDB verify wired into the GUI (opt-in, experimental) + close-crash fix (2026-06-18, TASKS #8).** Surfaced the Phase-1 CTDB verify library in the UI behind `Config.ctdb_verify_after_rip` (default off; Settings checkbox "Verify with CTDB after a rip (experimental)"). The rip-progress panel gained a CTDB status/result line (`set_ctdb_status`/`set_ctdb_result`; `ctdb_verdict_line` labels a MATCH as **EXPERIMENTAL** while `crc.CRC_VALIDATED` is still False — fail-safe, never a false "verified"). `workers/ctdb_worker.py` was converted from a `QObject` worker to a plain `verify_rip_dir(...)` function (no Qt) so it can run on the post-rip **daemon thread**, joining the post-rip tagging/cover-art thread first (`wait_for=`) so it reads settled files; it never raises. **Close-crash fix:** the first cut ran CTDB on a `QThread(self)` joined in `closeEvent` with `wait(3000)` — but CTDB decode can run far longer than 3 s, and **destroying a running `QThread` aborts the whole app**. Fixed by moving it to a **daemon `threading.Thread` + a queued `ctdb_verify_done` signal**, NOT joined in `closeEvent` (daemon threads die with the process). Regression test: window closes mid-verify without blocking. **KEY LESSON (graduated to `docs/architecture.md` §3.2):** for long, non-cancellable post-rip work, prefer a **daemon thread + queued signal** over a `closeEvent`-joined `QThread` — you can't safely bound-wait something that may outlive the wait, and destroying it on close is fatal.
- **Post-rip metaflac tagging moved off the GUI thread — GUI-thread sweep truly COMPLETE (2026-06-18, TASKS #11d).** The last synchronous blocking call: post-rip metaflac **tagging** ran on the GUI thread in `_on_rip_finished`. Moved it to a daemon thread mirroring the cover-art pattern. **Critical subtlety — the metaflac race:** tagging *and* cover-art embedding both run `metaflac` on the **same** FLAC files; running them on two independent daemon threads would race (concurrent metaflac on one file). Fix: a **single** post-rip daemon thread runs them **sequentially** — `_start_post_rip_processing(rip_dir, *, tag, launch_picard, release_id, embed, save_file)` does tagging *then* cover art (renamed `_cover_art_thread`→`_post_rip_thread`). `run_unknown_post_processing(rip_dir, launch_picard)` stays as the synchronous tagging worker body (tests call it directly). +behavioral regression test. With this, **every** path that enters the container or hashes/copies files is off the GUI thread — the responsiveness sweep (#11a–d) is done.
- **Documentation consolidation audit (2026-06-17, user-requested "combine as much as possible, reduce document count").** Full audit of all 21 docs via three parallel read-only subagents (testing cluster / research+investigation cluster / core-architecture cluster); found duplication concentrated in three places and consolidated **docs/ 15 → 9** (+ a new `docs/archive/`). Merges: **`best-practices.md` → `architecture.md`** (the threading discipline, never-raise parsers, and testing contract were near-verbatim in both; one canonical home per pattern now, with subprocess/error-logging/packaging/release/security depth + a consolidated References section folded in); **`release-testing.md` → `test-plan.md`** (renamed in spirit to "Manual & release testing": the EAC CRC baseline lived in both — now once, in Part B; uninstall/cyanrip/offset content collapsed to cross-references; kept the `test-plan.md` filename so the shipping-source comments that point at it stay valid); **`whipper-gui-research-rerun-prompt.md` → `whipper-gui-session-start.md` Step 0**. Archived (user chose archive over delete) the three dated investigations — `ecosystem-audit-2026-06`, `offset-investigation-2026-06`, `upstream-modification-investigation` — into `docs/archive/` with a README mapping each to where its durable conclusions graduated (KDD-18 / DEPENDENCIES / KDD-14-16 / adapter comments); the CTDB Phase-1 spec is preserved there and still pointed to from KDD-14. Slimmed `PLANNING.md §3` (was shadowing the `DEPENDENCIES.md` pin table) to a pointer. Updated every cross-reference: `docs/README.md` SSOT map + index, `CLAUDE.md` companion-docs list + graduation rule (references only — locked rules untouched, per the user's explicit OK), root `README.md` contributor list, PLANNING/TASKS/CHANGELOG links, and **five shipping-source comments** (`host_setup.py`, `cyanrip_backend.py`, `accuraterip_offsets.py`, `ctdb_client.py`, `ctdb/toc.py`) that named the moved investigation docs, plus `tests/test_gui_thread_discipline.py`'s pointer (`best-practices.md §5` → `architecture.md §3.2`). No code logic changed; suite + lint expected green.
- **Pre-test audit + debug-logging mode (2026-06-14, user-requested before they test).** Ran a second full audit (a read-only subagent over the new threading code + general correctness). It surfaced **one real bug**, now fixed: a **stale disc-info result** — when the user switches drives, the old probe's queued `finished`/`failed` could arrive after the new probe started and clobber the new drive's display; `_on_disc_info_ready`/`_on_disc_info_failed` now ignore a result whose device != the current selection (`_is_stale_disc_result`), + regression test. The audit's two "medium" flags were verified non-bugs (the dep `report.missing` mutation is on a fresh per-call report; the `_on_dependency_check_done` lambda captures a *local* `gui_manager`, distinct per call — not a shared attr). **New feature — Debug logging toggle:** `Config.debug_logging` (default off) + a Settings checkbox; `logging_setup` file handler is now INFO by default and DEBUG when on (`configure_logging(debug=)` + runtime `set_debug_logging()`), applied at launch (app.py, after config load) and immediately on Settings save. Verbose log for bug reports → `~/.local/share/whipper-gui/log.txt`. Help (Troubleshooting) + CHANGELOG updated. +7 tests (logging ×4, settings ×1, config ×1, stale-result ×1); 814→821. **Note:** user has EAC FLAC files but hasn't uploaded them here yet — offered offline CRC validation if they do.
- **Release/acceptance testing doc + help-file refresh (2026-06-14, user-requested ahead of onboarding external testers).** Audited the in-app User Guide (`help_content.py`) — current on cover art / cyanrip / in-app updates; fixed the stale Drive-setup section (now describes the AccurateRip auto-offset, not the old `offset find` flow). New `docs/release-testing.md`: the end-to-end clean-cycle acceptance run (uninstall → fresh install → first-run → drive setup → rip → verify → update → uninstall, "exactly what to do"), the **EAC output-parity** procedure (compare per-track CRC32 to `tests/fixtures/eac_baseline_police_classics.log`; documents the parity variables — offset +667, "appended" gap handling, overread off, null-samples-in-CRC — so a CRC mismatch is diagnosable, plus the banked facts: track 3 clean in EAC = whipper >587 bug; track 5 = real disc quirk), a **Linux-distro matrix**, and a **problem-permutation matrix** (D1–D14: no FUSE, no podman, no network heal, cold container, disc-not-ready, CD-R, scratched track, unknown disc, unknown offset, cancel, update-over-path, quit-mid-op) with a reporting template. Linked from the docs index + SSOT map. Complements the deep single-feature `test-plan.md`.
- **Launch `list_drives` moved off the GUI thread — GUI-thread sweep COMPLETE (2026-06-14, TASKS #11c).** The last launch freeze: `list_drives` (whipper `drive list`, container entry) ran synchronously in `refresh_drives` after show(). Fix: `workers/drive_list_worker.py::DriveListWorker` lists on a QThread; `DrivePicker.refresh()` split into `populate(drives)` + `show_error(msg)` (the population/error halves) so the launch path can fetch off-thread then populate on the GUI thread; `refresh_drives` is now async (single-flight, joined in closeEvent + teardown). The picker's Refresh button stays synchronous (user-initiated). **Net: the whole launch path is non-blocking** — all three container-entering probes (deps #11a, drive list #11c, disc info #11b) run off-thread; only a minor post-rip metaflac-tagging nit remains. +5 tests (worker ×3, refresh async ×2). 809→814.
- **`disc_info` moved off the GUI thread (2026-06-14, TASKS #11b).** Reading the inserted disc (`disc_info()` → whipper `cd info` / cyanrip `-I`) enters the container + reads the disc — several seconds — and ran synchronously in `_on_drive_changed`, freezing the window on *every* drive selection (and at the launch auto-select). Fix: `workers/disc_info_worker.py::DiscInfoWorker` probes on a QThread; `_on_drive_changed` split into the trigger (`_start_disc_info`, which also replaces any in-flight probe) + `_on_disc_info_ready`/`_on_disc_info_failed` GUI-thread handlers. Joined in `closeEvent` + `teardown_threads`. Core drive-change tests reworked to the split shape (cascade handlers tested directly; one integration test drives the real worker via the now-safe processEvents poll); +3 `DiscInfoWorker` unit tests. 806→809. **Still open in #11:** `list_drives` (whipper `drive list`) at launch + post-rip metaflac tagging.
- **Launch dependency check moved off the GUI thread (2026-06-14, TASKS #11a; user-chosen "launch-responsiveness refactor").** A cold-container `whipper --version` probe at launch could briefly freeze the just-shown window. Fix: new `workers/dependency_worker.py::DependencyCheckWorker` runs `DependencyManager.check_all()` (pure probing) on a QThread; `DependencyMixin` split into `_build_gui_dependency_manager` + `_apply_dependency_report` (the GUI-thread half, where resolver dialogs live) + the new `run_dependency_check_async()`; `app.py` calls the async path at launch. The synchronous `run_dependency_check` (menu/tests) is unchanged. `closeEvent` joins the new thread; `teardown_threads` joins it too. **Systemic test fix:** added an autouse conftest fixture giving `QMessageBox.question/.information/.warning/.critical` safe non-blocking defaults — a modal `.exec()` blocks forever headless, and any test that pumps `processEvents()` could fire a *stale* first-run `singleShot` from an earlier window and hang the suite (the conftest hazard). This also de-flaked the E2E test. +4 tests (worker ×2, async ×2); 802→806; suite green, lint+format clean.
- **GUI-thread architectural fitness test (2026-06-13).** Codified the "never block the GUI thread" rule (the freeze bug class) as an executable guard: `tests/test_gui_thread_discipline.py` AST-parses every `ui/` module and fails if any makes a synchronous blocking call (`subprocess.run`/`check_output`/`check_call`/`call`, any `urlopen`, `time.sleep`). `ui/` is clean today, so it locks the discipline in; `QThread.wait()`/`join()` at teardown are deliberately allowed. Ships with a meta-test proving the guard isn't a no-op (detects a planted offender, ignores a mention in a string/comment). `docs/testing.md` §4 gained an "architectural fitness tests" technique entry. Tests 778→802.
- **End-to-end test tier added + testing-template update (2026-06-13, user-requested "test end to end").** Researched current PySide6 GUI-testing practice (pytest-qt `qtbot.waitSignal` is the standard for worker-thread flows) and added `tests/test_e2e_rip_pipeline.py` — the suite's first true **end-to-end** test: it builds the real assembled `MainWindow` (all mixins, real signals, a real `RipWorker` on a real `QThread`) and fakes only the external edge (ripper subprocess, MusicBrainz, cover-art HTTP, metaflac), then drives one full unknown-album rip and asserts the whole "good music, good cover, good everything" outcome — tagged FLACs + embedded *and* saved cover art + a fidelity verdict. **Chose to stay dependency-free** (no pytest-qt) and wait via a bounded `qApp.processEvents()` poll. Two hard-won gotchas, now documented in `docs/testing.md` §2/§4: (1) **don't `QThread.wait()` on the GUI thread** — the worker's `finished→thread.quit` is queued *to the GUI thread*, so blocking it deadlocks; poll `processEvents` instead; (2) **suppress first-run offers** (`host_setup_prompted=True`, …) before pumping events, or a deferred `_maybe_offer_*` `QMessageBox.exec()` blocks forever headless. Tests 777→778; `docs/testing.md` gained the end-to-end tier + the threaded-flow pattern; README count refreshed.
- **Full code + documentation audit (2026-06-13, user-requested).** Two parallel read-only audit agents (docs-accuracy + code-consistency/correctness), then fixes. **Docs:** PLANNING.md + TASKS.md still showed the *mid*-refactor state — reconciled the module tree (added the three mixin modules + per-module entries), the `main_window.py` description (all six mixins, ~460 lines), KDD-19 status (COMPLETE), and TASKS #12/#13; dropped a stale test-file count. **Code:** audit found **zero correctness bugs, zero dead code, no GUI-thread-blocking regressions, and all four large files genuinely single-responsibility** (whipper_backend / host_setup / rip_worker / settings_dialog — left intact, cohesion over line count). Fixed the only real findings — four type-hint gaps (`_default_open`, `_build_host_setup`, `_show_dep_summary` param, `_on_update_install_finished` `dialog`) — and a **CI-red `ruff format` lapse** in `tests/test_ui_main_window.py` left by the earlier patch-target script-edits. Suite green (777), lint+format clean, coverage ~90%.
- **Claude-docs efficiency pass — lean CLAUDE.md + single-source-of-truth map (2026-06-13, user-requested).** The standards docs had grown to repeat the same lessons across files, and `CLAUDE.md` (loaded into every session) was ~⅓ chronological session log. Fixed: this session log moved out of `CLAUDE.md` into `docs/session-log.md` (CLAUDE.md 244→167 lines, now pure durable instructions + a pointer); added a **single-source-of-truth map** to `docs/README.md` (which doc owns which kind of content) plus the **graduation rule** (a lesson lives as a one-line *rule* in its canonical home + a dated *entry* here — distillation up, chronology down; anything more is duplication to delete). Goal: docs efficient + consistent so human and LLM can use *and* modify them without re-tangling. No code touched; suite green.
- **MainWindow decomposition COMPLETE (2026-06-13).** Finished the KDD-19 mixin split: `ProvisioningMixin`, `DriveMixin`, `DependencyMixin` extracted (after helpers/`UpdateMixin`/`RipMixin`). `main_window.py` 1707→459 (a pure assembler) over six focused modules (rip 504 · provision 251 · deps 231 · update 211 · drive 192 · helpers 115). Each extraction test-guarded (777 green); Drive needed the patch-where-the-code-lives fix again (repointed `is_offset_configured`/`diagnose_drive_access` test patches); Provisioning/Dependency tests used source-module patches and needed none. `_DialogQueuedResolver` moved to `main_window_deps` and re-exported for the test API.
- **Test-suite hardening + staged MainWindow decomposition + docs/standards pass (2026-06-13, user-requested "full refactor").** Three threads of work, all test-guarded (777 tests green at every commit, branch coverage ~90%): **(1) Tests** — added `tests/test_update_worker.py` (the update workers were at 0%→100%), `check_libdiscid` ctypes-probe tests, cover-art failure-path tests; consolidated 7 near-identical whipper flag tests into 2 parametrized ones; the redundancy audit found NO dead/obsolete tests. **(2) GUI-thread freeze class** — beyond the update freeze, fixed `_mark_trusted` (gio) → fire-and-forget Popen, and moved the launch dependency check (which enters the container via `whipper --version`) to *after* `window.show()`. Remaining: fully off-thread the launch probes (TASKS #11). **(3) MainWindow decomposition** — split the 1707-line god-object via **mixins** it inherits: `main_window_helpers.py` (pure fns), `main_window_update.py` (`UpdateMixin`), `main_window_rip.py` (`RipMixin` — rip lifecycle/eject/force-stop/cover art). Now ~1008 lines; still to extract: Provisioning/Drive/Dependency mixins → target ~300-line assembler. **KEY LESSON (now in docs/architecture.md + testing.md): when you move code between modules, move its monkeypatch targets too** — tests patching `mw.is_offset_configured`/`mw.RipWorker`/`mw.drive_control` silently missed after the move and one test started a REAL rip thread (abort); patch where the code lives, or patch the function's source module and call it module-qualified. New: `docs/architecture.md` (contributor guide + extension recipes). CLAUDE.md gained the GUI-thread rule, the mixin convention, and a "Working with the maintainer (learned)" section.
- **FIRST live in-app-update test DONE (v0.2.4→v0.2.5) — found 3 real UX bugs, all fixed (2026-06-13).** The update functionally WORKED (download+verify+swap succeeded, v0.2.5 installed) but the UX was bad: **(1) the window went "Not Responding" frozen at 100% for ~minutes** — root cause: `_on_update_install_finished` calls `ai.integrate()` → `_default_refresh()` which ran `kbuildsycoca6`/`update-desktop-database` **synchronously on the GUI thread** (each 30s timeout); the blocked event loop also made **(2) the Cancel button do nothing** (post-download there's nothing to cancel anyway) and **(3) the X take ages**. Fixes: `_default_refresh` is now **fire-and-forget `Popen(start_new_session=True)`** (never waits — menu cache catches up on its own); `download_and_install` gained a **`status` callback** emitting phase labels (Downloading→Verifying→"Installing — almost done, please don't close…") so the quick post-download steps don't look frozen; the progress dialog **retires its Cancel button once the un-cancellable install phase starts** (`setCancelButton(None)`). Tests: +3 (refresh-non-blocking, status-phases, kept the suite green at 88.96%). **NOTE: these fixes ship in the NEXT release (v0.2.6+); the user's current v0.2.4 still has the frozen-looking updater — but the swap completes regardless, so v0.2.5 IS installed; just relaunch from the menu.** Re the user's "main progress bar doesn't work": that's the main-window *Overall/task RIP* bars, which are correctly idle (0%) during an update — only the dialog's bar is relevant then; confirm with the user, not a bug.
- **v0.2.5 PUBLISHED (2026-06-13).** Cut autonomously via the workflow_dispatch path (tag v0.2.5, target main `aa36373`, all 5 assets incl. the `.zsync`, pre-release auto-marked). v0.2.5 is the first release to carry backend-independent cover art. Release decision was the user's (chose "cut now" over holding the v0.2.4 control); publishing handled autonomously.

As built: `adapters/cover_art.py` — `fetch_front_cover` GETs CAA `/front` (original full-res; stdlib urllib; injectable fetcher; magic-byte sniff rejects HTML error pages; every failure → None), `plan_actions(mode, ripper_fetches_art, release_id)` is the pure gate (whipper-with-release-id keeps using `--cover-art`; no release ID → no-op), `apply_cover_art` writes `cover.<ext>`, embeds via new `MetaflacAdapter.embed_picture` (removes existing PICTURE blocks first — no duplicate covers on re-rip), unlinks the file when only embedding was asked, returns a one-line outcome. MainWindow runs it on a daemon thread post-rip (`_start_cover_art_fetch`; `cover_art_done` signal → rip log view, NOT the status line — that holds the fidelity verdict); **`window._cover_art_fetcher` must be stubbed in any test that drives a successful identified-disc rip finish on the cyanrip backend** (same hang-the-suite gotcha as `_begin_update_install`). - **In-app self-update + per-file integration re-offer (2026-06-10, real-user feedback from the v0.2.1→v0.2.2 update walkthrough; ships as v0.2.3).** Three reports: old menu entry kept launching the old file; the integration offer never re-appeared for the new version (the one-time `appimage_integration_prompted` bool suppressed it FOREVER); and the user asked for update-without-manual-download + self-restart. **(1) KDD-17b AMENDED (user-requested reversal of "never hand-roll the download"):** new `update_install.py` — fetch published `.sha256` FIRST (gate), stream the AppImage to `<dest>/.whipper-gui-update.part` (1 MiB chunks, progress + cancel polled between chunks), verify sha256, chmod, `Path.replace()` atomically over `~/Applications/whipper-gui-x86_64.AppImage`; injectable opener, every failure → UpdateInstallError + .part cleanup, existing install never touched. `UpdateInstallWorker` + QProgressDialog in MainWindow (`_begin_update_install`/`_on_update_install_finished`); on success: `ai.integrate(new_path)` (repoints menu), offer restart → `Popen([new_path], start_new_session=True)` + `close()`. closeEvent cancels+joins the install thread. - **AppImage self-relocation to ~/Applications (2026-06-10, real-user feedback: menu entry pointing into Downloads "defeats the purpose").** `appimage_integration.relocate_to_applications()` MOVES (not copies — no stale duplicate) the AppImage to `~/Applications` (`APPLICATIONS_DIR`, AppImageLauncher convention; `CANONICAL_APPIMAGE_NAME` constant) before `integrate()` points the entries at it; never raises — already-there → no-op, failed move → integrate in place. Moving the RUNNING AppImage is safe on Linux (runtime holds the mount/fd); the session keeps working, future launches use the menu. Both UI entry points (first-run offer, Tools → Add app shortcut) relocate-then-integrate, and the prompt text explains the move. `HostTeardown._appimage_targets()` now also targets the canonical ~/Applications copy (step included whenever it exists, even with $APPIMAGE unset). **Test gotcha: the production flow calls the REAL relocate unless stubbed — two older wiring tests actually moved their tmp files into ~/Applications until `monkeypatch.setattr(ai, "relocate_to_applications", lambda p: p)` was added; stub it in any test that drives the integration flow.** Tests 714→719 (+5 new).

- **In-app Uninstaller SHIPPED (2026-06-09, current-plan item 4; standing user request).** `deps/host_teardown.py` (`HostTeardown`) = reverse arm of the deps subsystem, mirroring host_setup: idempotent steps **shortcuts → exports → container → whipper_config → appimage → app_data (ALWAYS last — log survives a failed step)**; injectable CommandRunner + `remove_file`/`remove_tree`; dry-run; **keep-contract test-pinned** — the only mutating command ever issued is `distrobox rm --force ripping` (distrobox/podman/music are never targets). `ui/uninstall_dialog.py` (Tools menu, after a separator): confirm gate + checkboxes (container, whipper.conf; AppImage step only when `$APPIMAGE` is set via `appimage_integration.appimage_path()`); on success MainWindow offers `close()` since the config dir is gone (staying open could recreate it). `HostSetupWorker` now types against a `StepEngine` Protocol so ONE worker drives both engines. `uninstall.sh` does cyanrip-export cleanup too (parity). **Test gotcha worth remembering: never call `qapp.processEvents()` in a widget test** — it fires stale deferred events from earlier tests' destroyed windows (here: a dead window's `_maybe_offer_host_setup` single-shot → hard abort); join threads via the dialog's `_stop()` instead. AccurateRip: N/M…" — NEVER claims Test/Copy CRCs (no dual read in cyanrip). - **cyanrip progress parsing SHIPPED (2026-06-09, current-plan item 1).** Bars/status/track-highlight now live during cyanrip rips. Key fact that made it cheap: cyanrip redraws ONE line with bare `\r` (`cyanrip_main.c`: `"Ripping%strack %i, progress - %0.2f%%"` + optional `", ETA - …"`/`", errors - %i"`), and **`Popen(text=True)` universal-newlines translates bare `\r` to `\n`** — so each redraw already arrives as its own line via `log_lines()`; no streaming changes (verified empirically with printf). `RipWorker` gained three cyanrip regexes (`_CYANRIP_TRACK_PROGRESS/_TRACK_DONE/_DISC_TRACKS` — total comes from the start report since progress lines don't carry it) mapped into the same 5–95% band; `_describe_activity` renders "Ripping track N… X% (ETA Ym)". No-total fallback: overall holds at the 5% floor, task bar still moves. TASKS.md P1.1 zero-CLI checkboxes contradicted ranked-item 7 (a/c shipped but unticked) — fixed; noted "export flac from container for CTDB" is NOT done. **TASKS.md now has a "⭐ Current plan & priorities" section — the live ordered queue with S/M/L difficulty + HW (hardware-gated) tags; keep it current instead of growing the historical ranked list.** Top of queue: (1) cyanrip stdout progress parsing M, (2) cyanrip log fidelity verdict M, (3) HW parity run, (4) in-app Uninstaller M/L (standing user request: keep distrobox/podman + music), (5) desktop icons/Multimedia S, (6) AppImage self-update M. - **cyanrip metadata model + templates + unified Settings SHIPPED (2026-06-09, KDD-18 phase 5).** The "most urgent to a running program" slice: a cyanrip rip from the GUI now tags and names correctly. **Metadata model decided: feed, don't self-lookup** — every rip snapshots the track table into `RipMetadata` (new frozen dataclass + `metadata=` param on the `rip()` ABC; whipper impl `del`s it, it tags from `--release-id`); cyanrip ALWAYS gets `-N` + `-a`/`-t` built by `_metadata_args` (no wrong-release risk, no in-container network — the known flaky spot; honors Critical Rule #5). **Escaping matters:** cyanrip parses -a/-t with FFmpeg `av_dict_parse_string(.., "=", ":")` whose tokenizer (`av_get_token`) treats `\` as escape and `'` as quote → `_escape_meta_value` backslash-escapes `\ : = '`. **Templates:** `scheme_from_template` maps whipper `%`-tokens→cyanrip `{…}` (dir part of track_template→`-D`, file part→`-F`; verified in cyanrip source that scheme-literal `/` nests dirs while tag-value `/` is sanitized; stray `{}` flattened to `()` so they're not parsed as tag keys; unknown `%x` kept visibly). disc_template is unused for cyanrip (log/cue land in -D already). **Unified Settings (user requirement 2026-06-09: one UI across backends, grey out + explain, never hide/lose):** `_apply_backend_capabilities` gates whipper-only widgets (CD-R, cover art, overread, keep-going, whipper-path row) live on backend-combo change; disabled tooltip = original + "Read-only: <why> … switch back to whipper to edit; value kept"; `to_config()` still reads disabled widgets so nothing is cleared. Cover art is whipper-only *by consequence* of -N (cyanrip's art comes from its own MB/CAA lookup). Tests 650→663. **Remaining KDD-18:** cyanrip stdout progress parsing (bars sit still during a cyanrip rip — cosmetic; exit code + .log discovery already work since cyanrip writes `Album.log` under the -D folder), cyanrip-log fidelity verdict (EAC CRC/AccurateRip lines), hardware parity run.
- **cyanrip `disc_info` SHIPPED (2026-06-09, KDD-18 phase 4).** `CyanripImpl.disc_info` runs **`-I -N`** — info-only AND offline, because cyanrip computes the MusicBrainz DiscID (SHA-1 per MB spec) and CDDB ID **locally from the TOC** (`src/discid.c`, filled unconditionally before the `disable_mb` check) — so identification needs no container network and the GUI's host-side MB lookup keeps doing the metadata (Critical Rule #5). New `parsers/cyanrip_info.py` parses the start report into the shared backend-neutral `DiscInfo`; exact labels (`Disc tracks:`/`DiscID:`/`CDDB ID:`, `MusicBrainz URL:` with the URL on the NEXT line, "(null)" guarded) verified against cyanrip master `cyanrip_log.c::cyanrip_log_start_report`/`cyanrip_main.c`. A failed `-I` (no disc) just prints errors → parser degrades to empty DiscInfo = the GUI's existing "unknown disc" path; note `CyanripImpl._run` ignores exit codes by design. Never-raises property test added per the institutional rule. Tests 638→650. **Remaining KDD-18:** `-D/-F` template mapping, metadata model (`-a`/`-t` feed vs self-lookup), hardware parity run. **Merged to `main` at the user's request (2026-06-09) so stale branches can be deleted.**
- **cyanrip container packaging SHIPPED (2026-06-09, KDD-18 phase 3).** Resolved the blocked research: **Fedora does NOT package cyanrip** (packages.fedoraproject.org: 0 results F43/F44/rawhide/EPEL), neither does RPM Fusion; the one prebuilt source is **COPR `barsnick/non-fed`** (cyanrip **0.9.3.1**, succeeded builds for fedora-42/43/44+rawhide x86_64, GPG-signed). Wizard impl: `HostSetup.include_cyanrip` (on when `Config.ripper_backend == "cyanrip"`; MainWindow injects via `_build_host_setup()`) adds a "cyanrip" step between tools and export — writes the standard COPR `.repo` stanza in-container (passed to `sh -c` as a **positional "$1" argument** so `$releasever` is never shell-expanded; avoids the `dnf copr` plugin, which dnf4/dnf5 ship differently), `dnf install -y cyanrip`, and the export step also exports `/usr/bin/cyanrip` → `~/.local/bin/cyanrip` (`paths.CYANRIP_BINARY_DEFAULT`). The COPR is enabled **only inside the disposable container**, never the host; fallback if it vanishes = meson source build (all build deps in Fedora proper) — written up in the audit doc's new "Packaging research" section. UX: switching Settings→cyanrip offers the wizard when cyanrip is missing (`_maybe_offer_cyanrip_install`; presence check `host_setup.cyanrip_on_host()` lives in the deps subsystem per Critical Rule #6); app.py prefers the exported absolute path (desktop launches have minimal PATH). Tests 625→638. **Follow-ups queued in TASKS #9:** setup-host.sh `--cyanrip` parity, uninstall removes `~/.local/bin/cyanrip`. **Remaining KDD-18:** `-I` disc-info parsing, `-D/-F` template mapping, hardware parity run (a real wizard run on hardware is the final proof, as usual).
- **cyanrip backend Phase 1 SHIPPED (2026-06-08, KDD-18) — triggered by a real bug.** Hardware testing the Police disc surfaced whipper's cd-paranoia **>587 read-offset bug** (whipper warns about it itself): the BDR-209D needs +667, and track 3 failed verification 5× → whole rip aborted. (The earlier blockers were all fixed first: offset auto-applied + actually passed as `--offset`; the "container has no network" NetworkError handled by an **autonomous heal** that re-rips as `--unknown` and tags locally — the user's metadata comes from the GUI's host-side MB lookup; and a clear "track unreadable" message.) cyanrip applies the offset with its OWN paranoia (`-s`), so it avoids the >587 bug — the concrete reason to start the backend. Built `adapters/cyanrip_backend.py` (`CyanripImpl`) behind the `WhipperBackend` ABC, `Config.ripper_backend` selector wired in `app.py` (default whipper). Tested core: rip argv (`-d/-s/-o flac/-r/-N/-G`, cwd=output_dir), `version`, `find_offset` (`-f`), backend-independent `/dev`+sysfs drive scan. **Remaining (docs/ecosystem-audit-2026-06.md):** `-I` disc-info parsing, `-D/-F` naming-template mapping, the release-id-vs-self-MB metadata model, a Settings toggle, container packaging of cyanrip, and the hardware parity run. NEVER fork whipper. Tests 610→621.
- **Real-hardware rip mostly works now (2026-06-08):** end-to-end on the new AppImage — drive detected (`/dev/cdrom`), offset auto-applied +667, network-heal → `--unknown` rip, **tracks 1-2 verified with matching Test/Copy CRCs**, files written to `~/Music/rips/The Police/…`. Only track 3 fails (the >587 bug / possibly disc damage). Container network was transient (resolved + HTTP 200 on retest; note it resolved to an *IPv6* addr — partial IPv6 is a likely cause of intermittent whipper NetworkErrors). **Still queued:** in-app Uninstaller + app/uninstaller Desktop icons + Multimedia-category verification (user-requested; user wants uninstaller to remove all our stuff but keep distrobox/podman + music). **Final-proof gate stands:** a clean verified full-disc rip on hardware (track 3) — likely needs a cleaned disc or the cyanrip backend.
- **Zero-CLI step 2: AppImage self-integration SHIPPED (2026-06-05, KDD-17 slice a).** `appimage_integration.py`: on first AppImage run (detected via `$APPIMAGE`), one-time/dismissible offer to add Whipper GUI to the apps menu — writes `~/.local/share/applications/whipper-gui.desktop` (Exec=the AppImage, quoted), copies the bundled icon from `$APPDIR`, sets the AppImage +x, refreshes menu caches (best-effort, injectable). `is_integrated()` matches on the Exec path so a moved/updated AppImage re-integrates. No-op on source/pipx (no `$APPIMAGE`). Wired in `_maybe_offer_first_run_setup` (before host/drive offers); `Config.appimage_integration_prompted`. Supersedes user-facing `install-appimage.sh`. Tests 581→591. **Remaining KDD-17: (b) self-update** (zsync update-information + `.sha256`). **The one Linux wart left:** the *very first* launch of a freshly-downloaded AppImage still needs the exec bit — the app can't set +x on itself before it runs. Real-end-user channel is the published Release (raw AppImage), NOT the CI `.zip` artifact. NOTE: integration logic unit-tested with injected tmp dirs; a real AppImage first-run (icon extraction from `$APPDIR`, menu refresh) is the final proof.
- **Zero-CLI step 1: GUI host-setup wizard SHIPPED (2026-06-05, KDD-17 slice c).** User reiterated the core requirement: download one file, double-click, it installs + asks questions — no terminal. Built the no-terminal replacement for `setup-host.sh`: `deps/host_setup.py` is the **bootstrap arm of the dependency subsystem** (Critical Rule #6) — an idempotent 5-step engine (distrobox → backend → container → whipper-in-container → export) behind an **injectable `CommandRunner`** (fully unit-tested + dry-run; the real command execution is the only hardware-gated part). `ui/host_setup_dialog.py` + `workers/host_setup_worker.py` drive it off-thread with live per-step progress; offered automatically on first launch when whipper is absent (`_maybe_offer_first_run_setup` → host offer before drive offer) and on **Tools → Set up Whipper GUI…**. **Key correctness call:** host-root installs use **`pkexec`** (graphical polkit), NOT `sudo` — a GUI subprocess has no TTY for sudo to prompt on; in-container installs stay `sudo` (distrobox grants passwordless sudo); on Bazzite/Silverblue the runtime is preinstalled so those steps are skipped (zero prompts). Config flag `host_setup_prompted`. Tests 572→581. **Remaining KDD-17:** (a) AppImage self-integration on first run + (b) self-update — NOT built (user chose "both, host wizard first"; self-integration is next). **Reframing the user needed:** the `.zip`/`chmod`/`setup-host.sh` instructions I'd been giving were the *CI-artifact/dev* path; the real end-user channel is the published Release (raw double-clickable AppImage). The unavoidable first-run exec-bit on a freshly-downloaded AppImage is a Linux reality to address in slice (a). NOTE: user away from hardware — wizard orchestration is unit-tested + dry-run-validated, but a real run (pkexec prompt, distrobox/podman) is the final proof.
- **Read-offset lookup by drive model — auto-detect refactor + FULL list bundled (2026-06-05):** Real-hardware test: whipper's `offset find` failed even with a *recognizable* CD (it's "primitive" + depends on in-container AccurateRip working). Root cause = the architecture leaned entirely on that probe and ignored the drive vendor/model we already parse. Fix ([docs/archive/offset-investigation-2026-06.md](archive/offset-investigation-2026-06.md)): new `adapters/accuraterip_offsets.py` (`OffsetDatabase`) resolves the offset by drive model the way EAC/dBpoweramp do — no disc. `DrivePicker.current_drive()` exposes the descriptor; `DriveSetupDialog` pre-fills the known offset (`known_offset`/`drive_label`) for one-click Save; `MainWindow._on_drive_setup` does the lookup; whipper `offset find` demoted to optional verify. **The ENTIRE AccurateRip `DriveOffsets.bin` list (~4,800 drives) is imported + bundled** in-code as a gzip+base64 blob (`accuraterip_offsets_data.py`) — works offline for any drive, no package-data risk (it's a .py module, not data). I reverse-engineered the binary format (69-byte records: `<int16 LE offset><67B null-padded name>`) and **validated it against the known BDR-209D=+667**; `scripts/update_drive_offsets.py` regenerates the blob and *refuses to write unless that sentinel passes*. `normalize_drive_name` collapses AccurateRip's `" - "` vendor/model separator so its names match whipper's split fields. Layered: user CSV > curated overrides > bundled. **Safety:** only ever *suggests* (wrong offset corrupts rips → human confirms). Tests 534→561 (TASKS #8 now `[x]`). NOTE: user is away — lookup/parse are unit-tested + the +667 sentinel validated, but a real from-scratch wizard run on hardware is still the final proof.
- **Testing institutionalized (2026-06-05):** Wrote [docs/testing.md](testing.md) as the authoritative testing strategy/standards (testing trophy + explicit real-hardware gate; five-tier case taxonomy easy/medium/hard/edge/unexpected; property/golden/fault-injection/mutation guidance; Definition of Done; portable to sibling projects e.g. `scheduling-engineV2`). Concrete infra added: **property-based tests** via `hypothesis` (`tests/test_parsers_property.py`) lock in "parsers never raise on arbitrary input" (300 examples each — all passed, confirming the silent-startup-crash wasn't a parser); CI now runs **branch coverage + `--cov-fail-under=88`** (baseline ~91%, ratchets up) on a **Python 3.11–3.13 matrix**; `pytest-cov`+`hypothesis` in the `dev` extra, `mutmut` documented as a periodic (non-CI) audit. Suite 529→534. Institutional rules now in CLAUDE/testing.md: **every shipped bug gets a regression test in the same PR; every new external-output parser gets a never-raises property test.** NOTE: `scheduling-engineV2` is a *separate repo* this session's GitHub tools can't reach (scope-locked to whipper-gui) — the testing doc is written to be carried there by a session scoped to that repo.
- **Ecosystem audit + backend-successor decision — KDD-18 (2026-06-04):** Real-hardware testing (Pioneer BDR-209D) hit whipper's `offset find` failing → prompted a researched audit ([docs/archive/ecosystem-audit-2026-06.md](archive/ecosystem-audit-2026-06.md)). Findings: **whipper is stalled — last release v0.10.0, 2021-05-17 (~5 yr)** — and imports `pkg_resources` (gone in setuptools ≥81 / Python 3.14; we paper over it with `python3-setuptools` in the container). **cyanrip** is the active successor (v0.9.3.1 2024-06-05, C/FFmpeg, LGPL-2.1, AccurateRip v1/v2 + EAC CRC). **Decision:** keep whipper now; build `CyanripImpl` behind the existing `WhipperBackend` ABC as a config-selectable backend (TASKS ranked item 9); **never fork whipper** (contribute to cyanrip if needed). CTDB is backend-independent (rides above either). Near-term backend-independent win = **auto drive-offset lookup from the AccurateRip list by drive model** (TASKS item 8) — what would've prevented the BDR-209D friction. Open unknown: cyanrip Fedora packaging (AUR yes; Fedora TBD). Also fixed live UX bug: the drive-setup wizard now locks the manual offset spinbox/Save button during detection (PR #23).
- **Zero-CLI distribution decided — KDD-17 (2026-06-04, user-approved HIGH priority):** Goal is download-one-file/double-click/no-terminal for non-technical users. Sanctioned evolution of the distribution model (Critical Rule #2 / deviation policy). Three independently-shippable slices, ranked-order **item 7** + detailed under **P1.1**: (a) AppImage **self-integrates** on first run (installs its own `.desktop`+icon; supersedes user-facing `install-appimage.sh`); (b) AppImage **self-updates** via standard **update-information (zsync)** + `.sha256` verify (do NOT hand-roll a download stub — that's what the user proposed, but AppImageUpdate already does skip-if-current+delta); (c) **GUI first-run host wizard** moves `setup-host.sh`'s work into the dependency self-management subsystem (Critical Rule #6), driving rootless podman/distrobox — one polkit prompt only where the runtime is missing (rootless/no-prompt on Bazzite). **Rejected:** Flatpak (sandbox vs host whipper — Critical Rule #3); bespoke remote-config download stub; downloaded `.desktop`/script (desktop trust model). Nothing built yet — design only. Also added a "recognized-CD walkthrough" to `docs/test-plan.md`.
- **Ruff + coverage + doc audit (2026-06-04):** Adopted **ruff** as the linter+formatter — config in `pyproject.toml` (`[tool.ruff]`, rules `E,F,W,I,B,UP`, `E501` off; `ruff>=0.15` in the `dev` extra) and a parallel **`lint` job** in `ci.yml` running `ruff check` + `ruff format --check`. Fixed all findings + raised coverage; the suite is now **525 tests** (was 454) and **35 CTDB tests** (was 30). Then did a **doc consistency pass** (unified `[ ]`/`[~]`/`[x]`/`[?]` markers across TASKS + test-plan, de-duplicated CTDB verify/repair to one authoritative spot, cross-linked docs) **and a doc accuracy audit** against the code: refreshed PLANNING.md's directory tree + per-module list (they predated the `ctdb/` package, `adapters/ctdb_client.py`, `drive_access`/`drive_control`/`offset_config`/`help_content`, the drive-setup/help dialogs+worker, `scripts/`, the new workflows, and `entrypoint.sh`), added `ruff` to DEPENDENCIES/PLANNING dep tables, and corrected stale counts + the README "Coming in P1" list (those EAC toggles already shipped). `docs/test-plan.md` now uses the repo's checkbox convention.
- **CTDB verify Phase-1 library landed (2026-06-03, KDD-16):** Clean-room lookup client + verify logic shipped — `adapters/ctdb_client.py` (GET `db.cuetools.net/lookup2.php`, XML `entry` parse, stdlib only) and `src/whipper_gui/ctdb/` (`toc.py` disc-TOC math + `toc=` builder; `decode.py` host-`flac`/`metaflac` wrappers; `crc.py` the audio CRC; `verify.py` orchestration + `Verdict` enum). Standalone `scripts/ctdb_verify.py` is the hardware test vehicle. **35 unit tests** cover the deterministic parts. **Deliberately NOT wired into the GUI** (user decision 2026-06-03: library+script+test-plan first) because **two pieces are hardware-validation-gated**: (1) the `toc=` wire format and (2) the bit-exact CRC (`crc.ctdb_crc_offset0` is a placeholder zlib CRC-32 with `CRC_VALIDATED=False`). Both are isolated behind a single seam and **fail safe** — a wrong CRC yields NO_MATCH, never a false "verified". PCM decode uses host `flac` if present (optional dep). Validation steps: `docs/test-plan.md` Test 1 (fix `ctdb/toc.py` if `not_in_db`; fix `ctdb/crc.py` from the **LGPL** `CUETools.AccurateRip` if `no_match` — never read `python-cuetoolsdb`). New **`docs/test-plan.md`** is the running manual/hardware checklist for all remaining gated work.
- **CTDB verify license gate RESOLVED — clean-room (2026-06-02, KDD-16):** Verified the licenses rather than assuming: we're GPL-3.0-only; `python-cuetoolsdb` is bare GPLv2 with *no* author version-election (no `license=`, no classifiers, no SPDX headers) → treat as GPL-2.0-**only** → one-way-incompatible with GPLv3, so we **cannot port it**. Decision: build CTDB verify **clean-room** from the **LGPL** `gchudov/cuetools.net` source (LGPL is GPLv3-compatible); protocol/CRC are facts, not copyrightable expression, so independent reimplementation is fine. **Never read/paraphrase `python-cuetoolsdb`.** Wrote an LGPL-grounded protocol+CRC spec in `docs/upstream-modification-investigation.md` (confirmed from `CUETools.CTDB/CUEToolsDB.cs`: host `db.cuetools.net`, `GET /lookup2.php?version=3&ctdb=1&fuzzy=&metadata=&toc=`, response `entry` w/ `crc`/`confidence`/`npar`/`id`/`hasParity`/`trackcrcs`/`syndrome`; audio CRC = `AccurateRipVerify.CTDBCRC(offset)` over ±`(5*588-1)`=±2939 samples — read `CUETools.AccurateRip`/`CUETools.Parity` for the bit-exact polynomial at impl time). **Only remaining blocker is hardware validation** (a real CD that's in CTDB); no code written yet by design.
- **openSUSE installer support + CTDB groundwork (2026-06-02):** `setup-host.sh` now auto-handles openSUSE — added `*suse*) zypper --non-interactive install …` to `ensure_distrobox` + `ensure_container_backend` (README distro table: openSUSE ⚠️→✅). Made distro detection testable via an `OS_RELEASE_FILE` env override (tests point it at a fixture). The **declined-dependency-cascade** backlog item turned out to be already done+tested (manager.py skips cascade on `user_declined`) — marked complete in TASKS. **CTDB verify is the ranked-next feature but was deliberately NOT coded:** it needs a real CD that's in CTDB to validate the audio CRC (a T32-style hardware test the cloud env can't do), and there's a **GPL-2.0 (python-cuetoolsdb) vs GPL-3.0-only (us)** license gate to resolve first. Instead, corrected a material error — **`ctdb-cli` is C#/.NET 10, NOT a C tool** (KDD-14 had it backwards), so Phase-2 AppImage bundling is heavy (bundle-vs-optional-install now open) — and wrote a concrete Phase-1 spec in `docs/upstream-modification-investigation.md`. Verify is pure-Python (not a `ctdb-cli` wrap); repair wraps `ctdb-cli`.
- **v0.1.0 shipped + EAC-parity upstream investigation (2026-06-02):** First public release [v0.1.0](https://github.com/rmccann-hub/Whipper-GUI-Frontend---CD-Rip/releases/tag/v0.1.0) is live (AppImage + `.sha256` + `install.sh` + `install-appimage.sh`), all three workflows green on real runs. *(Gotcha: it's marked a full release, not pre-release — created via the UI, and `release.yml` only sets `--prerelease` on the create path, not the asset-upload path. Future `git tag`-driven `v0.*` releases auto-mark correctly.)* Did a full test/doc audit: 454 tests pass; README now has an all-distros install table (Fedora/Bazzite, Ubuntu/Debian, Mint/Pop!_OS, Arch, openSUSE — note `setup-host.sh` doesn't yet auto-handle `zypper`, so openSUSE users `zypper install podman` first; tasked as a follow-up). New **investigation** doc `docs/upstream-modification-investigation.md` + TASKS.md section on modifying upstream open source for EAC parity: headline — most EAC *correctness* is already in whipper; the real win is **CTDB verify+repair** (wrap LGPL `ctdb-cli`, confirmed feasible — not a whipper change). **Guardrail: never fork whipper** (unmaintained; migrate the adapter to `cyanrip` if forced). Permanently non-feasible (don't revisit): AccurateRip/CTDB *submission* (policy/trust-gated), C2 error-pointer reading (C-level rewrite, marginal), byte-for-byte EAC log parity, separate offset DB.
- **One-file installer + AppImage CI (2026-06-01):** `install.sh` is the headline end-user installer — host stack (`setup-host.sh --no-gui`) + download the released AppImage + `install-appimage.sh` (desktop integration). It's curl-pipe-able and reuses the sibling scripts (downloads them if not run from a checkout). `install-appimage.sh` now also installs an **"Uninstall Whipper GUI"** launcher that runs a staged copy of `uninstall.sh` (placed at `~/Applications/whipper-gui-uninstall.sh`); `uninstall.sh` gained AppImage/icon/shortcut removal so it's the single comprehensive uninstaller for both install paths. AppImage download uses the **releases API** (not `/releases/latest/`) because `v0.*` are pre-releases. New `.github/workflows/appimage.yml` builds + smoke-tests the AppImage on every push to `main` and (via `workflow_dispatch`) uploads a downloadable artifact for any branch — see `docs/appimage-testing.md`. Gotcha fixed: an EXIT-trap cleanup must `return 0` or its non-zero status becomes the script's exit code.
- **Help menu + version 0.1.0 (2026-06-01):** `Help → About` (`ui/help_dialogs.AboutDialog`) shows the version (`__version__` in `src/whipper_gui/__init__.py` — the single source; `pyproject.toml` reads it dynamically, so there's nothing to keep in lockstep) plus Python/Qt/PySide6 versions and the config/log/whipper paths; `Help → User Guide` (`HelpDialog`) renders `help_content.USER_GUIDE` (a Markdown string kept *in code*, not as packaged data, to dodge AppImage package-data pitfalls — edit the guide there). About deliberately does NOT shell out to whipper (would enter the container/could stall). Also fixed a long-standing red `main` CI: the whipper-backend argv tests created `/music` (fails non-root on CI); they now no-op `Path.mkdir`.
- **Critical Rule #3 — one approved exception (2026-05-31): force-stopping a cancelled rip.** A rip runs whipper-in-`ripping`-container, which spawns `cdrdao` (TOC/"Reading table") then `cd-paranoia` (track rip); podman doesn't forward the host SIGKILL, so the drive can spin for minutes after Cancel (the physical eject button is inhibited while a read holds the device). `drive_control.force_stop_drive()` (host-first, all best-effort): **(1) `pkill -KILL -f 'whipper (cd|drive|offset|…)'`** — whipper is the *orchestrator* and respawns the reader if you kill only the reader, so you MUST kill whipper; the pattern is anchored to `whipper <subcommand>` so it can never match the GUI's "whipper-gui" cmdline or self-match; **(2) `pkill -KILL 'cdparanoia|cd-paranoia|cdrdao'`** by process *name* (no `-f`); **(3) `fuser -k <device>`** — name-independent kill of whatever still holds the device (GUI never opens it); **(4) `distrobox enter ripping -- pkill …`** only if the host pkill matched nothing (the user-approved into-the-container call); **(5) then `eject`**. Hard-won real-user lessons (2026-06-01): on rootless podman the in-container procs are host-visible so host `pkill`/`fuser` reach them; `pkill cdrdao` alone does NOTHING (whipper respawns it) — kill whipper; never `pkill -f whipper` (matches "whipper-gui" → kills the app) nor `-f` with reader names (self-matches the wrapper). `pkill`/`fuser`/`distrobox`/`eject` resolved to absolute paths (desktop-launched GUI has minimal PATH). **Scope is strictly force-stop on cancel** — ripping still goes through `~/.local/bin/whipper`. MainWindow auto-escalates `_FORCE_STOP_COUNTDOWN_MS` (5s) after Cancel, plus a manual "Force stop" button. Do NOT widen this exception without asking.
- **P0 status: COMPLETE — all 32 tasks done.** T32 closed 2026-05-30: a full 16-track rip ran **through the AppImage** on Bazzite, `success=True`, every Test CRC == Copy CRC, "No errors occurred". Next up is the P1 release milestones (merge to `main`, tag `v0.0.1`, publish the AppImage).
- **AppImage CA-cert bug (fixed 2026-05-30):** the bundled manylinux CPython has no CA certificates, so MusicBrainz HTTPS lookups failed with `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` — disc identification was silently broken in the distributed build (the editable install worked because it uses system Python). `entrypoint.sh` now points `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`SSL_CERT_DIR` at the host CA bundle (Fedora/Bazzite, Debian/Ubuntu, Arch/openSUSE, Alpine paths). Verified: the bundled interpreter does an HTTPS GET to musicbrainz.org → 200 once the env var is set. Guarded by `tests/test_build_harness.py`.
- **Progress + fidelity UX (2026-05-30, real-use feedback):** `RipWorker.progress` now emits `(overall, task)` — an overall bar (monotonic, whole-rip, 0-5% disc scan / 5-95% tracks / 95-100% finalize) plus a current-task bar; the pre-track disc scan animates instead of sitting at 0%. On finish the status line shows a fidelity verdict ("Done — all N tracks verified, Test/Copy CRCs match") via `_fidelity_summary`. A MusicBrainz lookup *error* now also falls back to placeholder track rows (previously only a clean no-match did), so the table is never empty.
- **EAC parity-gap Settings shipped (2026-05-30, roadmap #4):** cover art (`-C/--cover-art`, choices `file|embed|complete` — NOT the `none/embedded/file` the backlog guessed; verify flags against `whipper/command/cd.py`), force-overread (`-x`), max-retries (`-r`, always passed, default 5), keep-going (`-k`). Config fields + Settings widgets + `RipParameters` + argv passthrough. **Behaviour change:** `cover_art` defaults to `embed`, so rips now fetch art over the network by default.
- **Drive-access diagnostics shipped (2026-05-30):** `drive_access.diagnose_drive_access()` (pure stdlib, injectable probes) classifies the no-drive case as `no_device` / `permission` (gives `sudo usermod -aG <group> $USER`) / `ok`. `DrivePicker.drives_unavailable` → MainWindow auto-nudges once per session only when actionable; always available via Tools → Diagnose drive access…. Roadmap item #3 done.
- **Real-user fixes (2026-05-31, third from-scratch test):**
  - **Drive kept spinning after Cancel (rip + wizard):** killing only the whipper parent orphaned `cdparanoia` (the actual reader). Now launch the rip + setup subprocesses with `start_new_session=True` and cancel via `os.killpg` on the whole group (`_kill_group`). Caveat: the *in-container* (podman) ripper is a separate tree podman doesn't instantly signal, so the drive can still take a moment to spin down — documented, not fully solvable from our side.
  - **Cancel message:** distinguishes user cancellation ("Rip cancelled by user. Partial files may remain.") from a real failure; cancel status warns the drive may take a moment.
  - **Drive setup dialog** was tiny/clipped → `resize(560,420)` + min size + results in a scrollable read-only `QPlainTextEdit`.
  - **Picard "1 missing" nag:** added `DependencySpec.optional`; Picard is `optional=True`. The launch check no longer nags/counts optional deps; the summary lists them as "Optional (not installed): …".
  - **Album fields now drive the rip:** album-artist edit propagates to every track's Artist column (`TrackTableModel.set_all_artists`, fired on `editingFinished`; per-track edits still hold). And an unknown-disc rip names the folder from the (sanitized) album artist/title (`_safe_path_segment`) instead of the literal "Unknown Artist/Unknown Album".
  - **Desktop icon:** `dev-setup.sh` also drops a launcher on `~/Desktop` (chmod +x, GNOME-trusted); `uninstall.sh` removes it. Both refresh KDE's `kbuildsycoca` so the menu entry appears/disappears without a re-login.
- **`setup-host.sh` shipped (2026-05-30):** one-command bootstrap of README Steps 1-4 (Distrobox → `ripping` container → `dnf install whipper flac python3-setuptools` → `distrobox-export`) + clone + `dev-setup.sh`. Idempotent; `--dry-run`/`--yes`/`--no-gui`/`--container`/`--image`; runs in-place if inside a checkout, else clones; `curl … | bash`-able. **Only `--dry-run`-tested (no Distrobox in CI) — needs a real-hardware confirm.** Excludes drive calibration (GUI wizard) and Picard (GUI dep manager). Smoke tests in `tests/test_setup_host_script.py`.
- **Real-user fixes (2026-05-30, second from-scratch test):**
  - **Wizard crash fixed (critical):** closing the drive-setup dialog mid-detection destroyed a still-running `QThread` (Qt aborted the whole app) and orphaned the `whipper offset find` process (drive kept spinning). Fix: setup commands now run via a cancellable `Popen` (`_run_setup_capture` + `cancel_setup()` which SIGKILLs — the worker's `communicate()` is the sole reaper, so no waitpid race); `DriveSetupWorker.cancel()`; the dialog cancels + joins the thread on `reject()`/`closeEvent` and guards `_on_finished` with a `_closing` flag.
  - **Dependency check de-duplicated:** removed the Tools → "Check dependencies…" menu item; it lives only on the Settings button now (+ the launch-time auto-check).
  - **Manual offset in Settings:** the read-offset spinbox is editable again, with an "Override whipper.conf" checkbox → passes `whipper --offset N` (`Config.override_read_offset`, `RipParameters.read_offset_override`). Lets the user set the offset without editing whipper.conf; the wizard remains the primary path.
  - **Desktop entry:** `dev-setup.sh` installs `~/.local/share/applications/whipper-gui.desktop` (Exec = venv launcher, Icon = stock `media-optical`); `uninstall.sh` removes it.
- **Drive setup wizard shipped (2026-05-30, KDD-15):** Tools → "Set up drive…" (+ Settings "Re-detect…") opens `DriveSetupDialog`, which runs whipper's own `drive analyze` + `offset find` via `DriveSetupWorker` (off-thread) — they persist to `whipper.conf` themselves; `back_up_whipper_config()` makes a `.bak` first. New adapter methods `analyze_drive()`/`find_offset()` are an *optional* ABC capability (NotImplementedError default, so fakes/other backends still construct). Read-offset Settings field is now read-only + the wizard button (fixes the "misleading field" gap). Deferred: manual-offset write fallback, first-run auto-offer, live streaming output. roadmap item #2 done.
- **Post-v1 roadmap set (2026-05-30, after an "EAC successor" research review):** ranked in TASKS.md P1 — (1) release milestones, (2) **drive setup wizard** that writes `whipper.conf` via whipper's own `drive analyze`/`offset find` (KDD-15), (3) **drive-access permission diagnostics** (cdrom-group / `/dev/sr0`), (4) the four EAC parity-gap Settings widgets, (5) **CTDB verify** (pure-Python, Phase 1), (6) **CTDB repair** (Phase 2: wrap the `ctdb-cli` C tool, bundled in the AppImage, explicit trigger, submission shelved — KDD-14). Most of the doc's forensic content is already handled by whipper; its Flatpak/Snap sandboxing section is N/A (we ship AppImage+pipx to reach host whipper). Test&Copy dual-pass downgraded — whipper already gives per-track Test/Copy CRC.
- **Edited tags feed the unknown rip (2026-05-30):** after a successful unknown-mode rip, `_on_rip_finished` → `run_unknown_post_processing` writes the track table's (edited) album/track fields to the FLACs via `apply_track_tags()` (blanks fall back to placeholders; a typed year → `DATE`). Scoped to the just-ripped album folder (the `.log`'s parent), NOT the output root — globbing the root would re-tag the whole library. Edits flow to tags only, not filenames.
- **T32 AppImage build (2026-05-29):** `bash build/build_appimage.sh` produces `whipper-gui-x86_64.AppImage`; `--version` prints `whipper-gui 0.0.1`; a headless `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` launch reaches the Qt event loop (config created, MusicBrainz adapter up, dependency manager probes all deps). Building it surfaced **five recipe bugs the unit tests missed** — all fixed + regression-guarded in `tests/test_build_harness.py`:
  - python-appimage installs requirements.txt **one line at a time from a temp dir**, so a standalone `--find-links .` line fails → use `PIP_FIND_LINKS` (exported by the build script) instead.
  - `system()` runs each `pip install` through a shell, so `<`/`>` in pins are read as redirections → pins use `~=` (`PySide6~=6.7`, `tomli-w~=1.0`).
  - the entrypoint is globbed as `entrypoint.*`, so it must have an extension (`entrypoint.sh`) or the bare interpreter runs.
  - a space in the `.desktop` `Name=` breaks the unquoted appimagetool command → `Name=Whipper-GUI`; the script normalises the output to `whipper-gui-x86_64.AppImage`.
  - python-appimage fetches the CPython base image via the GitHub **API** (403s when rate-limited) → optional `WHIPPER_GUI_BASE_IMAGE` env var feeds a pre-downloaded base image; FUSE-less hosts also need `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN=1`.
- **T32 real-hardware rip (2026-05-29):** a 16-track CD-R ripped end-to-end on Bazzite + Distrobox + Pioneer BDR-209D. All Test CRCs == Copy CRCs, FLACs play, `.log`/`.cue`/`.m3u`/`.toc` written, AccurateRip correctly "not in DB". Findings + fixes (each has a test):
  - whipper refuses CD-Rs without `--cdr` → added `Config.continue_on_cdr` + Settings toggle + `RipParameters.cdr` + flag passthrough.
  - whipper `os.chdir()`s into `--working-directory` without creating it → adapter now `mkdir -p`s the working + output dirs before the rip.
  - **KDD-06 RESOLVED: libdiscid is NOT needed on the host** (whipper-in-container computes the disc ID; the GUI never calls libdiscid). The `libdiscid` registry entry stays unneeded.
  - **KDD-13 answered:** whipper writes `.cue` + `.m3u` + `.toc` next to the FLACs and fills ISRC/UPC slots (all-zero on a CD-R).
  - Unknown-disc UX: track table now shows `Track 01…NN` placeholder rows (`DiscInfo.num_tracks` salvaged from `cd info`'s partial output); status label stays live through the pre-track disc scan (`RipWorker.status` signal).
  - Path templates: two pairs picked per rip (`ui/rip_controls`). Known disc → `%A/%d/%t - %n - %d - %A - %y` (Artist/Album/`## - Title - Album - Artist - Year`); unknown disc → literal `Unknown Artist/Unknown Album/%t - Track %t` (avoids the disc-ID hash whipper puts in `%d`, so no post-rip renaming needed). All four editable in Settings; config schema v1→v2 migration upgrades untouched known templates. whipper templates are flat (no conditionals): year-less known discs get a trailing " - "; `%N` disc-number omitted from the default (add `/%N` for multi-disc).
- **Real-user testing on Bazzite (2026-05-28/29)** surfaced and fixed a series of upstream/environment issues; each has a code comment with the diagnosis:
  - `whipper` has no `-d`/`--device` flag (assumed wrong in early adapter) — `adapters/whipper_backend.py`
  - `whipper cd info` exits -1 with "unable to retrieve disc metadata" on discs not in MB/FreeDB; `--unknown` isn't accepted by `Info` even though `_CD.do()` requires it — adapter catches and returns empty DiscInfo
  - Whipper 0.10.0 imports `pkg_resources` which Python 3.14 dropped — `python3-setuptools` must be installed alongside whipper in the Distrobox container
  - On Bazzite (Atomic), Flathub is a system-level remote by default; `flatpak install --user flathub <ref>` fails with "No remote refs found for 'flathub'" — registry uses the `.flatpakref` URL instead, which auto-configures the user remote
  - `gh` isn't preinstalled on Bazzite and `sudo dnf install gh` doesn't work on immutable hosts — README recommends SSH key auth as the primary path for Bazzite/Silverblue
  - Fresh venvs ship outdated `pip` on most distros — `dev-setup.sh` runs `pip install --upgrade pip` before installing the package
- **Branch state (2026-05-30):** merged to **`main`** — `main` now carries the full project (default branch for fresh clones). Repo is public; license GPL-3.0-only. Ongoing work continued on `claude/lucid-babbage-JYI8c` and is merged forward to `main`. The `dev-setup.sh` branch-guard and the README dev-branch/auth steps were removed as part of the merge.
